Chapter 601: Scaring Oneself

Above the gray fog, Klein leaned back into his high-back chair and held Creeping Hunger. He remained silent for several seconds.

Finally, he followed the prescribed steps and released the Psychiatrist's soul.

A tall figure swiftly appeared beside the bronze table. It was a woman with indistinct features, her pain and anguish plain to see.

Klein looked at her and asked in a conversational tone, "Do you still remember who you are?"

In the mysterious space, he could directly channel her spirit.

The Psychiatrist's hostility subsided considerably, and she smiled bitterly.

"Of course I remember.

"I was a member of a secret organization and was planning to meet a friend in Toscarter Island, but I ended up encountering pirates on the way.

"Although I used my powers to resolve the crisis, the pirates who escaped told Qilangos what had happened. To obtain my powers, he deliberately changed course and intercepted our ship. You can probably imagine what happened after that.

"Thanks to his meticulousness, I was killed instantly, unlike the other female passengers who encountered things that were worse than death."

Klein fell silent for a few seconds before nodding slightly.

"Do you know of the ancient book known as Groselle's Travels?"

Since Groselle's Travels came from the dragons, he believed the Psychiatrist might have heard its name or learned something about it.

The female Psychiatrist thought carefully before shaking her head.

"I'm sorry. I've never heard that name before."

Klein let the subject drop and asked, "Since you're about to dissipate and find eternal peace, do you have any last wishes?"

The Psychiatrist lowered her head and chuckled.

"I wish to be revived.

"Alright. I know that this wish cannot be satisfied. Everything else isn't necessary. I've been dead for years. My family and friends have probably already received news of my death. Informing them again of my death will only dig up the pain of those memories.

"Let's leave it at that. Thank you. That should be it..."

Her figure rapidly dissipated and vanished, leaving behind a huge golden iris capable of laying bare the thoughts in anyone's heart.

This was the Beyonder characteristic of a Psychiatrist.

Klein sighed and began pondering over the details of what the Psychiatrist had said.

She was visiting a friend at Toscarter Island alone without any family...

Toscarter Island is located at the easternmost edge of the Sonia Sea, south of the Gargas Archipelago.

That's the easternmost colony of the Loen Kingdom, while the Gargas Archipelago belongs to the Feysac Empire... What kind of friend would be there? What kind of friend is worth visiting after taking such a long trip?

Although she only said that she was from a secret organization, chances are it was the Psychology Alchemists. Was she on a mission?

Klein respected the deceased and didn't wish to dig out her secrets, so he didn't probe deeper. Without any clues, he quickly stopped thinking about such matters and considered something else.

After hunting down Wormtongue Mithor King, regardless of the reason, I have to leave Bayam for the time being... I've really done quite a bit in the City of Generosity during this time... Yes, I'll attend about three Beyonder gatherings and confirm whether the Marionettist potion's main ingredients are available before leaving... Klein quickly made a decision and conjured a scene of The World praying. After shrouding it in gray fog, he cast the scene toward the crimson star representing Miss Justice.

Audrey stood on the third-floor balcony, looking toward the nearby village. Most of the rooftops were brick red and bore abstract or lifelike images of dragons.

Suddenly, the familiar endless void surfaced in front of her eyes.

Amidst the gray fog, a blurry figure was standing high above, praying to the god above.

"Honorable Mr. Fool, please inform Miss Justice that she can make preparations for the transaction."

I can make preparations for the transaction? He has obtained the Beyonder characteristic of a Psychiatrist? But he still didn't have one yesterday... The World's efficiency shocked Audrey as she nearly forgot to thank Mr. Fool.

Thankfully, she was no longer the inexperienced, naive young lady she had once been. Having weathered several major incidents, she quickly composed herself and sincerely thanked him. She asked Mr. World to wait another two or three days while she prepared to repay the money she owed Mr. Fool's Blessed. Although she had enough, she needed to maintain a minimum balance or others might notice something amiss.

After the illusory gray fog dissipated, Audrey silently looked down at Susie, who was admiring the garden on the first floor, before pacing around and secretly clicking her tongue.

Mr. World is really terrifying...

Evening, in the Seaweed Bar.

Having just squandered all his money at the casino, Blue Eyes Meath sat at the bar and ordered a glass of Lanti Proof.

He was just about to raise the glass when he suddenly heard the bartender whisper to him in a subdued voice, "Wormtongue Mithor is dead."

"What... Who did it?" After his initial alarm, Blue Eyes Meath asked excitedly, "Is someone challenging Vice Admiral Ailment?"

"An adventurer named Gehrman Sparrow. He wasn't known at all before. Who knew that he finished off Mithor!" The bartender didn't hide his shock and alarm. "He also killed Ozil, the real boss of Amyris Leaf Bar, Strongman Ozil!"

Blue Eyes Meath was just about to sigh when he frowned suddenly.

Back when Ozil's men came to ask him about Blazing Danitz, he had mentioned seeing the boatswain of the Golden Dream in the company of an unknown adventurer. He had used a ritual to provide a corresponding portrait.

Ozil is dead. Mithor King is dead. They were killed by an unknown adventurer named Gehrman Sparrow... Blue Eyes Meath suddenly trembled as a scene that had left a deep impression on him surfaced in his mind.

The gentlemanly young man stood at the bar's entrance. He had black hair, brown eyes, and a thin, sharply chiseled face. His cold gaze swept over the customers as though searching for prey.

Blue Eyes Meath shuddered, abandoned his drink, and strode out of the bar.

Terrifying! That man is too terrifying! Even Wormtongue died at his hands. He's definitely a powerhouse on the level of an admiral! He'll come for me and finish me off. No, I can't stay in Bayam any longer. I have to return to the ship and get far away from here! Blue Eyes Meath ran to the Red Theater, dragged his companions away, and led them through the woods by a roundabout route to the Resistance's private harbor.

Across from the Red Theater, the herb shop in the alley remained open. The chubby Apothecary Darkwill sat behind the counter, outwardly calm but deeply anxious within.

He had already contacted the Life School of Thought members he knew via various methods and sought their help. However, he had no idea who they were or when they would come. He could only bear with his fear and anxiety as he continued running the store, pretending as though nothing had happened.

"Darkwill, you're extremely uneasy." The chubby owl flew over from an unknown location and landed on the counter.

"There's no need for you to remind me. I know my state of mind very well." Darkwill waved his hands impatiently.

He still remembered his days under his teacher's tutelage. Roy King had often warned him to beware of official Beyonder organizations and true Sanguine, citing examples such as eternal imprisonment underground without sunlight or women; being used as a test subject to assess the dangers of particular Sealed Artifacts; enduring experiment after experiment until he mutated into a monster ruled by instinct; or being drained and devoured by the Sanguine, then made into a puppet.

Those examples had etched themselves deeply into his mind, wiping out what little courage he possessed. After leaving his teacher, he never stayed long in any city and departed at the first risk of exposure.

Darkwill tried hard to keep his horror under control, placing his attention on the problem of rescuing his teacher.

...Old Man has been captured for some time. Why is he still imprisoned in the governor-general's office? With the military's abilities, they should be able to gather any information they wish to know. Be it an outright execution, the gathering of materials, or being used as an experimental subject, they would definitely move Old Man... Could it be that Old Man used some techniques to hide his secret, or they wish to make him into a spy? Sigh, just agree to it! Darkwill scratched his head as his thoughts wandered aimlessly.

Gradually, he recalled the last letter he received from his teacher, Roy King.

Old Man often hides hints in seemingly ordinary matters. Could that letter contain something similar? It didn't say much. It only told me to meet him near the Red Theater and boasted about his gambling skills. Ptooey! He clearly relies on luck. He even told me to go to Mabel's Sundry Store on Enmat Street and buy a die in preparation for a lesson... I thought I'd buy it after we met, so I haven't gone all this time. Could there be a secret hidden there? Like a drowning man clutching at a straw, Darkwill seized upon the possibility.

Many of Bayam's streets were named after Loen Kingdom cities.

Darkwill spent a few minutes mustering his courage, brought the owl with him, and closed the door to his shop before walking out of the alley.

While waiting for a rental carriage, he bought a copy of the News Report from a paperboy and flipped through the international and domestic news, as well as news about the archipelago.

Suddenly, he saw a familiar face--it was the adventurer who had informed him of Roy King's location.

This afternoon, Gehrman Sparrow killed Wormtongue Mithor King, who had a bounty of 5,400 pounds... I actually entrusted a mission to such a powerful adventurer! Darkwill clicked his tongue.

He soon put the matter out of his mind, boarded the carriage, and headed straight for Mabel's Sundry Store on Enmat Street.

The owner of the shop was a genial old lady who said after sizing him up, "The die you want costs 1 pound."

This is a robbery! Darkwill roared angrily in his head, but this only made him more certain that there might be clues hidden in the die.

After paying, he received a tiny ring box and opened the lid. He saw a milky-white six-sided die with four red dots facing up.

Although puzzled by the die's ordinary appearance and the way it fit so snugly inside the ring box that no space remained, Darkwill cautiously refrained from examining it on the spot. He slipped it into his pocket and crossed to the other side of the street.

When he came to a secluded spot, he couldn't help but give it a glance, unable to find any problems with it.

At that moment, a carriage sped past and startled him. His wrist trembled, and he dropped the die on the ground.

The die continued rolling and ultimately came to a stop, a single red dot pointing up.

Darkwill cursed the carriage driver and widened his stride to pick it up.

The instant he stepped forward, he stumbled and crashed heavily to the ground despite there being no obstacle in his path. Stars burst before his eyes.

His mind went blank for a moment as he lay there, the milky-white die right in front of his eyes.

Chapter 602: Becoming Famous

Darkwill sprawled on the ground, momentarily forgetting to stand up. His mind remained blank.

Since reaching adulthood, he had never tripped over his own feet. After consuming his potions, his body had been enhanced to some extent, making such a mishap even less likely. Yet now, for no apparent reason, he had inexplicably fallen.

Did I step on something? Darkwill abruptly came to his senses, slapped a palm against the ground, and rolled to his feet, pretending that he hadn't been the one to fall.

He looked left and right but found nothing unusual on the ground. Puzzled, he took a few painful steps forward to pick up the milky-white die.

At that moment, a patrolling constable seemed to notice the disturbance. He jogged over with his baton in one hand and the other resting on his revolver.

At the sight, Darkwill grew alarmed and suspected that he had fallen into a trap.

They caught Old Man but haven't come for me all this time. It's because they were secretly observing me and waiting for me to find some clues?

Now that I've gotten my hands on this strange die, they've started taking action?

The official Beyonders are here to catch me?

Darkwill instinctively turned around and ran, but he had suffered a rather heavy fall, causing him to feel pain in his knees. He was momentarily only able to walk.

As the constable approached and escape remained impossible, a vision of an underground prison surfaced in Darkwill's mind: a place lit only by eternally burning candles, where he would be locked away with Beyonders who panted like monsters...

"What happened?" Keeping his distance, the patrolling constable rested a hand on his revolver and asked cautiously.

Darkwill suddenly felt an irrepressible fear. As his wrist trembled, the milky-white die he had just picked up fell to the ground again as it rolled a few times.

This time, six red dots pointed up.

Under the constable's scrutiny, Darkwill replied in a trembling voice, "I stepped on a damned banana peel and fell."

The moment he said it, his heart skipped a beat. There wasn't a single banana peel on the ground.

Damn it. I was too nervous. I should've said that I tripped myself... Darkwill thought in frustration.

He decided to summon the owl perched on the roof opposite him, preparing to make a last stand.

The patrolling constable gave him a glance and chuckled.

"Watch where you put your feet when you walk. I thought you'd been robbed."

He released his palm on his revolver, raised his baton, and walked away.

Dazed, Darkwill watched the constable recede into the distance. He couldn't understand how the man had so readily believed such a blatantly flawed excuse.

He retracted his gaze and silently looked at the milky-white die sitting on the ground. He slowly frowned.

I'm not pure Loenese. The constables here don't need to suck up to me... Could this all be thanks to the die? Was it responsible for both my strange fall and the bizarre way I convinced the constable? Is this the important Sealed Artifact Teacher was safeguarding? Darkwill quickly made certain connections as he cautiously stepped forward. He picked up the die again and stuffed it into the tiny ring box, where it had no room to roll.

Signaling to his owl, Darkwill picked up his copy of the News Report, hailed a rental carriage, and hobbled aboard. His destination was the Red Theater.

The carriage smoothly proceeded forward. Because dusk was setting in, the street lamps which were relatively far away didn't illuminate the area much. He wasn't in a rush to study the ring box or the die inside. He patiently waited until he returned home.

After entering the herb store and heading up to the residential area on the second floor, he lit the wall lamp and chased the silly bird out of his room. He sat in front of his desk and repeatedly checked on the ring box and milky-white die.

Finally, he took out a folded piece of paper about the length of a finger segment from the bottom of the ring box.

Darkwill inhaled silently as he quickly spread open the piece of paper and discovered that there were three paragraphs written in ancient Feysac.

"If I fail to appear within three days of the appointed time, I must have been betrayed and arrested. Do not seek help from other members of the School of Thought, because I cannot determine who betrayed me. Doing so will place you in grave danger.

"There is only one thing you need to do. Take the die to Oravi Island and give it to Carnot, the bellman in the port city. My teacher, Ricciardo, is hiding there. He will handle everything that follows.

"Don't worry that I'll divulge this secret. Once I finish writing this letter, all related memories will disappear completely. Until I'm rescued, I won't even remember having an apprentice like you. Remember, do your utmost not to use the die. It possesses living characteristics. The more you use it, the more easily it will awaken. When you aren't watching, it will roll on its own even when there is no room to move. If it lands on 1, believe me when I say you will suffer a fate worse than death, because almost everything you attempt will end in failure--including your activities in bed."

Indeed, this die is very dangerous... Darkwill sighed unconsciously and immediately realized the foolish mistake his good intentions had led him to make.

To rescue his teacher, Roy King, he had overcome his cowardice, remained in Bayam, and sent a plea for help to members of the Life School of Thought.

And according to the information, this meant that he might very well have been targeted by the person who betrayed Roy King!

Why didn't you say so earlier! No, why didn't I head out to buy the die earlier!? Darkwill raised his hands and yanked at his hair.

He dared not stay any longer. He decided to leave his residence and buy a ticket from a scalper that night, then depart for Oravi Island early the next morning. The island lay along the route between the Rorsted Archipelago and Toscarter Island.

My charms, my revolver, my bullets... Darkwill quickly counted the items that could provide him with safety, feeling concern over his lack of combat strength.

He quickly thought of an idea as he paced around and mumbled, "I need to hire a bodyguard. A bodyguard..."

Who should I hire? How much money must I spend? Amid these thoughts, Darkwill's gaze swept across the copy of the News Report he had just brought back.

Suddenly, he thought of an excellent bodyguard--Gehrman Sparrow!

A powerful adventurer who could hunt a pirate worth 5,400 pounds!

If he's willing to accept the mission, I should be able to reach Oravi Island safely as long as the "councilor" doesn't take action... How can I find him? Right! I should post notices in the bars adventurers frequent! Darkwill nodded almost imperceptibly as he stuffed the items back into his packed suitcase. Taking the plump owl with him, he left the herb store once again, his heart aching.

Beyond the Rorsted Archipelago, the Black Death lay brazenly at anchor in a small Loen port, surrounded by three ships.

Fresh from a hot bath and wearing a loose man's shirt, Vice Admiral Ailment Tracy administered the final treatment to her nearly healed wound.

Scars meant nothing to a Demoness.

At this moment, the blonde female attendant knocked on the door and entered the captain's cabin after receiving permission.

When she saw Tracy, her cheeks flushed red as she moved her gaze away.

"Captain, a telegram was sent from Bayam to the port over here.

"Mithor, uh--the third mate has been killed."

Tracy stopped her actions as her expression sank. She asked hesitantly, "Do you know who did it?"

She had sent Wormtongue Mithor to Bayam to investigate the assassination attempt on her, first as punishment for his dereliction of duty, and second to spread the news that she was gravely injured so as to lure those who harbored malicious intent into attacking her. She never expected Mithor to make any breakthroughs or progress in such a short time.

After the demigod addressed as Death Consul by the Demoness of Unaging visited her, she had deliberately concealed this matter and didn't quickly inform Mithor. She allowed the punishment to continue as she felt that it was naturally for the best if his investigations turned fruitful. Even if there was nothing, she wouldn't be too disappointed either.

She never thought about the possibility of Mithor being placed at risk, as it was a part of the punishment.

But to her surprise, Mithor had been killed so quickly!

The blonde female attendant handed over the telegram and said, "It was done by an adventurer named Gehrman Sparrow. It can be confirmed that he had used a Beyonder power similar to Dragon's Might."

"Gehrman Sparrow... Dragon's Might... Heh heh. Qilangos also knew Dragon's Might. He likely Grazed a Psychiatrist or Hypnotist with Creeping Hunger." Tracy sneered after receiving the telegram as she spoke to herself.

She was quite certain that Gehrman Sparrow was the enemy who had disguised himself as Helene to attack her. Furthermore, he had been using the Faceless powers which Qilangos had Grazed.

Creeping Hunger is with him, and he has an ancient demigod, who Mother addressed as Death Consul, backing him... Does this mean that Qilangos was really killed by that Death Consul? Tracy muttered silently as she waved her hand to send the blonde attendant out.

After the door to the captain's cabin closed, she chuckled to herself.

If I were to divulge this information, the organization that ordered Qilangos to assassinate Duke Negan would definitely be very interested.

For a brief moment, she truly had the urge to do so, but her reason stopped her.

This was because it meant that she would directly offend the Death Consul!

And on the off chance that he was recognized as the murderer, she would immediately be suspected of divulging the information.

When the time comes, unless I hide at Mother's side, death's shadow will hang over me every moment... I'm not afraid of other Saints. They would have to find me before attacking, and there would certainly be warning signs. The process would take considerable time, giving me ample opportunity to escape, b-but the Death Consul can travel through the spirit world. The moment he determines my location, he can appear beside me... Tracy bit her lip, deeply troubled.

She abandoned the idea and decided to keep track of Gehrman Sparrow's whereabouts. She wouldn't spare him once a good opportunity arose!

At this moment, Klein was still living in the Teana Inn, waiting for his bounty to be delivered to him.

Chapter 603: Conditions

10 p.m. Swordfish Bar.

After waiting all afternoon without receiving his bounty, Klein deliberately went out. Oz Kent told him that the process had begun, but the money would not arrive until the following morning.

He disguised himself and participated in a private exchange among the adventurers in the Swordfish Bar, but he didn't find the main ingredients of the Marionettist potion or any mystical items.

After restoring his appearance, Klein pressed down his hat and squeezed through the crowd, leaving the bar at its rowdiest, noisiest hour.

Gazes swept across his face. Most people looked momentarily startled, then froze for two seconds as recognition dawned.

Suddenly, they retracted their gaze and retreated far away from Klein like the receding tide.

They had already learned of Gehrman Sparrow's appearance and strength in the papers and rumors. More details had gradually spread in areas where adventurers, pirates, and gang members gathered. Many people gained a basic understanding of the present situation and knew that Gehrman Sparrow had taken the crazy action of instantly drawing his revolver once he discovered Wormtongue Mithor King. Therefore, everyone knew better and chose to stay away from the dangerous man.

The merchant, Ralph, was drinking in front of the bar when he suddenly sensed the disturbance. He instinctively half-turned his body around and looked over.

He soon spotted a quiet gentleman with a thin face and chiseled features, and the portrait from the News Report surfaced in his mind--Gehrman Sparrow, the man who had hunted Wormtongue!

Then, Ralph recalled his conversation with the Resistance leader Kalat that evening. He had confirmed that the crazy adventurer before him wasn't averse to the Sea God, and he had even witnessed the private trade between the Resistance and Vice Admiral Iceberg.

Perhaps I can convert him into a believer of the Sea God... Even if he has no interest in the archipelago in the future, he might still be able to help with certain matters... Ralph clasped his hands and held them to his mouth as though blowing into a conch. This was one of the gestures used to pray to Sea God Kalvetua.

Then, he suddenly got up and walked to Gehrman Sparrow with a beer in hand.

At this moment, Klein also noticed Ralph's approach and recognized him.

This was a devout believer willing to offer a third of his twenty-thousand-pound fortune to the Sea God!

This was the former pirate, now a merchant, whom he had persuaded to establish a children's charitable foundation!

Based on the news regarding the charity foundation and his frequent pious praying, he understood Ralph in all aspects. Therefore, he was very puzzled as to why the man would attempt to approach Gehrman Sparrow.

He has a job to entrust to me? Things that the Resistance isn't able to do for some reason or another? Klein looked at Ralph as he slowed down his pace.

"Mr. Gehrman Sparrow?" Ralph raised the cup of beer in his hand.

Klein nodded and maintained his persona by saying, "I don't know you."

"Haha, it's always so simple to get to know each other among adventurers. Perhaps a cup of beer is all it takes." Ralph pointed at the bar counter. "Are you interested in a drink?"

"Alright." Deeply puzzled, Klein answered simply.

They sat in the corner of the bar as he ordered a cup of Southville beer. As he drank, he looked at Ralph without saying a word.

Being stared at silently by a powerhouse at the level of a pirate admiral wasn't a good experience. Ralph drank a mouthful of beer to hide his high-strung nerves and laughed.

"I've heard about you. Even before what you did today, I knew you were a true gentleman who harbored no prejudice against the colonial natives."

Different things to say flashed through Klein's mind as he finally decided on a few sentences that corresponded to his persona.

"Get to the point."

Ralph nearly choked as he cleared his throat.

"Do I have the honor to introduce you to our god, the savior of the archipelago, the Blessed of the sea, Kalvetua?

"As you know, the sea is vast, and its storms are terrifying. Even the Four Kings cannot guarantee that they will overcome every peril at sea and survive. We need a god--one who answers you and holds authority over the sea and storms."

Introducing me to myself, then asking me to believe in myself... Klein resisted the urge to let his mouth twitch and said, "I'm more interested in whether you have any mystical items with powerful offensive capabilities."

Ralph revealed an honest smile.

"We don't have any.

"However, as long as you piously believe in Sea God, there might be a day when you would be bestowed with one."

I don't have one... Don't make promises for me! Klein instantly found it amusing and laughable.

He felt that he couldn't carry on the conversation. Hence, he downed his half-filled cup of Southville beer and said, "I'll consider it."

He was about to leave when the bartender suddenly came over and squeezed a smile.

"Mr. Gehrman Sparrow, someone entrusted you with a mission."

"What is it?" Klein glanced at Ralph.

Knowing his place, the latter left his seat with his beer.

He was already very pleased with today's outcome. This was because he never had the intention of developing the crazy adventurer into a believer of Sea God in one try. His goal was only to let him know of the possibility and to think about the pros and cons of doing so.

Once silence returned to the corner of the bar, the bartender immediately said with a smile, "He says he's an apothecary you know. He has a mission to entrust to you. If you're interested, you can wait here for him. We will contact him using the method arranged beforehand."

An apothecary I know? The chubby Darkwill? The Darkwill who rears a plump owl? What kind of mission would he have for me? Rescue his teacher, Roy King? That isn't something I will do. The risk is just too high... As his thoughts raced, Klein decided to first figure out the details of the mission.

"Okay."

At nearly 11 p.m., Klein met the apprehensive Darkwill in Billiard Room 3 of the Swordfish Bar.

He had already changed out of his rather eye-catching witch doctor robe and was dressed as a native. He wore a Taraba shirt, pantaloons, and a brown jacket. As for his round-faced owl, it perched silently on his right shoulder, observing the adventurer with penetrating eyes.

This feels very similar to how Miss Justice observes the other members of the Tarot Club... This owl might truly be a Beyonder creature. A Spectator? Klein made a judgment and asked in a deep voice, his expression unchanged, "What mission do you have?"

"It's this." The chubby Apothecary kept his left hand in his pocket and clenched the ring box tightly. "I'll be making a trip to another island. Heh heh, it will probably take three days. Due to certain developments, I might encounter danger. Of course, it might not happen as well. In short, I need a bodyguard, and I think you're the best choice.

The way you put it makes me sound like merely the best among the few people you know... You're not rescuing your teacher, but leaving Bayam to seek help? Or has he already been rescued, with the danger coming from the military's pursuit? Klein considered the truth of the matter and calmly asked, "How great is the danger?"

Darkwill's lips trembled. "I can't be sure. I-if the danger exceeds what you can handle, you can hand me directly to the other party. We'll agree to that now, so it won't damage your reputation."

If I didn't know that you just have a nasty mouth, then I would've imagined this as a form of goading... Klein thought and said, "What kind of payment can you provide?"

Darkwill had intended to give the answer he had prepared long ago, but he hesitated. The matter truly was dangerous. Without sufficient stakes, he could not persuade Gehrman Sparrow to agree. He feared the councilor would appear and that his bodyguard would abandon the fight before the situation became desperate. That was precisely why he needed to hire a powerhouse.

Klein glanced at him and coldly said, "You can first consider it for a moment.

"I'll use the washroom. Tell me your answer when I'm back."

With that said, he turned around and walked to the door. Pulling the handle, he stepped outside.

His response made him seem experienced, possessing the cool detachment of an adventurer and bounty hunter. In truth, he wasn't giving the chubby Apothecary time to think; he was merely seeking an opportunity to enter the washroom and divine the matter above the gray fog.

This was key to whether he accepted the mission!

After leaving the billiard room, he reached the washroom, joined the line, and eventually secured an empty cubicle.

When he entered, he frowned as the dirty environment and disgusting stench left him grossed out. He nearly turned his head and left.

He held back his disgust as he pulled the flush lever in contempt. As he sighed at the terrible environment for his divination, he took four tiny steps counterclockwise and began the ritual.

Inside the billiard room, Darkwill agilely went to close the door after he saw Gehrman Sparrow's back completely disappear. He asked the owl on his shoulder, "Will he betray me?"

"No," the owl mumbled. "Also, when asking me questions, be polite. Call me Mr. Harry."

The fat on Darkwill's face trembled.

"Mr. Harry, what kind of payment do you think he will accept?"

"I can't see through him. He's good at concealing his emotions," the owl said frankly.

Darn Mr. Harry. No, darn silly bird! Darkwill cursed inwardly as he paced around and considered the chips he could offer.

After a while, Klein returned to Billiard Room 3 and asked, "Have you thought it through?"

He had already divined above the gray fog that the mission's danger was acceptable, and he happened to plan on leaving Bayam for the time being.

"800 pounds for three days, as well as our friendship. I'm referring to the friendship of my teacher and his friends," Darkwill said without confidence.

Klein remained silent for a few seconds and said, "1,000 pounds for three days.

"In addition, your organization needs to help me obtain a mystical item with powerful offensive abilities. I will pay in cash based on a reasonable price."

1,000 pounds. How could I have that much... Darkwill hesitated for a moment before saying, "I'll pay an advance of 300 pounds. The rest will be paid by the person I'm looking for when we arrive at my destination."

He planned on getting his teacher's teacher to pay the rest.

Klein nodded slightly and said, "Deal."

Darkwill immediately heaved a sigh of relief before puffing out his cheeks and asking with a smile, "May I assume that the protection takes effect immediately?"

Chapter 604: A Different Enemy

Upon hearing the chubby Apothecary's question, Klein smiled inwardly as he wore a stoic expression.

"From the moment you make the first payment."

Darkwill immediately took out a thick wad of cash and counted it, his heart aching with every note.

"Here's 300 pounds.

"You can fulfill your promise now." Klein accepted the notes and nodded. "That won't be a problem."

Darkwill immediately felt relieved, as though he was a drowning person who had finally caught onto a float.

Half an hour later, in the Teana Inn, Darkwill watched in a daze as the powerful adventurer, Gehrman Sparrow, said to the reception counter, "Switch us to a luxurious suite."

With that said, Klein took two steps backward as though giving his spot to the chubby Apothecary.

Darkwill swallowed and asked hesitantly, "I'm paying?"

"During the mission, all expenses are borne by the employer. This is the rule adventurers follow," Klein said without a change in expression.

I'd be a dumbass if I believed you! Am I to help you pay if you call for a few Red Theater prostitutes? Darkwill thought as he squeezed out a smile.

"We can use an ordinary room. This will make the protection job easier."

"Then stay by yourself." Klein slipped effortlessly into his Gehrman Sparrow persona.

Darkwill gave a dry laugh, walked to the counter, and said expressionlessly, "A luxurious suite."

When Darkwill finally checked in and entered the smaller bedroom, he opened the window and left an "opening" for the owl, Mr. Harry. He took out the ring box in his pocket and checked the situation of the strange die.

After confirming that the die remained unchanged, with four facing upward, Darkwill gradually breathed a sigh of relief.

Backlund, inside an ordinary house.

Ince Zangwill woke up from his slumber, and the first thing he did was check on his body.

He had to repeat this inspection every day, because he never knew what kind of story 0-08 might compose while he slept or what unforeseen accidents it might cause.

After confirming that he wasn't injured, Ince Zangwill put on a pair of gleaming leather boots and rose.

Without any surprises, he saw 0-08 silently sitting on his desk as though it was an ordinary quill.

However, it was locked inside a metal box covered in symbols and magic labels.

Ince Zangwill walked over with a heavy heart and grabbed 0-08. He flipped open the notebook beside him and discovered that there was another page of text.

"Ince Zangwill couldn't recall what he had done last night, but he acutely sensed some problems.

"He looked into the mirror and found his reflection somewhat unfamiliar, as though his body had given birth to another Ince Zangwill.

"He looked down and saw there were obvious abnormalities under his fingernails, but he couldn't recall what he had done the previous night no matter how hard he tried..."

After seeing this description, Ince Zangwill instinctively looked at the full-body mirror in the room. He saw that he was still blind in one eye. His classic chiseled face didn't have a single wrinkle, but there was a smile lingering at the edges of his mouth. It formed a stark contrast with his heavy gaze.

At that moment, Ince Zangwill noticed that his face was slightly livid. He had bags under his eyes, which, paired with the inexplicable smile, made him appear sinister, vicious, and strange.

He raised his hands and looked down. Black stains filled the spaces beneath his fingernails, as though he had spent half the night digging up roots in the garden.

Although he had switched from the Death pathway to the Evernight pathway, and he had become a Nightwatcher, Ince Zangwill didn't lose the Beyonder powers he had from before. He was still a powerful Spirit Guide, a Spirit Guide in the domain of dead spirits. Therefore, he immediately planned to communicate with the spirits inside and outside the house to figure out what had happened last night.

At that point, he caught sight of the notebook's final passage from the corner of his eye.

"Ince Zangwill attempted to channel spirits, but sadly he realized that it was to no avail. It was as though a person of a similar profession had wiped out all clues. He was very worried, unaware of what he had embroiled himself with the previous night."

Ince Zangwill's expression grew grave as he attempted to channel the spirits. As expected, the attempt yielded no pleasant surprises.

On Wednesday morning, outside the Amyris Leaf Bar, which had changed hands behind the scenes.

Klein circled around to a secluded alley and saw Oz Kent carrying a tiny suitcase.

"Your bounty." Oz Kent tossed him the small suitcase.

This wasn't the official bounty in the strict sense. The military had advanced the money, since claiming the actual bounty involved the governor-general's office and the kingdom's Ministry of Finance. The complicated procedure required at least three days.

Klein caught the suitcase and opened it on the spot. He saw stacks of neatly arranged cash, with most of them in one- or five-pound denominations.

"A total of 5,400 pounds. We didn't take a cut," Oz Kent said as he forced a smile.

If it were any other adventurer, he would have given 4,000 pounds at most. The rest was the income for the personnel involved in the entire procedure.

But faced with a madman whose strength approached that of a pirate admiral, fear of being executed on the spot had forced him to work for free.

The military is unlikely to fool me with fake notes... Klein grabbed a stack of cash and waved it.

5,400 pounds... Hiding outside the alley, Darkwill heard Oz Kent's words and secretly shot a glance at the suitcase. The stacks of pound notes seemed to blind him.

This was the first time he was seeing so much money placed together.

An adventurer really is a profitable profession. Successfully hunting a pirate earns thousands of pounds. Protecting me nets him 1,000 pounds and one request. Occasionally, he can find sunken ships and treasure... Why did I become an apothecary instead of an adventurer? If I had known, I would have chosen that lucky Beyonder pathway... Darkwill thought with atypical admiration.

However, he quickly recalled his reality. Most adventurers didn't live such rich lives. Even if they could make a killing, a third of their income was given to apothecaries, either to treat their injuries and remove any latent injuries, or to buy medicine to add to the fun and deal with unmentionable diseases.

It's still safer being an apothecary... Darkwill sighed inwardly.

After only a rough count of the money, Klein closed the suitcase and said to Oz Kent, "I'll be leaving Bayam for a while. If I obtain any intelligence, how should I inform you?"

"You're leaving Bayam?" Oz Kent asked in surprise.

Klein nodded slightly and said, "I received a bodyguard mission."

Understanding dawned on Oz Kent, followed by an inexplicable sense of relief.

If this madman stays in Bayam for too long, sooner or later he'll cause trouble. It's better for him to drift out at sea and fight those pirates... Oz Kent immediately said with a smile, "You can send me a telegram directly and include my address. We have people planted at the telegraph office."

"Alright." Dressed in a black tweed coat, Klein didn't speak further. With the suitcase in hand, he turned to leave the alley.

He headed for the harbor with Darkwill, planning to catch the earliest liner to Oravi Island. The owl flew furtively between the trees. They had already bought scalped tickets the night before.

Half an hour before they boarded the ship, Darkwill reeled with anxiety. He was afraid the person who had betrayed his teacher would suddenly rush over and attack him.

The feeling persisted until he entered his first-class cabin. Once the liner put to sea, Beyonders who could neither fly nor command ships would find it difficult to intercept him.

"Quickly start the ship. Quickly start the ship..." Darkwill looked out the window as he muttered to himself.

At this moment, the owl had followed and perched on his left shoulder. Klein sat on a chair in the room, prepared for any possible attacks.

The sky suddenly darkened. The wind rose to a howl, and the air grew noticeably more humid.

"A storm?" Darkwill blurted, his mouth falling open.

This meant that the ship might not set sail at the scheduled time. It might be delayed for hours!

That would expose Darkwill to many unnecessary risks!

He turned his head to look at Gehrman Sparrow and forced a smile.

"Do you have any solutions?"

I do have one. For example, you can immediately pray to Sea God Kalvetua, and I'll answer your prayer from above the gray fog. I'll disperse the storm... But without a doubt, Sea King Jahn Kottman will rush over within minutes, or perhaps seconds. His attacks will arrive even sooner... Klein looked at the chubby Apothecary and said calmly, "I'm only an adventurer."

Darkwill knew that he shouldn't have had any expectations. He cursed the weather silently and turned around, looking out the window to check on the exact situation.

Boom!

A silver bolt of lightning struck Darkwill before Klein could even react.

Darkwill immediately collapsed, convulsing as smoke emitted from his body. His skin was charred as snakes of lightning buzzed.

Klein nearly went into shock. This was the first time he had seen someone struck by lightning during a storm.

That's way too unlucky, right... He briefly forgot to rescue Darkwill.

The owl, Mr. Harry, was similarly stunned for a few seconds before it screamed, "Quick! Quick! There's a bottle of medicine in the second secret pocket on his left. Feed it to him!"

This owl can talk... Klein pricked up his brows, took a few steps forward, bent down, and found a bottle of blood-red medicine. Then, he poured it down Darkwill's throat.

After a few minutes, Darkwill finally regained his senses. As charred black skin peeled off, he stood up with great difficulty.

"I-I'll deal with my wounds."

He staggered to his room and locked the door.

After doing all of that, he took out the ring box and opened it with a solemn look.

In the ring box with almost no room to roll, the milky-white die had already changed at some point in time. Two red dots were facing up!

In the living room outside, Klein stood on the spot and recalled everything that had happened before frowning.

He had the inexplicable feeling that the enemies he would face on this bodyguard mission would differ from any he had encountered before.

After Darkwill partially recovered and came out, Klein sat on the chair and leaned forward slightly.

"Explain.

"This determines how I should provide you with protection."

Chapter 605: Judgment

At Gehrman Sparrow's words, Darkwill's first impulse was to reveal everything about the strange die. He wanted the adventurer on whom he had spent such a vast sum to grasp the root of the problem and protect him effectively.

On second thought, however, he recalled his teacher's warnings. The die might well be an important Sealed Artifact of the Life School of Thought, and he feared the truth might tempt the adventurer and lead to even worse developments!

There are plenty of rumors about adventurers killing their employers during missions to obtain greater benefits. I don't know Gehrman Sparrow, and I have no idea what kind of person he is. I need to be cautious! Besides, the die has automatically rolled a two. The situation isn't at its worst or most desperate. I can still wait for a chance... Darkwill hesitated for a few seconds and finally decided to discuss superficial matters while avoiding the key points.

He instinctively avoided Gehrman Sparrow's seemingly calm gaze.

"It's this. My teacher and I belong to a secret organization. I'm escaping because a traitor has appeared internally.

"They control the pathway related to fate and can make themselves sufficiently lucky while making their target unlucky... I might have been cursed, which is why I was unlucky enough to be struck by lightning."

After the explanation, he forcefully hid the nervousness in his heart and awaited Gehrman Sparrow's reply.

Will he believe me? An adventurer with this much experience and strength is probably difficult to deceive... If he discovers that I'm lying, he'll likely sink me into the ocean... Darkwill stood there anxiously, like a student summoned to the teacher's desk.

He's indeed from the Life School of Thought... Monster pathway... Klein nodded in thought.

"I understand.

"Try to do as little as possible. I will consider how I'll deal with your bad luck."

Uh... Darkwill was taken aback, completely unable to believe that Gehrman Sparrow would so easily accept a lie he fabricated on the spot.

He forced a smile and hurriedly expressed his gratitude. Then he returned to his room, leaned against the door, and took out the ring box.

Pa! His hand trembled as he opened the lid, only to discover that the milky-white die had somehow turned on its own, leaving the six facing upward!

Does this mean I was lucky enough to fool Gehrman Sparrow? Darkwill realized.

Mr. Harry swooped down and didn't choose to perch on the chubby Apothecary's shoulder. Apparently, it was still somewhat worried about how it could've also been implicated when Darkwill was nearly struck by lightning.

It stood on a wooden desk and stared ahead with its round eyes.

"Darkwill, you're very nervous."

"You don't have to tell me that," Darkwill said in frustration.

"I believe I should consider changing owners.

"Gehrman Sparrow seems to be a pretty good choice."

"...Then what about me?" Darkwill asked in surprise, momentarily forgetting his anger.

"If I were you, I would've thrown it out the window and into the sea and gotten your teacher's teacher to fish it out himself."

"...How do you know about my teacher's teacher?" Darkwill blurted out the question.

Mr. Harry proudly lifted its head and said, "Don't doubt an owl's vision."

Darkwill fell into thought, not dwelling on its answer.

"No, that won't work. Throwing the die into the sea wouldn't resolve all my problems.

You lack general knowledge in mysticism, and you don't know how some important Sealed Artifacts are just like the most popular prostitutes in Red Theater. They'll always be able to attract hungry men."

"Including you," Harry calmly replied. "As for my lack of general knowledge in mysticism, I believe a famous quote explains it. Emperor Roselle once said that if a child doesn't receive a good education, it's the father's fault. This sentence can also be used to describe problems between a pet and its owner. Regardless, Darkwill, I believe you need to discuss the die with Gehrman Sparrow. Otherwise, the danger will only increase."

"Let me wait and see a little longer. Perhaps it will be fixed at six..." Darkwill said with some hesitation.

He sat on the edge of the bed before lying down.

The howling storm gradually subsided and the sky began to clear. The liner sounded its horn, signaling its departure.

Inside the first-class cabin's living room, Klein looked out the window and saw blurry rainbows that appeared after the rain. Yet, his heart wasn't calm.

He wasn't too afraid of enemies. Out at sea, apart from the Four Kings and the official demigods, or the pirate admirals who appeared with their fleet, there were very few matters that could threaten him, who had Creeping Hunger and several mystical items. Even if the ship was sunk, he was able to escape thanks to the many charms he had from the Sea God domain.

But he had no confidence whatsoever where luck was concerned. It lay beyond his abilities, and he could think of no way to contend with it.

Although my title is "the King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck," that was adapted from the luck enhancement ritual. And the luck enhancement ritual clearly wasn't meant to help people escape bad luck... Darkwill's matter is quite difficult to handle. I can only put more effort into keeping a close eye on his situation. I'll immediately rescue him if some potentially fatal misfortune occurs... Let's hope we can last these few days until we arrive at Oravi Island. His seniors should be able to help him change his luck... Klein rubbed his temples, but he showed no outward sign of concern.

Darkwill, who had been too nervous the previous night, fell into a deep sleep without realizing it.

After a while, his stomach's growling informed him that it was time for lunch.

He tried hard to open his eyes, but he felt as though an invisible person was pressing down on him, making it impossible for him to move.

Sharp, swelling pain throbbed at the back of his head. His breathing grew labored, and his heart raced unnaturally.

D-don't tell me I'm going to die suddenly in my sleep... Darkwill struggled with all his might, but he failed to wake up. He grew weaker and weaker over time.

Something sharp pried open his mouth, and an ice-cold liquid poured in. Some spilled down his face, wetting his chin and neck.

Darkwill's body recovered, and he finally managed to open his eyes. He saw two bright, round, golden eyes almost pressed against his face.

Keeping a Beyonder pet can be quite useful sometimes... Darkwill first felt rueful before quickly sitting up and taking out the ring box.

Inside, the die had rolled to another side.

One!

So unlucky that I nearly died in my sleep? No, I don't think it's that simple. It seems to magnify certain probabilities, such as the chance of being struck by lightning or dying suddenly in one's sleep... No, this can't continue. If it does, I'll definitely die! Darkwill thought hysterically.

Horror quickly overwhelmed his thoughts, and he forced his hand to grasp the ring box before rushing to the door.

Perhaps because he had nearly died in his sleep, he was weak and could barely turn the handle.

"Help! Help!" Mr. Harry screamed sharply.

Bang!

The door was opened, slamming into Darkwill's head. If Klein hadn't controlled his strength, the chubby Apothecary's head probably would've cracked open with blood spewing out everywhere.

Without even rubbing his bruise, Darkwill frantically shouted, "It's this! It's this!

"It's this die that gives me bad luck!

"When it lands on one, I'll fail at everything I do!"

He had already decided to inform Gehrman Sparrow of the whole truth, and he looked forward to the powerful adventurer giving him some effective suggestions.

He couldn't rule out the possibility that greed would drive Gehrman Sparrow to kill him, but he had weighed the alternatives. Given a choice, he would certainly choose the less terrible one.

Telling Gehrman Sparrow might lead to death, but not telling him would spell certain death. This choice was no longer something that needed consideration.

I don't care if the die is lost. My life is more important! Darkwill thought with righteous indignation.

Then, he saw a smile appear across Gehrman Sparrow's mouth.

"Thank you for your humor.

"The joke isn't too bad."

C-could it be that even a perfectly logical explanation will fail... The chubby Apothecary suddenly fell into despair.

"It's real! He's speaking the truth!" Harry flapped its wings.

Darkwill, who felt hope arise in him, heard Gehrman Sparrow say in a deep voice, "Then why don't you throw it into the ocean?"

With that said, Klein politely closed the door and returned to the living room.

This fellow must be hiding a secret. I mustn't be fooled by such a clumsy excuse... Klein sat in his chair and waited for Darkwill to describe the situation in detail.

Darkwill sat down, depressed. He sat there motionless, afraid of encountering another failure.

Before lunch, Klein entered the washroom to relieve himself.

After washing his hands, he went above the gray fog while he was at it, preparing to browse through the prayers of the Sea God believers.

Just as he sat in The Fool's high-backed chair, he suddenly recalled numerous details, and his eyes widened.

H-how could I believe such a weak explanation...

Why do I think that it's because of that die?

During both incidents, it felt as though my mind had been clouded. No, that's not it. Darkwill's explanation happened to agree with certain theories of mine, so I instinctively accepted the falsehood as truth. Klein's pupils contracted as he considered it.

At that moment, he had already made a judgment.

That die is indeed problematic!

Chapter 606: Intimidation

Above the gray fog, inside the palace that resembled a giant's residence.

Klein tapped the edge of the mottled table, his thoughts revolving around the strange die.

It can influence me without me realizing it... This is already somewhat similar to 0-08 from back then. It's just that one is right in front of me, while the other is hidden... It's definitely a Sealed Artifact, and it's the kind that exists at the very pinnacle. Even if it's not a Grade 0, it has to be a very special existence among Grade 1s...

I'm temporarily unable to interfere with matters involving fate, but I can't simply face it passively. As time passes, objects at the level of Sealed Artifacts often cause increasingly severe damage. It might begin to affect the people around the chubby Apothecary, such as me or all the passengers on the ship... Klein carefully considered the matter but could think of no solution. He decided to return to the real world immediately.

He didn't know how to seal the die or diminish its influence, but he knew someone who might.

It was the Snake of Mercury, Will Auceptin, who was still inside his mother's womb as a fetus!

He folded the paper crane back to its original form, stuffed it in his wallet, and went to the door of the servant's room before knocking on it twice.

This didn't violate Gehrman Sparrow's persona, for he was a gentlemanly madman. Of course, Klein was chiefly afraid that flinging open the door might bash Darkwill to death--a lesson he had learned from the Final Destination film franchise.

In addition, Klein was also worried about witnessing something disgusting or unpleasant.

Given the preferences Darkwill has exhibited, if he realizes he's in extreme danger and has no way to escape, leaving him with no choice but to wait for death, there's a nonzero chance he'll pleasure himself to climax one last time... Klein lampooned as he heard the chubby Apothecary's weak reply, "What's the matter?"

It's good that you aren't dead yet... Klein carefully opened the door and leaned forward slightly, looking at Darkwill and the open ring box in front of him. He calmly asked, "What's the score?"

"Can't you check the newspaper yourself... the sports section..." Darkwill remained listless.

"I'm asking about the die," Klein simply added.

"It's still on three..." Darkwill answered instinctively before turning his head and jumping up. "You believe me?"

Klein didn't reply so as to prevent him from recalling how Gehrman Sparrow had been fooled by the die.

He turned around and said in an unperturbed tone, "Bring the die outside."

"Alright!"

"Alright!"

Darkwill and Harry cried out in joy almost at the same time.

They either raised their arms or wings!

After the chubby Apothecary carefully carried the ring box out, Klein pointed at the coffee table and said, "Put it on there."

With that, he sat on the sofa beside it. He placed his arms on his thighs and leaned forward to observe the strange die.

On the surface, the die didn't seem mystically unusual in any way. The only difference from an ordinary die was that even its three pips were dyed red.

Klein didn't touch it out of caution. He slightly straightened his body and looked at Darkwill, who was seated across him on a chair, as well as the fat owl, Harry, who was perched on the chair's back.

Darkwill concealed nothing further. With a smile uglier than tears, he said, "This is an important Sealed Artifact belonging to our organization. My mission is to deliver it to a particular person on Oravi Island.

"When it rolls a six, the wielder becomes extremely lucky. Everything succeeds with relative ease, just as my clumsy lie managed to fool you."

Don't mention this matter... You will one day be taught a serious lesson because of that mouth of yours... Klein listened with a stoic expression.

Darkwill continued, "One means that the wielder will be extremely unlucky. Nothing will succeed; you wouldn't believe me even if I were to tell you the whole truth...

"Two should mean bad luck of a lesser degree, but as for why it caused me to be struck by lightning... It's inexplicable!

"Three and four represent ordinary degrees of bad and good luck. That much has been confirmed. Five is the opposite of two."

You aren't unlucky enough; otherwise, I would've already beaten you up... Klein thought before calmly instructing, "From this moment forth, both of you will take turns monitoring this die. Once it drops below three, you are to inform me immediately."

"Including me?" The owl perched on the edge of the chair's back raised its right wing.

Klein leaned back on the sofa, crossed his right leg, and said calmly, "This will help you maintain your focus and energy."

And now, Gehrman Sparrow didn't show any fear, and he even seemed very confident!

Darkwill secretly sighed in relief and thought, His performance was really professional...

He isn't afraid at all!

He can even escape the die's influence and accept my explanation. He lives up to his reputation as the powerful adventurer who easily hunted Wormtongue! He's definitely a powerhouse at the pirate admiral level!

Darkwill stood up and walked to the door to pull the service bell.

Just as he returned to the sofa, he heard Harry's sharp screech.

"It has changed! It has changed!

"Two!"

Bang!

Did a sailor's gun misfire? Or did the bullet ricochet when aiming at some seabird or something? Regardless of the situation, the chances of that happening are very low; yet... Klein turned his head to the chubby Apothecary.

"Are you injured?"

Darkwill shook his head with a lingering sense of fear.

"I'm fine."

Over the period that followed, Klein dealt with more than ten accidents in succession. The room's chandelier suddenly collapsed; a maintenance worker slipped and dropped a hammer straight toward Darkwill's head; and Darkwill nearly choked to death on a fishbone.

These didn't seem like anything serious, but for Klein to remain highly focused, it was inevitable for him to be exhausted.

Thankfully, the die later rolled to four on its own, finally ending the disaster-fraught hour.

No, I can't continue like this. Who knows if something will happen to me when I'm rescuing him... Klein sent away the captain who came to apologize and said to Darkwill, "I'll sleep for half an hour.

The chubby Apothecary and the owl nodded like hungry woodpeckers.

When he woke up in his dream, he heaved a sigh of relief when he saw the pitch-black desolate plains. He followed the same-old path and reached the depths of the black steeple.

Dense new lines of silvery writing covered the raised ground surrounded by tarot cards.

"It's called the Die of Probability.

This doesn't mean that two is safer than one, because once the probability is high enough, any accident can happen. Three increases the probability of unfavorable events. Four through six have the opposite effects.

"The Die of Probability has living characteristics. It always makes the wielder swing back and forth between having good and bad luck. If one isn't careful, it's possible to die from an accident. When it awakens to a certain degree, it will control the people and objects around the wielder, controlling the probability of their actions.

"Although it hasn't been confirmed, I suspect that given enough time, it will be able to influence the entire world, making every action of every living being determined by its result. Deities, of course, are excluded."

To put it simply, it's digitizing the entire world... Th-this is definitely at the level of a Grade 0 Sealed Artifact. It's extremely dangerous! How terrifying... Klein couldn't help but frown as he eagerly continued reading.

"To truly seal the Die of Probability is rather complicated. To put it simply, it's to use specific Sealed Artifacts to isolate it from the spirit world, astral world, and the real world. You can give it a try.

"Haha, I'll reveal my cards. I have long discovered that you can resist my prophetic senses. Perhaps you can use it to seal it.
