"Finally, thank you for your help.

Sincerely yours,

Will Auceptin"

As expected of a Snake of Fate. He has indeed discovered my uniqueness... Throwing the Die of Probability above the gray fog should be able to seal it. But the problem is that a Grade 0 Sealed Artifact with living characteristics might influence the space above the gray fog and chase me away, making itself the owner... Such matters cannot be answered with divination. It will definitely be able to resist, just like 0-08... Klein rejected the idea of using the gray fog.

As the thoughts whirled in his mind, he suddenly had an idea.

Klein quickly left his dream and woke up. He said to Darkwill, "I have a method of suppressing its influence."

"What is it?" Darkwill asked in pleasant surprise.

Klein didn't answer. He picked up the Die of Probability, which had landed on four, and entered the washroom.

What does he want to do? I remember that in some folklore, evil objects are treated by throwing shit on them... Ugh... Darkwill didn't wish to pursue the thought.

After entering the washroom and locking the door, Klein immediately set up a ritual to summon himself. Following that, he went above the gray fog and brought out the iron cigar case.

Then, he picked up the Die of Probability and opened the cigar case. Inside the cigar case was the All-Black Eye which came from the Marionettist.

Under the darkness, Klein's face was cloaked in the shadows and he watched with a deep and gloomy gaze as he brought the Die of Probability close to the strange eye. It was a Beyonder characteristic which contained the True Creator's mental corruption.

The corners of his mouth cracked open as he revealed a genial smile and whispered to the die in his hand, "Here, let me treat you to a concert."

Chapter 607: Discovering an Abnormality

The washroom window stood too high to admit much sunlight. What little entered barely dispelled the darkness, leaving everything gloomy.

Klein moved the Die of Probability in his right hand to the All-Black Eye, and suddenly, it shook. Then, he threw the item into the other side of the washbasin.

The milky-white die rolled a few times before finally stopping with four red dots pointing up.

Suppressing the faint smile at his lips, Klein stepped aside and picked up the Die of Probability again. He lowered his head and asked with gentlemanly courtesy, "You don't wish to listen?

"Let's do this. Give me an answer. 6 represents cooperation, while everything else represents a rejection."

With that said, he threw the Die of Probability up and reached out to catch it.

The milky-white die fell straight down and rolled rapidly, showing six red dots!

"Very good," Klein praised with a chuckle.

After throwing the All-Black Eye back above the gray fog, he turned to open the door to the washroom and slowly walked to the living room.

Under the expectant, worried, curious, and bewildered gazes of Darkwill and Harry, he abruptly stopped and tossed out the Die of Probability.

"No!"

"No!"

Darkwill and the owl cried out together, afraid it would land on three or lower. Harry instinctively flew upward, distancing himself from the fat man who might be struck by lightning.

With a crisp clatter, the milky-white die rolled several times across the coffee table before settling on two.

Just as the color drained from Darkwill's face, the die rolled languidly and settled on four.

"For the next 12 hours, it will be relatively quiet." Klein calmly sat down and began enjoying his breakfast which had long turned cold.

He really had a solution? Darkwill lowered his body and stared at the strange die on the coffee table.

After nearly a minute, he could no longer resist reaching out and throwing the die. It landed on six.

As soon as his palm left it, the die moved on its own to four.

How magical... What method did Gehrman Sparrow actually use? Did he really shit and soak the die in it? Ugh... Darkwill decided to accept the result and stop wondering about the reason, lest he vomit.

Looking up at Gehrman Sparrow, who was calmly buttering his bread, Darkwill suddenly felt that a promise and 1,000 pounds were an extraordinary bargain for a bodyguard of this caliber!

He can definitely be ranked alongside any pirate admiral! If a tycoon encountered a situation like mine, they wouldn't hesitate to hire him for half their wealth... Thankfully, I only paid 300 pounds. Teacher and the others will bear the rest... Relieved that he didn't need to worry about the die rolling at random for the next twelve hours, Darkwill couldn't help standing up and stretching his back.

He walked to the window and opened the tightly shut window to see the sparse clouds scattered across the clear skies.

The boundless azure sea stirred beneath the bright sunlight as though strewn with countless fragments of gold. Darkwill felt invigorated, a burden lifting from his chest.

Unlike Backlund, which would only become warm at the end of February, the Rorsted Archipelago was no longer plagued by the cold. Life had returned to this area.

Facing the sea, with flowers blooming in the warmth of spring... Klein, who had finished his bread, walked to a spot behind Darkwill and felt like an animal awakening from hibernation.

He didn't recite the poem that flashed through his mind. First, it didn't suit Gehrman Sparrow's persona; second, it would probably make Darkwill marvel at Emperor Roselle's poetic talent.

Until the evening, the die still didn't roll, but the weather had changed. Strong winds howled as dark clouds gathered. A storm was brewing.

This was the most common danger at sea. Even along safe, well-traveled routes, ships occasionally encountered such conditions, though usually less terrifying ones.

Klein looked at the tall waves and the dark sky, and he felt as though the ship was cruising through a mountain valley. By the sides were tall dark-blue "cliffs" that could topple down at any moment.

The intense pressure made even a Mid-Sequence Beyonder like him feel oppressed. He felt the urge to pray to a god, hoping that the liner would weather the storm safely.

It's no wonder sailors, pirates, and merchants who live at sea for extended periods can't help but respect the Lord of Storms. They would more or less believe in "Him"... Klein sighed inwardly.

Although he didn't believe a storm of this strength could sink a liner powered by both sails and steam engines, he remained cautious and prayed to his other identity, Sea God Kalvetua.

He was afraid that the Die of Probability would suddenly go crazy during the storm and roll a one, causing the ship to sink to the bottom of the sea. Therefore, he decided to take precautions in advance. Klein did believe the Snake of Fate Will Auceptin's judgment that the Die of Probability would remain quiet for twelve hours after the first act of intimidation, but that was under normal conditions. He believed that a Sealed Artifact with living characteristics was more likely to respond to situations like a person than according to rigid rules. Hence, he had to guard against any trouble it might cause.

Stealing a glance at Darkwill and the owl who were looking somewhat uneasy due to the storm, Klein calmly said, "I'll take a nap.

"Continue watching the die. Take turns and do not relax."

"Alright." Watching Gehrman Sparrow enter the master bedroom, Darkwill walked to the side of the coffee table and sat down. As he stared at the die, he asked Harry, "What thoughts do you have now?"

Harry flew around and mumbled, "Why am I not a petrel?"

In the master bedroom, Klein went above the gray fog and summoned the Sea God Scepter from the junk pile.

He held the scepter with blue gems embedded at the top and quickly replied to his prayer.

Klein didn't expend too much of his spirituality to calm the storm. First, they weren't far from the waters surrounding the Rorsted Archipelago. Such an obvious and exaggerated supernatural phenomenon could easily attract the attention of Sea King Jahn Kottman, who shared the same domain, or the Storm Priests aboard the ships. Second, he had to conserve his strength to deal with the Die of Probability.

What he did was simple. He augmented the ship with layers of Beyonder effects, allowing it to sail steadily through the storm without any risk of capsizing.

For a Sequence 5 or 6 Beyonder below the rank of demigod, accomplishing something similar for a liner carrying hundreds of people would require extensive preparations, a complicated ritual, and near-total exhaustion. For a Sea God who ruled this domain, however, it was simple and effortless.

A demigod already possesses traces of divinity... Klein sighed and threw the scepter back into the junk pile before silently disappearing from above the gray fog.

In the time that followed, the ship bobbed up and down like a leaf in the wind, but no matter how terrifying or towering the dark blue waves were, nothing happened to it until the end of the storm.

At 4 p.m. on Thursday, on Williams Street in Backlund.

Fors Wall once again arrived here by carriage.

She didn't sit in the coffee shop again. Instead, she strolled down the street and observed the pedestrians and surrounding buildings. She also took note of figures with special traits as material for her novel.

There really are a lot of foreigners. They're still mainly from Feysac and Intis. Heh heh, one of them is a barbaric and stocky white bear, while the other is a colorful, ostentatious rooster... Fors chuckled silently to herself.

At this moment, she came to the middle of Williams Street where there was an abandoned chapel. Withered vines crawled over its walls, and gray stones were strewn everywhere.

Not wishing to waste Mr. World's payment, Fors deliberately approached it to check for any abnormalities.

She circled around the chapel without discovering anything.

She then entered, avoiding the disgusting excrement and rotting debris as she swiftly inspected every corner.

Suddenly, her gaze came to a stop as she frowned bit by bit.

In a particular corner of the half-collapsed ruin, the ground had been dug up by someone. The pit wasn't too big or deep, and there were remnant traces of it being dug with fingers!

This should be considered an abnormality, right... Fors cautiously retreated and didn't do a detailed investigation.

After traversing the whole of Williams Street, she immediately returned home and organized her notes on the anomaly together with the material she had gathered. She then sacrificed the report to Mr. Fool and asked Him to pass it to The World. Fors concealed nothing she had seen, even if it appeared innocuous, because she knew full well that The World, rather than someone as ignorant of the situation as she was, should judge what counted as an anomaly.

In the ancient palace above the fog.

Klein immediately began reading the report Miss Magician had submitted.

That abandoned chapel has signs of being dug up? The previous digging attempts had been dealt with by me and Miss Sharron... Who would dig it up again? Rafter Pound, that descendant of the Tudor family? He nearly died from the evil spirit's possession. Without any helpers, it's unlikely he would take further risks... Who could it be... Klein thought for a moment, but he couldn't lock onto a suspect. All he could do was continue reading.

While he read the notes written in a beautiful language, he acutely noticed a point.

There are many foreigners on this street. They are mainly from Feysac and Intis...

It wasn't like that back when I went to Williams Street... Did a Feysac or Intis company recently open there?

Feysac, Intis...

As Klein mulled over the names of those two countries, he suddenly remembered something!

The Einhorn family, Feysac's royal house, and the Sauron family, Intis's former royal house, both controlled the Hunter pathway, also known as the Red Priest pathway. Those were two of the bloodlines the evil spirit had named as capable of breaking the seal!

Together with the descendants of the Medici family, who had been destroyed at Bansy Harbor, all three factions that controlled the Red Priest pathway are involved!

Bansy Harbor was destroyed, causing the Medici family's Beyonder characteristic to contact the evil spirit suspected of being the Red Angel through some mystical connection. Were members of the Einhorn and Sauron families drawn here as a result?

No, that's not realistic. If the Church of Storms didn't discover any Beyonder characteristics, they definitely would've noticed a problem in this...

Another reason? Is the evil spirit using another method to attract people from the Einhorn and Sauron families? What or whom did it use to accomplish this? Only a handful of people know of the evil spirit's existence--me, Miss Sharron, as well as... Amid his racing thoughts, Klein suddenly thought of a possibility.

It was Rafter Pound, the descendant of the Tudor family who had previously been possessed by the evil spirit! He had unknowingly become the evil spirit's slave! He had secretly helped it spread the news!

And the evil spirit had sought me and Miss Sharron's help to numb us into thinking that we are the only ones who can rescue it! For this, it didn't mind betraying its descendants! Klein was alarmed as he felt like he had been fooled by the evil spirit.

Chapter 608: Professional

The boundless gray fog hung in silence until a faint scraping sounded across the bronze table.

Klein shifted in his seat and focused more intently on the details concerning the evil spirit. He grew increasingly convinced that he and Miss Sharron had overlooked the possibility that something had happened to Rafter Pound.

This is a Beyonder power of the Red Priest pathway, Conspirer?

Furthermore, this is even closer to an ordinary fraud. Supernatural powers were used only in the details. Therefore, even after entering the mysterious space above the gray fog, my senses would still have been passively deceived. I could only discover the problem by proactively considering and analyzing the situation?

If it wasn't for Mr. Azik, who knew of the evil spirit suspected to be the dead Red Angel Medici, I wouldn't have realized this problem. Nor would I have hired Miss Magician to seek out any abnormalities on Williams Street...

After a few minutes of contemplation, Klein conjured a pen and paper, preparing to use divination to confirm his theories.

After a moment's deliberation, he wrote the dream divination statement: "Baronet Rafter Pound's current situation."

After putting down his dark red fountain pen, Klein held the piece of paper with the divination statement and leaned back in his chair.

He first recalled the information he had regarding Rafter Pound before closing his eyes, chanting silently, and entering Cogitation.

Klein's thoughts rapidly calmed as he entered a dream state.

In a gray world, disconnected scenes flashed past before finally settling on Unit 29 on Sivellaus Street.

Inside the warm activity room, Rafter Pound was dressed in cotton pajamas, holding a wine cup with red liquid. He was standing silently by the window, looking at the Backlund police station headquarters diagonally across the street.

The baronet had graying sideburns and puffy eye sockets tinged a sickly blackish green. The wrinkles across his forehead and at the corners of his eyes and mouth were far deeper than those of an ordinary man in his forties.

His pupils weren't severely dilated, but they were abnormal. His cheeks were flushed, and a furtive smile played on his lips. He looked distinctly different from the last time Klein had seen him.

Indeed, something is wrong with him... Klein left the dream and began considering how he could deal with the evil spirit.

As always in such situations, his thoughts followed a familiar path. Unable to contact Miss Sharron, his first impulse was to report the matter!

But how should he report it? Klein carefully considered the matter, then conjured The World's figure and had him pray.

"Through a reliable channel, pass this information to the Church of the Evernight Goddess and the Church of the God of Steam and Machinery.

"The information is: High-level spies from Feysac and Intis have gathered on Williams Street for unknown reasons.

"Reward of 100 pounds."

This was the account Klein settled on after careful consideration. Directly mentioning a King of Angels, the Red Priest, the Medici family, or the Tudor Dynasty's ruins would certainly draw the attention of the Churches and military, but it could also make Miss Magician, the informant, a target of the official organizations. The risks were immense.

The claim that "high-level spies from Feysac and Intis have gathered on Williams Street" was not only milder and plausible for an ordinary Beyonder to notice, but serious enough to put the Churches and military on guard. They would dispatch the appropriate experts and employ the most effective methods.

Whatever the subsequent investigation uncovered would be credited entirely to them and have nothing to do with the informant.

Klein had considered getting Mr. Azik to help, but he ultimately chose to abandon the thought. This was because the evil spirit was suspected to be a King of Angels and was very dangerous. Mr. Azik, who was still in the stage of recovering, might not be able to deal with it.

After some thought, Klein turned the conjured scene into a stream of light and transmitted it to the crimson star representing The Magician.

Backlund, Cherwood Borough.

When Fors received the response from Mr. World, she was taken aback.

Those are high-level spies from Feysac and Intis? she exclaimed inwardly, certain that nothing in the information she had provided could support such an inference!

However, she quickly felt at ease, believing that Mr. World had suspected something abnormal on Williams Street precisely because he had received corresponding intelligence about high-level spies. Once he confirmed the presence of people from Feysac and Intis, it was easy for him to reach such a conclusion.

Pass this information to the Church of Evernight and the Church of Steam? Isn't that a euphemistic way of saying "report it"... Unfortunately, I won't be able to watch from the sidelines, or I'm sure a great show would unfold... Fors was no stranger to reporting such matters; after all, her housemate and good friend was a bounty hunter.

She quickly had an idea, deciding to hand the matter of reporting the intel to the experienced Xio.

When she came out of the bedroom, she saw Xio sitting on a sofa. She was flipping through documents about her target, raising her hand to grab at her messy blonde hair from time to time and appearing extremely serious.

Fors casually picked up an item to lend credibility to her approach, walked to the sofa, and held it out.

"Here, have a piece of cake."

Xio glanced at the cake slathered with a layer of cream. Without losing focus, she raised her hand to grab it.

At this moment, Fors flipped her wrist, and the cake in her palm had turned into a golden decorative flower.

"Surprised?" she asked with a smile.

Xio couldn't help but roll her eyes.

"Stop your performance. I prefer food."

"Alright. I have a matter for you to do. 70 pounds." Fors smiled as she sat down.

After dealing with the matter of the evil spirit, Klein returned to the real world and performed a ritual along the way. He brought the radio transceiver, which had been above the gray fog for several days, back to his first-class master bedroom.

He lay in bed and used Cogitation to recover his energy until he was awoken by the tapping sounds.

When Klein opened his eyes, crimson moonlight was already streaming in, veiling both the dim room and the radio transceiver that was automatically feeding out illusory white paper.

This really feels like a horror film... A shame it's connected to a magic mirror without any moral integrity or bottom line... Klein sat up and walked over. He saw lines of Loenese on the illusory white paper.

"Honorable existence above the spirit world, your loyal and humble servant, Arrodes, has arrived and would like to greet you.

"Do you have something you would like to test me on?"

Look at this, look! This is what I call smooth-talking! This is what a professional is like! In that instant, Klein really wished to pull Darkwill into the room to witness the magic mirror's art of communication.

I clearly have questions to ask it, but it turned the situation into me testing it. And it even threw in a question... Klein suppressed the twitching of his lips and replied in a deep voice, "Yes."

"Please speak. The ignorant and limited Arrodes is ready." Amidst the tapping sounds, the illusory paper no longer produced Loenese, but instead produced a fawning smiley face.

It has already started using emoticons... This fellow evolves really quickly... Klein asked directly, "Where can I obtain a mystical item that can steal the Beyonder powers of others?"

The tapping suddenly intensified as the illusory white paper quickly produced scenes resembling movie stills.

They depicted places and people familiar to Klein: the Chanis Gate beneath Saint Selena Cathedral in Tingen City; the handsome, black-haired, green-eyed poet Leonard Mitchell; a middle-aged man sitting on a sofa and smiling at the noble ladies opposite him; and an arrogant young woman loitering in the sewers...

There were twelve scenes in all. Finally, a line of Loenese appeared: "These are the people or places from whom you can acquire one with relative ease or convenience. Many more exist, but they are either difficult or troublesome to reach, or they involve such high levels that I cannot see them clearly."

Not bad. He even knows how to filter the results for me... This is a mystical fantasy version of Google... Klein nodded slightly and, despite knowing the answer, said, "It's your turn to ask."

"You have already answered." Amid the tapping, Arrodes's unsurprising response appeared on the illusory white paper.

Klein chuckled inwardly and asked, "Where will Leonard Mitchell be staying for the time being?"

The tapping sounds became intense again as one scene after another appeared before Klein's eyes.

It was a famous landmark in Backlund, a towering Gothic bell tower with a Bell of Order hanging above.

There was a road sign bearing the words "Pinster Street."

It was Unit 7 along a row of terraced houses. Inside, the extremely suave Leonard Mitchell wore a black coat and red gloves. Klein's poet friend was reading through the dossiers on Lanevus and Capim.

This fellow is in Backlund, and he's investigating the Lanevus and Capim cases? What the hell... The corners of Klein's mouth twitched slightly as he carefully thought of the clues he left behind in those two cases.

The only clue is that Detective Sherlock Moriarty is involved in both cases. If Leonard enters Daisy's dream, he should discover that. But I wore a beard and had a pretty good disguise back then. It's unlikely that he can recognize me from the rather blurry dream scene and portraits... As long as he doesn't recognize me, it doesn't matter. What do Sherlock Moriarty's problems have to do with me, Gehrman Sparrow? Klein reined in his thoughts and memorized Leonard Mitchell's current residence.

7 Pinster Street, Backlund.

He planned on entrusting The Moon Emlyn White to visit Leonard Mitchell the day after tomorrow and use the badge of the Hermits of Fate to purchase a mystical item.

I hope my dear poet friend has additional items to spare... If not, the price will definitely carry a premium... Klein answered tersely, then said to the radio transceiver, "It's your turn to ask."

He was honestly curious as to what Arrodes would ask this time.

Amidst the clacking sounds, the radio transceiver spewed out new illusory paper. On it was a question composed in Loenese: "Great Master, Leonard Mitchell has a huge secret. Do you wish to know?"

...This can also be considered a question? Amused and surprised, Klein raised his head and looked at the crimson moon silently illuminating the dark sea.

Soon, he honestly gave an answer.

"Yes."

Chapter 609: Paying a Visit

Amidst the clacking sounds, the radio transceiver spewed out a brand new piece of illusory white paper under the crimson moonlight in the dim environment.

"Inside Leonard Mitchell resides an angel from the Zoroast family. 'He' once changed my question."

Angel? There's an angel residing in Leonard's body? An angel from the Fourth Epoch's Zoroast family? Although Klein was mentally prepared to hear an incredible secret, he was still shocked by what Arrodes divulged.

Concern accompanied his astonishment.

An angel was a Sequence 1 or 2 Beyonder, an existence approaching the state of a true god. They possessed all manner of mystical characteristics and could even influence Low-Sequence Beyonders of the same pathway within a certain range. They were mighty figures who stood at the pinnacle of the real world. Within the various Churches, only popes, pontiffs, patriarchs, and certain legendary ascetics were Grounded Angels. Klein therefore believed that being parasitized by an existence of this level could hardly be a good thing.

In the dark Second Epoch, the angels all had their divine names, and they were subsidiaries to the ancient gods...

I haven't directly or indirectly interacted with many angels--Blasphemer Amon; Calamity Queen Cohinem; the evil spirit suspected of being the Red Angel Medici; Tail Devourer Ouroboros, as described by Little Sun; Miracle Invoker Zaratul from Roselle's diaries; the Creator's son, Adam; Hermes, who hasn't been confirmed to be an angel; and the Snake of Fate Will Auceptin, who's still inside his mother's womb...

Apart from the last two, the others appear extremely evil. Even the words they leave behind might drive a reader mad or make them lose control... Will my dear poet ultimately end up as a sacrifice to this angel parasite...

This can explain why he thinks of himself as the protagonist of a play, the special one, and why he was willing to help me hide my secret... The Zoroast family controls the Marauder pathway, which can steal the Beyonder powers of others. That's why Leonard was able to find and participate in the gathering of the Hermits of Fate... It's no wonder he offered to use Blood Vessel Thief--it's because he has a Grandpa to help him. He was able to instantly steal Megose's most potent power...

Heh, what Hermits of Fate? They're just a bunch of thieves and frauds. At best, the targets of their theft and fraud are time and fate...

In short, my dear poet's Grandpa isn't a good person. I have to find a chance to warn him... But therein lies the problem: they're always together. Any warning will only hasten the danger.

Klein's thoughts raced, but he ultimately found no workable solution. He could only set the matter aside for the moment and plan to ask Mr. Azik and the Snake of Mercury, Will Auceptin, whether they knew what to do.

During those few minutes, he even considered using Blasphemer Amon. Simply put, he would divulge to the Creator's son that an angel from the Zoroast family resided in Leonard Mitchell's body, prompting "Him" to "eat" the parasite.

According to what Klein knew, the Amon family supported the Tudor Empire during the Fourth Epoch, while the Zoroast family belonged to the Solomon Empire, making the two enemies. Furthermore, according to the Law of Beyonder Characteristics Conservation, powerhouses from the same pathway would have greater conflicts the higher their Sequence was. This was akin to the battle between the two Snakes of Mercury.

Unfortunately, reality constrained this solution, so Klein ultimately abandoned it. First, he didn't know where to find Blasphemer Amon. Second, he feared that a battle between angels would directly destroy Leonard Mitchell. Third, a powerful Amon might create an even worse problem.

I'll first have Emlyn attempt the transaction and observe the situation. I can decide what to do when I know more... Klein reined in his thoughts and said to the radio transceiver connected to Arrodes, "Ask your question."

The radio transceiver clacked with increasing briskness as it gently fed out illusory white paper.

"No, there's no need.

"This is an addendum to my question which doesn't need to abide by the rules.

"Great Master, I sense a special item outside your room, but I am unable to see through it. Can you tell me what it is?"

This magic mirror is impressive. It seems able to see everything. It only suffers interference when dealing with high-level matters, as though they're obscured by a mosaic... Klein calmly replied, "The Die of Probability."

Amid the tapping, Arrodes presented new words on the illusory white paper.

"So it's that thing... Great Master, you can ask your question."

Klein thought for a moment before saying, "What do you have to mention regarding the Die of Probability?"

At that moment, the radio transceiver seemed to brighten and no longer looked as gloomy as before. The speed at which it spewed out illusory white paper slowed.

"It's a fellow that's especially petty and bears grudges. Master, you have to quickly give it to someone else!

"It's an item formed from the Uniqueness of the Wheel of Fortune pathway. You can give it to any other Snake of Fate, and 'They' will be very grateful. In short, it isn't suitable to be your servant.

"The aura is dissipating. Your loyal and humble servant, Arrodes, has no choice but to leave. Finally, let me praise you once again, my Great Master, the ruler above the spirit world. Bye bye~"

Uniqueness... The Die of Probability is actually the Uniqueness of the Monster pathway... This is the first time I've encountered a so-called Uniqueness. Something capable of digitizing the entire real world truly is terrifying... The Monster pathway is also called the Wheel of Fortune pathway. Is Sequence 0 Wheel of Fortune? Klein looked at the radio transceiver, which had returned to normal, and temporarily reined in his thoughts.

He felt no greed toward the Die of Probability. It didn't belong to the Seer pathway and carried terrifying negative effects. He also feared that, given time, this vindictive die would draw Tail Devourer Ouroboros to him.

Even if this thing can be isolated and sealed when placed above the gray fog, there's a high chance of it digitizing the entire mysterious space. In the future, the Tarot Gatherings might end up becoming a tabletop role-playing game... Klein had never come into contact with items of this level before, so he was unable to determine what would happen if he were to throw the Die of Probability above the gray fog.

He decided to continue intimidating the die and safely deliver it to Oravi Island, where members of the Life School of Thought would have the means to seal it. He didn't mind that the mission's difficulty exceeded the payment he would receive, because the greatest benefit he would gain was the friendship of the Snake of Fate Will Auceptin.

Backlund, Harvest Church.

Emlyn White held a strange badge which was the size of an eyeball and chuckled inwardly.

The World certainly is long-winded. I'm only looking for a Nighthawk named Leonard Mitchell on Pinster Street in North Borough, aren't I? He even went out of his way to emphasize that the man has a secret and will probably pull me into a dream.

This is showing doubt in my abilities!

Emlyn stood up and changed into a tailcoat and white shirt. He then turned his head to look out the window.

Heh heh, he has a secret, while I have mine. I dare bet that Lord Nibbs or the other Earls are secretly watching me. If anything happens, they will definitely help me to some extent... Being pulled into a dream... Emlyn contemplated for a few seconds before using spiritually rich materials to concoct a blue medicine.

Putting away the bottle and the remaining ingredients, he picked up a silk top hat and walked out of the break room. After bidding farewell to Bishop Utravsky, he left the Harvest Church.

It was still morning, but Backlund's sky was gloomy. There was a faint fog that spread like water.

Emlyn narrowed his eyes, put on his hat, and murmured, "The sunlight is a little dazzling..."

He hailed a rental carriage and went straight for the steam metro station. He spent six pence on a first-class ticket to North Borough.

This saved him far more time than traveling there directly!

About forty minutes later, he arrived at his destination and stood outside the door of 7 Pinster Street.

Emlyn politely rang the doorbell and patiently waited for a minute.

Just as he was about to write a note and slip it through the gap beneath the door to arrange another time, he heard languid footsteps approach.

Doesn't sound like a servant... Emlyn gave an almost imperceptible nod, took out the medicine he had prepared, and drained it in one smooth motion.

Then, he watched the door open and saw a black-haired, green-eyed man appear before him. He was dressed in home wear--a white shirt and black trousers. His shirt was untucked, allowing it to flutter gently in the wind. Amidst his suaveness were hints of uninhibited freedom.

Although he's a believer of Evernight, I have to say that his looks are barely comparable to those of us Sanguine... Emlyn took off his hat and gently raised his chin.

"Good morning, are you Mr. Leonard Mitchell?"

Leonard frowned almost imperceptibly at the handsome, red-eyed gentleman. He raised a hand to cover his mouth and yawned indifferently.

"You are?"

"A visitor. I have something I would like to seek your help on." Emlyn didn't reveal his identity as he smiled with a rather obvious arrogance.

This attitude gave Leonard a baffling sense of familiarity. It was as though he had seen his former self--the person who always thought of himself as being the most special, the protagonist of this era.

He cleared his throat and said, "I'm only an ordinary citizen. I do not accept missions.

"If you have any issues, then you can find a private detective."

Emlyn White smiled and said, "This matter is something only you can complete."

He looked to his sides and continued, "I wish to purchase a mystical item that can steal the Beyonder powers of others."

Leonard's eyes focused as he asked in a deep voice, "Who are you?"

At that moment, Emlyn didn't answer immediately. Instead, he surveyed his surroundings and clicked his tongue with a smile.

"Impressive. I nearly failed to realize that I had been pulled into a dream."

He wasn't in a rush to leave as he took out the small badge he received from The World in front of Leonard Mitchell's solemn expression.

Leonard took a glance as his expression eased slightly. He cocked his head slightly and paused for a few seconds.

In silence, Emlyn White watched the scene around him fracture and vanish like broken glass.

Leonard sneered and pointed inside the house.

"Let's talk inside."

Chapter 610: Throwing the Die

At Leonard Mitchell's invitation, Emlyn showed no fear. Top hat in hand and a faint smile on his lips, he entered the house without hesitation.

He didn't remove his coat, for a Potions Professor often carried numerous supplementary items, and the sight beneath his carefully arranged concealment would be anything but elegant.

Emlyn sat with his starched tailcoat immaculate, leaned leisurely against the backrest, and said, "Actually, there's no need to go to such trouble.

"If you have it, name the price; otherwise, say so frankly."

"Of course, I'm very certain you have one."

He chuckled, his bright red eyes reflecting Leonard Mitchell's carelessly dressed figure.

Knowing that Leonard had a secret while Leonard knew nothing about him delighted Emlyn and filled him with an intense sense of superiority.

Leonard combed his fingers through his raven-black hair and casually sat opposite Emlyn. Showing neither panic nor confusion, he smiled. "At the very least, I need to know who wants it."

"Perhaps I do, or perhaps a friend does." Emlyn raised his chin and smiled faintly.

Leonard squinted his eyes as he cocked his head, as though in thought.

Finally, he laughed.

"Alright, since you've already taken out that badge, I'll answer you frankly.

"I do have a mystical item that can be used to steal the Beyonder powers of others, but I only have that one item.

"If you wish to buy it, 7,000 pounds. No negotiation."

7,000 pounds? A mystical item of this kind is that expensive? Although he didn't have to pay for it, Emlyn was still shocked. He nearly failed to maintain his bearing as a Sanguine.

He automatically converted that sum into the number of dolls and doll dresses it could buy.

After two seconds of thought, Emlyn revealed a smile and said, "I'll consider it. I'll answer you in two days."

"Sure." Leonard curled the corners of his lips.

After leaving 7 Pinster Street, Emlyn calmly hired a rental carriage to the North Borough steam metro station, then returned to the bridge in South Borough.

Removing his hat, Emlyn glanced back at the street, where carriages and pedestrians passed to and fro. He chuckled and stepped into the Harvest Church.

In between a tree and a black street lamp, an inconspicuous shadow suddenly moved, revealing the black-haired, green-eyed Leonard Mitchell.

His appearance was silent, but it didn't garner the attention of any pedestrians.

"Someone from the Church of Earth Mother?" Leonard frowned slightly as he muttered to himself.

He paused for two seconds before leaving Rose Street where the Harvest Church was situated.

7,000 pounds? Why doesn't he just go rob a bank? Above the gray fog, Klein, who had received the feedback from Emlyn, nearly blurted out.

As Gehrman Sparrow, he had previously said something similar, but the circumstances and his mood were completely different.

Using the bounty on the Golden Dream's third mate, Flowery Bow Tie Jodeson, as a reference, Klein estimated that a similar mystical item was worth about 5,000 pounds. Even with a premium, the price should have topped out at 6,000. Yet Leonard Mitchell had demanded 7,000 pounds outright!

Could it be an item left behind by a High-Sequence Beyonder, with stealing Beyonder powers as only one of its effects? No, if that were true, its price would start at 10,000 pounds... My dear poet, I never knew you were such a profiteer. You always seemed so carefree and indifferent to money... Klein couldn't help sighing.

Since he had a clue and a glimmer of hope, and the problem could be resolved with money, Klein didn't wish to seek out other channels and risk a mishap.

He quickly calculated his wealth and discovered that it was an acceptable price.

Including the 300-pound advance from the Apothecary, the 5,400-pound bounty for Wormtongue, and the cash I got from the bodies, then subtracting the 200 pounds to be paid to Miss Magician, I have a total of 12,767 pounds in cash and 5 gold coins. This doesn't include my 3 soli and 8 pence in change.

In addition, Miss Justice will pay the 2,000 pounds she owes me before the end of the week, as well as the 1,800 pounds for the Psychiatrist characteristic. I'm actually richer than I thought I was. Even in Backlund, I can be considered a tycoon.

Klein inhaled and didn't hesitate any further. He made the cash fly out from the junk pile and land on the table.

He carefully counted out 7,500 pounds in cash and set it aside--7,000 pounds for the mystical item and 500 pounds for Emlyn's "hazard pay."

Sigh, after all the effort it took to surpass ten thousand, more than half of it will be gone in an instant... Klein conjured The World in a praying posture to inform Emlyn that there was no problem with proceeding with the transaction. Fifteen minutes later, Emlyn was to hold a ritual to receive Mr. Fool's bestowment.

In addition, Klein had The World warn Emlyn not to rush to complete the deal after receiving the cash. He had to wait until tomorrow. Klein feared that the angel from the Zoroast family inside Leonard Mitchell would sense the gray fog's aura on the money. He wanted to let it "air out," just as he had when giving Emlyn the badge.

Fifteen minutes later, Klein glanced at the cash pile which had shrunk by more than half and sighed before returning to the real world.

It was already 10:40 on Friday morning. They were about eight hours from Oravi Island.

It's time to intimidate the Die of Probability again... Klein muttered, held the ritual, and brought out the iron cigar case which contained the All-Black Eye.

He guessed that Darkwill would suspect something, so he deliberately took the die into the washroom to deal with it.

Once the Die of Probability had calmed again, Klein quickly put away the All-Black Eye and iron cigar case. He breathed a long sigh of relief and silently calculated the time.

It should be enough to last until Darkwill hands it to the contact. There's no need to deal with it again.

With this in mind, Klein began to consider another problem with a certain degree of concern.

The All-Black Eye has appeared before the True Creator, and it bears "His" mental corruption. Taking it out every few hours might allow "Him" to lock onto me and send powerhouses to find me.

However, it was exposed for only a minute or two each time, so the problem shouldn't be too serious. Even if "He" senses it, "His" estimate of the area won't be very precise. Sigh, when the True Creator is involved, divination can't provide an answer. I can only remain vigilant. Thankfully, this is the last time, and only about eight hours remain before we reach our destination... Why am I raising flags for myself? Ptooey! Ptooey! Ptooey! I wasn't thinking anything!

Klein picked up the Die of Probability and returned to the living room, where he saw the chubby Apothecary slumped in the reclining chair, looking completely exhausted. The owl Harry, by contrast, brimmed with energy, as though it needed no rest.

Are these the different traits that different creatures gain from taking the same potion? At the very least, Miss Justice has never mentioned that she doesn't need sleep... Is this a unique trait arising from an owl's physiology? Sigh, I don't know much about owls. Indeed, I'm just a keyboard warrior and a jack of all trades... Klein sat on the sofa, placed the milky-white die in the ring box, and patiently waited for evening, when the ship was scheduled to dock.

Time ticked by as the sun gradually set in the west.

Klein, whose eyes had been closed, suddenly sat upright. He sensed an extreme danger descending upon the area!

It had exceeded a Seer's spiritual intuition and the Clown's intuition for danger. It seemed to stem from the formless gray fog which strangely rippled around him.

An enemy! Tail Devourer Ouroboros or the Aurora Order's Saint? Klein opened his eyes as he began considering countermeasures with an abnormally serious expression.

Any mistake at a time like this would force him to contemplate resurrection.

As for Darkwill and Harry, there was absolutely no way for them to be spared!

At first, he hoped the visitor could sense only a vague general area and might not find him or the Die of Probability. However, considering that he was aboard an isolated ship at sea, it wouldn't be too difficult to lock onto the target. Once that happened, the situation would only worsen, leaving surrender as the only choice.

Above the liner, space was suddenly torn open as an invisible door filled with complicated symbols appeared.

Two pale hands reached through the door and pulled sharply backward, allowing the man's entire body to emerge.

He wore a black bonnet and a traditional dark robe favored by the elderly, yet he appeared no older than forty. His slightly curled brown hair looked unnaturally stiff.

Countless illusory images flitted through his dark eyes, as though layer upon layer of chaotic worlds lay hidden within.

As Klein's sense of danger intensified, just the change in his expression frightened Darkwill and Harry enough for them to freeze.

Without hesitation, he followed the plans he had made for such situations. He leaned forward and picked up the Die of Probability.

"Determine the fate of the malefic visitor who just arrived. I want a one!" Klein commanded in a deep, soft voice as he threw the milky-white die. He instinctively prayed to the Goddess, hoping the recently intimidated die would remain obedient and honor the rule he had declared.

Meanwhile, he appeared extremely calm to prevent the die from sensing his anxiety and seizing the opportunity to cause trouble.

The Die of Probability rolled several times with a series of clinks, then settled with the blood-red one facing upward.

Above the liner, the black-robed man, who looked no older than forty, had the entire ship reflected in his eyes.

He emanated his spirituality and scanned the surroundings. Extending a hand, he grasped the space before him and suddenly pulled open a nearly invisible door.

The powerhouse stepped in and vanished from the spot.

Inside the first-class cabin, Klein immediately felt the danger distancing itself from him. He couldn't help but heave a sigh of relief.

He looked at the Die of Probability on the coffee table and couldn't help but sigh inwardly.

If this die didn't have such a negative side effect and could be utilized to its full extent, it would be a divine artifact!

As expected of a Grade 0 Sealed Artifact. As expected of the Uniqueness of the Monster pathway...

Only then did Darkwill recover enough courage to ask cautiously, "W-what just happened?"

Klein maintained his posture and calmly replied, "There's no need for you to know."

"I understand, I understand. The more I know, the greater the danger I'll be in." Darkwill wiped the cold sweat from his face.

There were no further accidents over the next few hours. After the sun sank below the horizon and the sky grew completely dark, Klein finally saw a towering lighthouse appear nearby.

Chapter 611: Fate Councilor

Oravi lay northeast of the Rorsted Archipelago. The straight-line distance wasn't great, but the safe sea route forced ships to make numerous detours, adding several hundred nautical miles to the journey.

It had originally been a primitive island, uninhabited by humans and home to many kinds of Beyonder creatures. After the hunting era ended, the Loen Kingdom exiled certain criminals there, and villages and towns gradually appeared across Oravi Island.

As more islands to the east were discovered and colonized, Oravi's convenient position along the sea route and wealth of natural resources attracted many new immigrants, turning it into a thriving port.

The lighthouse shone warmly through the darkness, guiding liners into the harbor to dock.

"We've finally arrived. My thanks to the crimson moon and fate for their blessings." Darkwill jumped from the last gangway and landed on solid concrete.

To be precise, apart from fate, you should thank The Fool and Sea God for their blessings... Klein held his leather suitcase with one hand as he tapped his cane.

Darkwill carefully put away the ring box containing the Die of Probability, then immediately asked where Carnot the bellman lived. He hired a carriage and went straight to St. Draco Cathedral, where he entered the magnificent bell tower with its bold red, blue, and yellow decoration.

Inside the bell tower, Carnot had a cramped room he could call his own.

Knock! Knock! Knock! Darkwill knocked on the door thrice, eager to hand over the item on his person.

With a creak, the brown wooden door opened, and a large, slightly hunched man emerged.

He appeared to be in his forties. None of his facial features looked strange on its own, yet together they formed a deeply discordant whole.

One glance was enough for Klein to notice that his eyes weren't level and his nostrils differed in size. The muscles on the left side of his face sagged slightly, pulling down the corner of his mouth, while the right side was the exact opposite.

Carnot's legs were plainly different lengths, and one arm was thick while the other was thin. His entire body was badly disproportionate, making him extremely ugly.

"Who are you?" Dressed in a black robe, Carnot glanced at the duo.

"Are you the bellman, Carnot?" Darkwill asked cautiously.

Carnot chuckled, one corner of his mouth rising while the other remained low.

"There shouldn't be a second person as ugly as me."

"Indeed." Darkwill nodded with complete honesty, then laughed. "I can tell you have a healthy frame of mind. To be honest, a man's looks don't matter. What matters is his skill in bed."

Tsk, that's because you've never met women obsessed with handsome faces... Having lived through the age of mass celebrity, Klein scoffed inwardly.

Carnot's expression darkened as he said, "I don't wish to discuss such matters."

"You have problems in that area? No problem. I have all kinds of medicine that can cure them..." Before Darkwill could finish, Klein stepped forward, afraid he would be beaten to death.

He put half his body between them as he said in a deep voice, "He's Roy King's student."

"I figured. Roy King once described his traits." Carnot made way and invited the two in.

His room was extremely tiny, with only a single bed and a cabinet that doubled as a dining table. The washroom was at the bottom of the bell tower.

Darkwill pulled out a ring box and handed it to Carnot as he forced a smile.

"My teacher got me to hand this item to you."

Carnot glanced at it and was visibly relieved to see four pips. He said to Darkwill, "You aren't as unreliable as your teacher made you sound. I can tell you didn't try to use it. That would've awakened it and made it realize it was no longer sealed."

The chubby Apothecary flushed and admitted honestly, "That's only because it's relatively quiet right now. In another hour or two, it'll roll itself automatically. You'd better find a way to seal it again."

Carnot's face twitched.

"Again?"

"W-well, I accidentally dropped it on the ground, and then i-it came alive..." Darkwill wanted to glance at his owl, Harry, but realized it hadn't followed him in. It was perched outside the bell tower, serving as a sentry.

Carnot's eyes clearly widened as his hunched back nearly straightened.

"How did you arrive here?"

As far as he was concerned, Roy King's student should have died at the die's whims long ago.

Darkwill hurriedly pointed to Gehrman Sparrow and said, "It's all thanks to this gentleman. He's a powerful adventurer. He provided the most effective protection and a temporary method of sealing the die."

"What method?" Carnot blurted out.

Klein laughed and replied, "Trade secret."

Carnot's expression changed a few times as he pressed his hand to his chest and bowed.

"On behalf of my organization, I thank you for your help."

Darkwill continued, "Hiring him cost 1,000 pounds and the fulfillment of a request. He wants help finding a mystical item with powerful offensive abilities but no severe negative effects. He'll buy it at a reasonable price.

"Uh, I only had about 300 pounds, so that was all I could pay. I'll have to rely on you to pay the rest..."

Carnot fell silent as the corners of his lips twitched.

"I only have about 100 pounds..."

The bellman's room fell silent. Wind slipped through the cracks and brushed the three men's faces.

Finally, Carnot spoke again.

"Perhaps Councilor Ricciardo has the money. I'll lead you to him."

From the looks of it, he trusts us. After all, a Fate Councilor is in no way comparable to a Die of Probability. If there were really problems with us, there would be no need for us to bring the die over to prevent any accidents and snatch away the Uniqueness... Klein watched as Carnot picked up a lantern from the corner of the room.

At that moment, he secretly activated his Spirit Vision and swept his gaze over the man.

Klein nearly raised his eyebrows. Carnot's aura colors were highly unusual.

His emotional colors were perfectly ordinary, but the Ether Body representing his physical form appeared unnatural. His heart was green, his head purple, and his digestive system yellow. They resembled disparate parts mashed together in profound disharmony.

Carnot's soul gave him the same impression.

A monster stitched together internally? Klein made a rough judgment based on what he knew.

After Carnot got the lantern, he turned his head to glance at the adventurer, Gehrman Sparrow, and said without much of an expression, "I'm very sensitive to Spirit Vision.

"I believe you've noticed certain problems. I wasn't born naturally. I'm a product of the Church of Earth Mother's attempts to refine the human body. I'm considered a failed specimen, which is why I lack ability in that regard."

His final sentence was directed at Darkwill.

Carnot didn't speak again as he carried the lantern and donned a black robe. He hunched his huge body and used the dim light to walk down the stairs of the dark and silent bell tower.

A product of the Church of Earth Mother's attempts to refine the human body? But St. Draco Cathedral belongs to the Church of Storms... Since he's a failed specimen, they paid him no further attention. Did Carnot turn his back on the Church of Earth Mother and join the Life School of Thought? That would be understandable. Both the Church of Earth Mother and the Life School of Thought possess the Apothecary pathway, so their interests must overlap... But why would the Life School of Thought send this fellow, who seems far from simple, to serve as a bellman in the Church of Storms? Is it merely an ordinary disguise, or is there a particular connection between the two? Seemingly calm, Klein followed Carnot out of the bell tower and around to a stone bridge.

Just as he came close to the left side of the bridge, Klein suddenly felt something as he looked diagonally upwards.

The owl, Harry, had flown out as well and landed on a tree branch.

Kacha!

The branch suddenly snapped, sending Harry plummeting. The owl barely managed to take flight again before hitting the ground.

Darkwill was just about to say something when Carnot explained in a deep voice, "Councilor Ricciardo is injured, so his powers are somewhat out of control. Once anyone enters a specific range, they will be tainted with bad luck."

Carnot stopped and threw the ring box containing the die under the bridge.

"Mr. Councilor, Roy King's student has delivered the die."

"Very good." A hoarse voice sounded from deep within the bridge's belly.

A silver-scaled palm reached from the darkness and pinned the ring box where it had fallen in the mud.

He opened the ring box and removed the milky-white die.

His other silver-scaled hand emerged holding a paper-thin object made of leather.

The object resembled a water pipe whose ends had been joined. It appeared grayish-white beneath the crimson moonlight. Its surface bore many intricate symbols and markings, along with numerous words in Hermes that pointed to the Snake of Mercury, Will Auceptin.

Fate Councilor Ricciardo pulled the item apart and stuffed the Die of Probability into it before connecting the two ends together.

Using a small-scale cycle of fate to seal the Die of Probability, keeping it perpetually dormant or calm? The Snake of Mercury, Will Auceptin, truly foresaw this situation and made preparations... Klein withdrew his gaze in relief as Ricciardo's hoarse voice rang out.

"I already know of your agreement, Mr. Gehrman Sparrow.

"I will help you find the mystical item after I've recovered.

"As for the 700 pounds... I'll immediately augment you with good luck. Go to the few casinos in Oravi and make some bets and you'll receive the corresponding reward. Remember, do not win more than 200 pounds at each casino. You must not win more than 700 pounds in total."

Which is to say that you don't have the money either... Seeing how you're hiding while injured, it's considered normal not to have money with you... Klein nodded indiscernibly and said, "Okay."

He waited for about ten seconds without sensing anything. Councilor Ricciardo heaved a sigh of relief and said, "You have become sufficiently lucky."

Is that so? Klein chose to believe him and didn't interrupt Darkwill as he reported his teacher's imprisonment to a senior member of the organization. Following Carnot's directions, he found a nearby casino and sat at a blackjack table.

After about ten minutes, he walked out of the casino with a blank look.

Not only had he failed to win, but he had lost a total of 30 pounds!

What happened to the good luck? Why did I believe him? That darn old man is terrible... Klein didn't hesitate to return to the stone bridge and approached its belly.

"So soon? You have pretty good luck." Ricciardo laughed and coughed beneath the dark bridge.

By then, Darkwill and Harry had already found rooms at a nearby inn.

Klein replied without a change in expression, "I lost."

"Lost..." Ricciardo pondered the word, unable to formulate a sentence for a long time.

In the darkness, an ordinary owl flew over nervously.

Chapter 612: In Hand

The two streetlamps flanking the stone bridge silently illuminated the road but couldn't dispel the darkness beneath it. Only the crimson moon high above shed a faint light on the brown earth.

Hidden beneath the bridge, Fate Councilor Ricciardo remained silent for so long that Klein wondered if his severe injuries had killed him. He suspected they were largely responsible for the failed luck augmentation.

Just as Klein was about to ask a probing question, Ricciardo coughed and said in a deep, hoarse voice, "You live up to your reputation as a powerful adventurer capable of temporarily sealing the die...

"What happened exceeded my expectations. Ahem, I'm very curious why my luck augmentation failed on you.

"I regret that I can't come out to meet you in person. Otherwise, I should be able to see what Beyonders of other pathways would struggle to perceive, even with relatively powerful Spirit Vision.

"That is the Fate pathway's unique advantage."

So the problem lies with me? That's not the point. The point is that you need to reimburse me 700 pounds--No, 730 pounds... Klein cursed as he couldn't help but recall the orphan, Ademisaul, who had the nickname of Monster in Tingen City.

The young man who was equal to half a Sequence 9 had cried out after seeing Klein before falling to the ground. His eyes bled, and the fear he exhibited was as if he had seen a devil.

Because of the Wheel of Fortune pathway's unique divinity, he saw that something was wrong with me. Did he see something related to the gray fog? Unfortunately, this Fate Councilor is badly injured and surrounded by a domain of misfortune. There's no way for him to examine me... I should ask the Snake of Fate, Will Auceptin, when I return. Perhaps he knows something. However, this pathway does have the air of a charlatan. He might not answer me directly and will likely respond in vague terms... Klein remained expressionless and asked, "Then what do you plan to do?"

Ricciardo sighed and said, "Get Darkwill here. I'll augment him with good luck and get him to win the money at the casinos on your behalf."

Using the name of the inn Councilor Ricciardo gave, Klein quickly found the chubby Apothecary, Darkwill. Then, while carrying the suitcase and cane, Klein watched as he kept winning money at the major casinos. By midnight, he had already won 750 pounds.

After receiving the remaining 730 pounds, Klein realized a problem.

The luck augmentation had failed because of him!

Why? Klein clutched his bulging wallet and frowned in thought.

He soon thought of the thin, invisible grayish-white fog surrounding him and realized the reason.

After advancing to Faceless, the gray fog has integrated with reality to a certain extent. Not only can it shield me from ravings that aren't too powerful, but it can also negate influences that alter my fate to a certain degree? Is that why this councilor's luck augmentation was ineffective?

According to this logic, bad luck to a certain extent shouldn't affect me either.

Heh, what the heck? It keeps me from becoming some unlucky fool, but it also destroys my chances of becoming a lucky winner... Klein secretly shook his head and followed Darkwill and the owl back to the stone bridge. He told Ricciardo how to summon his messenger and asked him to send word immediately if he learned of a mystical item with powerful offensive abilities.

Afterward, Klein found an inn in Oravi's port city and rented a simple room.

Backlund, morning.

Beneath his beloved gloomy sky, Emlyn White walked through fog that, though thick, couldn't compare to that of November or December. Once again, he arrived outside 7 Pinster Street in the North Borough.

After pulling the doorbell, he straightened his back as he lifted his chin and waited patiently.

When Leonard opened the door, he looked at the handsome but detestable face before making way and allowing the visitor to enter.

He was still dressed in a white shirt and black trousers, but now wore a dark vest over them.

"Have you considered the matter?" Leonard asked with a smile.

"No problem." Since it wasn't his money, Emlyn had no intention of haggling.

He even suspected that Leonard would probably raise the price if he tried.

Leonard gently nodded and smiled.

"You're very rich."

I can tell that you're trying to lead me to say more... Emlyn silently scoffed and replied, "That's not something you need to concern yourself with."

With that said, he raised the suitcase in his hand and opened it, revealing 7,000 pounds in cash.

Leonard removed the stacks of cash one by one and inspected them carefully.

Then, he pulled off the red glove he wore on his left hand and handed it to Emlyn.

"This is the mystical item you require. It can change colors to disguise itself.

"It's called Tinder. It enhances the wearer's charm, lending his words great persuasive power. It can steal a Beyonder power from a target within 50 meters. The better you understand the target, the easier it is to steal the power you want. The less you know, the more random the result, leaving it to luck.

"When the target is at Sequence 6, there is a chance of failure. The higher the Sequence, the higher the chance of failure.

"Once the theft succeeds, the target loses that Beyonder power for at least twelve hours, while Tinder's owner can use it proficiently for ten minutes.

"It's expensive because it has very few negative effects."

Emlyn, who silently took in the introduction, had his interest piqued by a mystical item like Tinder. He asked, "What are its negative side effects?"

Leonard ran a hand through his hair and smiled.

"First, when wearing it, there is a small probability of losing something on you. Second, when using it, a particular Beyonder power belonging to you will be lost for at least twelve hours, but during this period, it will be the only Beyonder power lost. In other words, even if it's used repeatedly, there will only be one power lost the entire time."

"Compared to the many Sealed Artifacts I know of, its negative side effects are indeed trivial." Emlyn cleared his throat as he nodded.

He received Tinder and checked it carefully before taking out the stacks of cash from the suitcase and placing them on the coffee table.

He then placed the glove in the suitcase rather than carrying it openly.

With the suitcase, Emlyn returned to the Harvest Church just like he did before. Once he entered the priest's break room, he set up a Sacrificial ritual.

The illusory and mysterious door opened as the red glove tore through the dark and deep void as it was sent directly above the gray fog.

At the moment the gray fog made contact with the glove, a tiny, distorted, terrifying face suddenly appeared.

It kept evaporating as it struggled to sustain itself.

Klein wasn't surprised by this scene. After all, Grandpa, who resided in Leonard's body, was an angel of the Marauder pathway. If Amon could do it, "He" was almost certainly capable of doing the same.

Klein had made ample preparations for this. He believed that even if Grandpa had truly reached Amon's level, he could erase the corresponding aura without allowing him to sense the gray fog or prompting him to take action against Emlyn.

Picking up the Sea God Scepter, which had long been placed before him, Klein raised it commandingly and made all the blue "gems" light up at once.

Suddenly, silver light bloomed above the gray fog. Lightning imbued with some of the gray fog's power descended like a storm, striking the tiny illusory face.

The face dissipated completely before it could even cry out, leaving behind a dead translucent worm with twelve transparent rings.

Worm of Time... but it seems weaker than Amon's... Klein muttered as he beckoned, making the red glove and peculiar worm fly toward him.

After some serious scrutiny, he confirmed that the worm was basically identical to the one Amon's avatar had left behind.

My dear poet's Grandpa is at the same Sequence as Amon? Sequence 1? No, not necessarily. Perhaps this is a unique trait of one of the preceding Sequences...

If--if it's really a Sequence 1, the notion that Amon is the son of the Creator and a King of Angels makes it possible that he has fused with the Uniqueness or has another Sequence 1 characteristic... Klein made a guess, but he was unable to verify it.

Lacking the relevant knowledge, he could only toss it into the junk pile, uncertain what the dead Worm of Time could be used for or when its spirituality would completely dissipate.

7 Pinster Street, Backlund.

Leonard Mitchell sat on the sofa with his eyes tightly closed and his legs propped on the coffee table, as though trying to make up for an entire sleepless night with a brief nap.

After an unknown length of time, he suddenly opened his eyes and asked in a low voice, "What happened? Have you determined the true buyer of Tinder?"

An elderly voice rang out in his mind.

"The aura I left behind has been eliminated, completely eliminated."

"Did you discover anything?" Leonard retracted his feet as he asked in a deep voice.

The ancient voice sighed.

"Nothing.

"Everything was over before I could react.

"If I were in my best condition, I might've grasped the relevant clues. Unfortunately, I've remained weak for years."

Leonard remained silent for more than ten seconds before he shook his head.

"Then we'll temporarily treat it as if this never happened."

Above the gray fog, Klein listened to Emlyn's report and took note of the information regarding the mystical item. He fiddled with the glove named Tinder as he examined it.

"Heh heh, this way, I'll have a glove for my right hand," he chuckled to himself.

After careful consideration, Klein abandoned the idea of immediately extracting the True Creator's mental corruption from the All-Black Eye. To maximize his chances of success, he decided to save the opportunity for when he concocted the Marionettist potion. According to the City of Silver's records, such a procedure posed no problems even without the gray fog's protection. With the gray fog, there should be no additional accidents, even when dealing with mental corruption left behind by the True Creator.

I can also use Tinder normally. Yes, when I need it, I'll have to leave my cash, pocket watch, and other mystical items above the gray fog. I mustn't let anything get lost... In fact, even if I don't, it won't be a serious problem. I'm a Seer. Do I need to fear being unable to find something I've lost? Heh heh. In an ordinary battle, I'll have Creeping Hunger on my left hand and Tinder on my right. The thought alone is formidable...

Klein reined in his thoughts and discovered that everything related to the Sequence 5 Marionettist potion was ready, apart from the two commonly seen supplementary ingredients.

He nodded indiscernibly and muttered silently to himself, I just need to wait for the Faceless potion to be completely digested!

Then I'll board a ship belonging to the Admiral of Stars or The Hanged Man and head east of the Sonia Sea to find mermaids.

Chapter 613: Leonard's Investigation

Loen Kingdom, East Chester County, Stoen City.

Having left the manor, Audrey moved into a villa. After she finished engaging in social gatherings with the local nobles, she sent her servant to the Varvat Bank to withdraw a sum of cash.

She didn't need to worry about this any further. She could easily pay off the 2,000 pounds she owed Mr. Fool's Blessed, and she could also pay the 1,800 pounds she owed Mr. World for the Psychiatrist characteristic.

Fifteen minutes later, Audrey opened her bedroom door and glanced at her personal maid, Annie, who was supervising the other servants. She looked down at the golden retriever sitting beside the wall and smiled. Lowering her voice, she asked with shining eyes, "Susie, you'll receive a gift soon. Are you looking forward to it?"

If it were in the past, Audrey would've definitely said, "Susie, your present is here," making the golden retriever realize that she could find the actual item in the room as well. Otherwise, it was very easy for Susie, who had been studying the basics of mysticism, to guess that Audrey had used ritualistic magic.

With this new phrasing, Audrey could later claim to have received a mysterious letter or news in her bedroom confirming that the gift was about to be delivered. That left far too many possibilities for Susie to guess the truth.

Susie could perceive the joy and sincerity deep in Audrey's heart. She instinctively opened her mouth, hoping to stir the air and produce a voice. She wanted to ask about the gift, but keenly sensed Audrey's personal maid, Annie, approaching. The alert Susie abandoned the idea.

She returned to the state of being an ordinary dog as she slowly wagged her tail to express her joy and anticipation.

After making an excuse to head out, Audrey entered a "chemistry laboratory" that she had specially marked out for herself. She placed the Psychiatrist characteristic and supplementary ingredients on the table.

"Susie, do you still remember the potion-concocting process?" She cleared her throat and straightened her back as she excitedly played the role of a teacher.

"Woof, I remember!" Susie already knew what her present was. In her joy, she barked.

Audrey added, "Try to concoct it by yourself."

Susie looked down at her paws and suddenly fell silent.

Audrey was taken aback before a brief silence ensued.

A few seconds later, before the golden retriever could speak, Audrey covered her mouth as naturally as possible and giggled.

"Alright, Susie, there's no need to say anything. I know what you mean. You're only a dog and can't concoct the potion, right?"

How embarrassing... At the same time, Audrey, who wore a charming and graceful external bearing, facepalmed herself inwardly.

"Woof!" Susie nodded forcefully.

Audrey took the opportunity and turned around. Soon, she finished concocting the Psychiatrist potion.

She had previously asked Susie and learned that she had already finished digesting the potion on Wednesday.

That's less than two months... Yes, a large part of the reason is that no one pays attention to Susie. She can roam anywhere in the manor or villa and eavesdrop, allowing her to discern the maids' true thoughts... That's good as well. She always shares those tidbits with me. If not for her, I wouldn't have learned about the dark sides of many people who usually appear normal and kind... Audrey poured the potion into a bowl and placed it on the floor.

She watched Susie approach and begin lapping up the potion, unable to curb the anticipation in her heart.

Susie might be influenced by the potion and become unstable.

But it's alright. Psychiatrist Miss Audrey is already prepared to use Placate at any moment! Yes, I prefer the name Psychoanalysis. That sounds more professional.

Audrey stared intently at Susie with her beautiful emerald-like eyes and saw the golden retriever's pupils gradually fade and become vertical. Dark gold scales seemed to grow beneath her thick fur, and Susie's spirituality radiated outward as though interweaving with the space throughout the villa.

After calming her nervous emotions, Audrey scrutinized Susie's condition. If anything abnormal happened to Susie, she would immediately use the Beyonder power Psychoanalysis.

Suddenly, Susie's voice sounded in her ears.

"Audrey, I'm done!"

Audrey was momentarily at a loss for words.

In her dream, Daisy returned to East Borough and to the old apartment she had lived in for years.

She pushed open the door and saw her mother, Liz, and her sister, Freja, doing the laundry diligently.

Daisy instantly became delighted and was just about to join them. She was in charge of ironing the laundry.

At this moment, she heard knocking at the door.

She turned her head and realized that the visitor was a young man in a black-and-white checkered police uniform.

The officer had black hair and green eyes, with a somewhat blurry face. He held a notebook and fountain pen as he asked, "In the Capim case, apart from what you mentioned, is there anything else you didn't mention to us?"

"Nothing important," Daisy answered groggily.

The handsome officer looked down at his notebook and said, "It's fine. I'm willing to listen."

Daisy looked back at the clothes hanging to dry, feeling as though she had forgotten some instructions.

She honestly described all sorts of trivialities. In the end, she said, "...After I was abducted, my mother and sister had hired a private detective to search for me. His name is Mr. Sherlock Moriarty. He's a good man. Although he didn't find me directly, he later contacted a reporter to help me get compensated with the foundation money..."

The black-haired and green-eyed officer looked up again and glanced at Daisy before revealing a warm smile.

"Very good. Your answer is very satisfactory.

"Do you still remember the private detective's appearance?"

Daisy nodded. Without any alarm, she saw Mr. Sherlock Moriarty standing beside her.

The detective had a thick beard and wore gold-rimmed glasses. He looked almost identical to the man in her memories.

After the black-haired, green-eyed officer sized up the image several times, he vanished without Daisy noticing. For some reason, her mother and sister had also disappeared.

She ran through East Borough in search of the familiar figures, but eventually awoke from the sorrowful dream. Seeing the dark ceiling of her school dormitory, she lay there in a daze for several seconds.

Daisy didn't make a sound as she turned her body and buried half her face into the pillow.

At the edge of the pillow, a wet stain gradually spread.
