That can be done? This circus master is quite smart... For the first time, Fors realized that a novel could "advertise" just like in newspapers or magazines. Furthermore, the format was more obscure and natural.

Amid the waves, a liner with rows of cannons traveled along a safe sea route without daring to stray far from it.

Any deviation from the sea route east of Oravi Island usually meant disappearing. Even pirates didn't dare to stray too far from the safe zones.

This sea was full of unexplored regions and all manner of sensational legends!

After two months of volunteer work and four opportunities to engage in true acting, Klein bade farewell to carrying patients, scrubbing toilets, cleaning up vomit, and other miscellaneous chores. He boarded a ship headed for the Gargas Archipelago.

At the Tarot Gathering in early March, he had hired The Hermit Cattleya in a private conversation. He would meet her in Nas, the City of White and capital of the Gargas Archipelago. He would then board her ship and head for the dangerous, almost illusory waters at the far eastern edge of the Sonia Sea. There, he would search for unaffiliated mermaids.

The Hermit Cattleya seemed very interested in meeting members of the Tarot Club in the real world. After only a few seconds of thought, she agreed to The World's request. However, because of the great danger involved, she had named a high price.

3,000 pounds!

Klein's first reaction was to give up and take Mr. Hanged Man's ghost ship, but that would mean traveling with many sailors from the Church of Storms, limiting his freedom. Considering this and the extreme danger in the easternmost area of the Sonia Sea, he ultimately accepted Ma'am Hermit's terms. She would wait near the Gargas Archipelago for a month beginning in early April. Any delay would mean the end of their cooperation.

To avoid wasting the thousand-pound deposit, Klein didn't wait to complete his digestion before heading from Oravi Island to the Gargas Archipelago.

Of course, with the summaries and chances at true acting from before, he was already very close to completely digesting the Faceless potion. Even if he didn't use true acting, just acting in his capacity as Gehrman Sparrow was enough to complete the digestion in two to three weeks.

For this reason, and because The World's identity was tied to Gehrman Sparrow, Klein transformed back into the powerful, lunatic adventurer once he left the hospice. However, he did disguise himself to a certain degree.

As he watched the perturbed sea surface, Klein finally saw a port city that had houses mainly made of white rock.

The Feysac Empire's easternmost outpost and the capital of the Gargas Archipelago--Nas!

I'm finally overseas... Klein watched a rugged fishing boat carrying whale meat cruise into the harbor.

Meanwhile, he discovered several ships hanging pirate flags docked at the harbor without any signs of concealment.

Indeed, the waters east of Oravi Island are a playground for pirates... Klein put on his hat, picked up his luggage, and left the cabin after the liner came to a halt. He descended the gangway into the harbor.

After a few steps, he saw a pirate crew apparently come into conflict with a local gang. Both sides drew their weapons and clashed.

Klein calmly walked past, showing no signs of stopping them.

At that moment, he saw a local pull several cans from his pocket, open their lids, and throw them into the middle of the road.

What's the point of that? Klein nearly laughed aloud before recalling an infamous item found at sea.

Canned wolf-fish!

Canned wolf-fish was popular on the east coast of Feysac and the Gargas Archipelago!

The moment the thought surfaced, an indescribable stench assailed Klein's nose.

His facial muscles twitched as he fought his body's discomfort and quickly left the area.

A few pirates struck by the stench vomited immediately, while the rest frantically fled, dragging their companions as though they had lost all ability to fight.

A minute later, in a secluded corner, the crazy adventurer, Gehrman Sparrow, crouched down and silently belched.

Chapter 635: Meeting

The smell of canned wolf-fish is truly overpowering... Not only does it stink, but it's also nauseating... It's practically a biological weapon! Crouched in the corner, Klein took nearly a minute to recover.

Until that moment, he had underestimated canned wolf-fish and failed to take the necessary precautions. He could've used a Paper Figurine Substitute or created an invisible air tube to imitate Underwater Breathing, but he had done neither.

Now he finally understood the wisdom of banning canned wolf-fish from being opened in public!

Phew... Klein exhaled as he slowly stood up. Carrying his suitcase, he walked toward the harbor.

His first impression of Nas was of its many white houses, with stone being the most common building material. His second was that, although the city wasn't too far north, it was rather cold. Even though it was already April, the temperature was still only a few degrees Celsius. His third was of the many whaling facilities. Gigantic beluga whales were being butchered for their skin, flesh, fat, bones, and ambergris.

The latter two could be used, respectively, to make panniers for formal dresses and top-grade fragrances. Whether used in incense or perfume, these were luxury goods that only the wealthy and nobles could enjoy.

The beluga whales' skin, flesh, and fat also had their own uses. They were used to make clothing, food, and oil-based products. In Nas and the Gargas Archipelago, methods of preparing beluga whale had developed into a unique culinary culture, with all sorts of techniques and famous restaurants.

Klein passed the various whaling facilities and saw rendered fat being carried in freight wagons toward nearby factories that spewed black smoke. These were oil refineries characteristic of Gargas. Whale fat could be refined into oil, bottled for use as an excellent incendiary, and used as the lifeblood of certain industries.

Very unique... Klein exhaled some mist as he stopped and watched for a while.

Upon leaving the harbor and entering the city, a cacophony of spoken Feysac inundated his ears.

Having mastered Ancient Feysac--the source of the Northern Continent's languages--Klein had long been familiar with the tongue of the Northern Continent's barbarians. He turned toward the din and saw tall, slightly blond locals marching through the streets with banners held aloft.

The banner at the very front clearly stated the reason for their protest.

"Oppose the poaching of beluga whales! We need sustainable development!"

Pfft... Klein nearly lost his cool as he believed that the concept of "sustainable development" was highly likely to be "invented" by Emperor Roselle.

As he swept his gaze, he looked at the banners behind it and understood the goals of the protest.

"Whaling for survival, not entertainment!"

"Humans aren't more important than beluga whales!"

"Greedy devils should leave Nas!"

At that moment, policemen dressed in gray uniforms held shields, riot forks, and batons to stop the protesters from advancing.

After a brief argument, the scene quickly turned violent.

Many young protesters threw open cans of wolf-fish and Molotov cocktails. The police showed no restraint as they charged forward, raising their shields and striking with their batons.

Klein pinched his nose as he watched fires burn in the street. He realized that many passersby were completely unfazed. Apart from a small group of spectators, the rest continued about their business.

It appears such things happen often in Nas... Do protests regularly develop into riots? As expected of the Feysac Empire... Klein muttered to himself, detoured around the street, and casually found an inn.

He continued to register as Gehrman Sparrow, unconcerned that the news released by Vice Admiral Ailment Tracy would lead the Church of the Evernight Goddess to identify him as a Faceless. He had no intention of traveling along the whaling routes to find mermaids who believed in the Goddess. Instead, he planned to head to the dangerous region at the easternmost edge of the Sonia Sea.

As for the safety in the Gargas Archipelago, there was nothing he needed to worry about. This was a colony of the Feysac Empire. The only legal Church was the Church of the God of Combat. They were enemies with the Church of the Evernight Goddess.

Klein had originally worried that he would encounter many Faceless here, perhaps meeting seven or eight simply by stepping next door to enjoy some whale cuisine. However, after careful consideration, he rationally dismissed this possibility.

First, Beyonders of the Seer pathway were rare to begin with. Klein had met only three who were above Sequence 8. Second, Sequence 6 Beyonders weren't commonly seen. Even in a playground for pirates, people with bounties of 5,000 pounds were considered rare. Third, once a Faceless had made sufficient preparations, they would board a whaling ship in search of mermaids. They would then either begin believing in the Goddess, sink to the bottom of the ocean, or become research subjects. The exceptionally clever ones would seize the opportunity to advance successfully and leave safely. It was very rare for them to remain in the Gargas Archipelago for long.

In all of Nas, excluding myself, there can't be more than two Faceless... Klein straightened his clothes. In no hurry to contact Admiral of Stars Cattleya, he took to the streets in a good mood and began searching for delicacies he had heard about during his travels.

Raw slices of beluga whale, fried whale steak, whale skin with oil, roasted whale meat... Like an ordinary traveler, Klein visited three restaurants and sampled a variety of dishes.

Not bad. It's quite distinctive and not very fishy. Instead, it's appetizing and delicious... Burp... Klein covered his mouth as he emerged onto the street. He noticed that the street lamps were sparse, but the houses lining the road were brightly lit, reducing the darkness to some extent.

Icy winds blew in from the ocean, prompting Klein to raise his collar. The blue cufflinks were fastened securely at his wrists, their luster subdued.

Compared to rings, cufflinks were better suited to Gehrman Sparrow's persona. Therefore, Klein didn't blame the Artisan for making changes without his permission.

As for the harmonica into which the summoning ritual for a spirit world creature had been inscribed, it perfectly matched Klein's private wishes. It could be used for a year and a half and was exquisitely crafted from silver.

When he received the harmonica, Klein had imagined this scene--a crazy and powerful adventurer playing a mournful tune beside a boat on a silent night beneath the dim moonlight.

Unfortunately, the harmonica couldn't produce any sound, and it could only be used to summon Reinette Tinekerr.

Shaking his head almost imperceptibly, Klein walked steadily through Nas's empty, cold streets before returning to his inn.

After a night's sleep restored him to his optimal state, he went to Gray Amber Street the next morning and entered a general store named Hot Whale Dance.

Upon seeing the grizzled boss who was a head taller than him, Klein tapped the counter and said in Feysac, "Whale oil."

The boss's face was deeply lined, but he wore only a coat made of beluga whale skin. Its pale patterns possessed a strange beauty.

"How much?" The boss gulped liquor, ignoring the disarray of the goods around him.

"One and a quarter buckets," Klein replied according to the predetermined secret password.

The boss's movements immediately slowed as he placed his cup of brownish-green liquor on the counter.

"Do you want to try some? It's many times purer than Nepos. It's considered the mistress of all Feysac men."

This distilled liquor was a Feysac specialty brewed from potatoes or grain. Its alcohol content was high, and it rivaled Blaze in both potency and fame. Compared with Sonia blood wine, it was inexpensive and popular among ordinary Feysacians.

"There's no need." Klein shook his head.

The boss chuckled.

"What kind of man are you if you don't drink Nepos?

"Are there only women in Loen?"

He mumbled before drinking another mouthful.

"Who introduced you here?"

"Ma'am Gehrmuses," Klein said, giving a name that followed local conventions.

The boss exhaled, causing a strong scent of distilled liquor to linger in the air.

He staggered as he got up, like a polar bear performing at a circus.

After giving the store employee instructions, he brought Klein to a tiny room on the second story of the warehouse at the back.

"Let me search for it. Let's see..." the boss mumbled as he crouched.

Klein controlled the twitching of his facial muscles as he recalled a widely circulated joke in the Loen Kingdom.

"When is a Feysac man not drunk? When he's in his mother's womb."

After waiting for a moment, Klein saw the boss find a pure crystal ball from his rummaging.

Then, the tipsy "polar bear" turned his back to Klein, rubbed his hands over the crystal ball, and softly chanted in tongue-twisting ancient Hermes.

The room gradually darkened, and every unlit corner seemed to sink inward as though exerting a strange pull.

The crystal ball quickly lit up, producing the figure of a woman wearing a black, classic robe.

She had an oval face and very fair skin. Her deep-black eyes held a faint purple hue and an air of mystery.

I'm seeing another Tarot Club member's actual appearance again... Klein stepped forward and received the crystal ball.

On the opposite end of the crystal ball, The Hermit Cattleya could also clearly see The World. He had black hair and brown eyes, with a thin and angular face.

Her gaze paused for a moment before she hesitantly said, "Gehrman Sparrow?"

She discovered that the Tarot Club members' true strength and caliber far exceeded her expectations. The World was none other than the mad hunter Gehrman Sparrow, whose strength was considered comparable to that of a pirate admiral!

My various assumptions of The World were problematic...

Controlled, steady, experienced, and ruthless... Admiral of Stars Cattleya wasn't overly surprised.

"Yes, Ma'am Cattleya." Klein gestured for the boss to leave the room.

After it became extremely quiet, Admiral of Stars Cattleya asked once again, "I'm very curious. How did you recognize my identity? I've been very careful when participating in the gatherings."

Of course, she had jumped in fright when The World, who had chosen to speak privately with her, immediately greeted her with "Admiral of Stars."

And it was partly because of this that she chose to take on The World's commission.

"A secret." Klein smiled politely.

Since he didn't want her to connect this to Mr. Fool, he calmly added, "Your eyes are very special."

"Can I understand that as a form of praise?" Cattleya smiled, feeling somewhat enlightened.

She believed that The World had previously suspected she was Admiral of Stars based only on her eyes and other details, but hadn't been certain. Hence, he had tested her verbally, and her reaction had revealed the answer.

Klein didn't respond as he switched to asking, "When can we set off?"

Chapter 636: Slaughterer

Admiral of Stars Cattleya considered the question before answering, "Tonight at eight. Dock 6."

Not bad. There's no delay at all... Klein nodded slightly. "Okay."

Just as he said that, the light inside the crystal ball suddenly converged. The figure dressed in a classical black robe darkened and blurred before quickly vanishing.

Holding the crystal ball which had turned very ordinary, Klein turned around, pulled open the door, and walked out.

Glancing at the boss who was guzzling alcohol while leaning against a wall, he threw the crystal ball at him.

The boss fumbled to catch it as Klein unhurriedly walked down the stairs and left the warehouse.

Once he was out of Hot Whale Dance, he took out his golden pocket watch and snapped it open to check the time.

It's almost noon. Most bars will be open... Klein hailed a rental carriage and told the driver in Feysac to head to the Lerdal Bar. Its name meant Dawn Bar in the local language, and it was a gathering place for adventurers in the Gargas Archipelago.

To Klein, gathering intelligence and news was worthwhile. It might help him make the best decision at a critical moment and escape a fatal misfortune. Therefore, although he disliked bars, he often visited them. He would order a mug of thick malt beer, sit silently at one end of the counter, and listen for anything noteworthy people said about the sea.

In addition, he wanted news of Roy King, who had been imprisoned in Bayam's Governor-General's office. Over the past two months, Snake of Mercury Will Auceptin hadn't sent him any dreams. Similarly, Fate Councilor Ricciardo hadn't summoned his messenger to provide any clues about a powerful mystical item.

About twenty minutes later, the carriage came to a halt. A huge vertical signboard with the words Lerdal Bar appeared before Klein's eyes.

He habitually took out two one-soli notes and handed them to the carriage driver.

Only when he saw the carriage driver's weathered face did he suddenly realize something.

He was overseas and this was a colony of Feysac. It had its own currency!

The smallest denomination was the kopek, followed by the feysilver, and then the gold hoorn. They maintained the very intuitive base-ten denomination, making conversions very simple.

I forgot to visit a bank to exchange money for kopeks and feysilver... I've used Loen currency at the inn and restaurants all this time... Klein was about to look around for a bank when the carriage driver happily accepted the two one-soli notes. After checking them repeatedly, his wrinkled face beamed.

"Thank you, thank you for your generosity!"

Klein got out of the carriage. As he walked toward the Lerdal Bar, he recalled the driver's delighted expression. Gold pounds, soli, and pence can be used here too? Yes, the Gargas Archipelago isn't far from Toscarter and Oravi. Many goods are traded here, and many Loen adventurers visit the Sea of Beluga Whales. The private use of Loen currency must be quite common... Heh heh. Loen's industrial and economic strength exceeds that of the Feysac Empire. It can even be said to be far greater. The gold pound is worth much more than the gold hoorn... Wait!

Suddenly, he recalled something. One gold pound could be used to exchange for 5.5 gold hoorn.

This also meant that two soli were equal to 5.5 feysilver.

And in the City of White, a rental carriage cost four feysilver an hour. There were no prorated fares.

It was no wonder the carriage driver was so happy! Klein turned back and realized that the carriage had long disappeared.

Sigh... Gently exhaling, Klein pressed down on his hat and pushed open the heavy wooden door before entering the Lerdal Bar.

In this world, bars near the docks and factory districts often served lunch and dinner. Therefore, they opened around eleven in the morning. At that moment, quite a number of adventurers with nothing better to do were gathered at the bar counter. They ordered liquor and smoked fish or vegetable soup with oil floating on the surface, heartily eating it all with bread.

Drinking Lanti Proof and Nepos at noon? Are they planning to be drunk the entire day? The customs of Feysac are really unacceptable... Klein mumbled as he walked to the bar counter with a deadpan expression. He sat in a corner and rapped the wooden counter.

"Two slices of toast with red sausage, a serving of smoked whale meat, a bowl of thick vegetable soup, and a cup of Golas beer."

Golas beer was quite popular along Feysac's eastern seaboard but rather rare on Sonia Island and in the Gargas Archipelago.

"New here?" The bartender glanced at Klein. "A total of 4 feysilver and 6 kopek."

I'm new because I don't drink liquor? Klein ignored the bartender's question and threw 2 soli in notes.

It was equivalent to 5 feysilver and 5 kopek.

Considering that he was Gehrman Sparrow, Klein resisted the urge to ask the bartender for change and treated it as a tip.

Of course, in a Backlund bar, that amount of food would've cost him about two soli.

Having received a tip, the bartender asked no further questions. He skillfully poured a mug of Golas beer and pushed it toward Klein.

The beer was nearly black and crowned with abundant foam. It had a pleasant roasted flavor and a slight sharpness beneath its mellow taste. Klein sensed that the alcohol content was rather high and suspected that another liquor had been added.

While waiting for his food, he sipped his beer and silently listened to the conversations of the surrounding adventurers.

The group's conversation centered on who had made a windfall, who had been killed by pirates, who had slain a pirate captain without claiming the bounty and instead inherited the dead captain's crew, which woman in Nas had borne an illegitimate child, and who had become a laughingstock after failing to perform at a brothel.

When the Gargas specialty, thick soup brewed using sweet vegetables, onions, cabbage, carrots, fish, and butter, was served before Klein, he finally heard something interesting.

An adventurer lowered his voice and said to his companions, "Have you heard? There's a Fourth Epoch ruin east of Gargas."

"No! Who discovered it?" his surprised partner curiously asked.

The adventurer glanced around before saying casually, "Gareth discovered it. As you know, he's a Seafarer who's good at diving.

"He was drunk, so he was thrown off the deck and sank to the bottom of the sea. Who would've thought he'd discover the remains of steel buildings? They were definitely man-made!"

"And then?" his partner pressed.

The adventurer chuckled.

"Gareth followed the ruins and discovered an abandoned sea well of unknown depth. Although it was already filled with seawater, it still filled him with extreme terror. Holy Lord of Storms, it might even lead to the center of the earth.

"He said that there was something summoning him inside, but he didn't dare explore it. So he floated up in fear."

A deep-sea well... A strange attraction... This might not be a Fourth Epoch ruin. Perhaps it's from the Third or Second Epoch. Little Sun mentioned that during the Dark Epoch, sea monsters were subordinate to Elf King Soniathrym and helped "Him" rule the ocean depths... Sweet, salty, and a little tart... Klein drank a mouthful of thick vegetable soup as he speared a piece of smoked beluga whale meat with his fork.

The adventurers didn't continue discussing Gareth's encounter, as there was no sign of enticing gold, jewelry, mystical items, or Beyonder ingredients.

The conversation quickly turned to several adventurers they disliked. They mocked them for marrying beautiful natives who had all become stout women a few years later, with strength comparable to that of Low-Sequence Beyonders.

Finally, they concluded that it might have to do with how people from Feysac had tiny bits of giant blood running in their veins.

They didn't say anything of value until Klein finished his lunch and drank his beer, but the number of people inside Lerdal Bar kept increasing.

Suddenly, the heavy door was pushed open as it slammed into the wall.

A top-hat-wearing young man who was clearly of Loen descent rushed in. He shouted nervously, "Is anyone here from the Adventurer Association?"

Yes, Bilt still owes me the creation of one item... Klein watched as the young man looked around frantically while constantly glancing behind him. It appeared as though someone was pursuing him.

Before he could consider whether to render assistance, three adventurers stood up in different corners. One of them was more than two meters tall, with broad shoulders, firm muscles, slightly blond hair, and deep-blue eyes. He was quite striking.

His apparent strength matches his build... He isn't a Beyonder of any particularly high Sequence... Klein retracted his gaze and resumed his role as a spectator, looking toward the door.

Soon, a man in a linen shirt and brown jacket appeared. He was of medium build, and his lips were purple. His brown eyes could barely conceal his intense malice.

He... Klein instantly connected the man to a portrait on a bounty!

It was Slaughterer Kircheis, second mate of the King of Immortality Agalito, with a bounty of 9,500 pounds!

This is a notorious pirate... Klein's nerves tensed up as his gaze locked onto the man. He naturally hung his left hand down as his body prepared to lunge forward.

Kircheis swept his gaze at the two-meter-tall "giant" and saw Klein before retracting his gaze. He turned to leave the Lerdal Bar without stopping at all.

Very decisive and alert... Klein frowned slightly, confident that he hadn't revealed any killing intent. He had even controlled his gaze very well.

Was he wary of that "giant," or is his intuition exceptionally sharp, like a Devil's premonition of danger? Klein wondered as he finished his Golas beer without joining the private conversation among the Adventurer Association members. He left the bar and stepped onto the street.

For now, he didn't wish to invite any new trouble unrelated to finding mermaids.

After circling the area, Klein discovered that Slaughterer Kircheis had long since departed and was nowhere to be found. Hence, he silently returned to his inn.

City of Silver. Inside the spire.

Derrick Berg once again met the Chief of the six-member council, Colin Iliad.

He clearly remembered that nearly seventy "days" ago, Colin had told him that in two months he should prepare to be sent out on an exploration mission.

Chapter 637: The Future

"Have you finished your preparations?" Colin asked with his back to the window, nothing unusual in his tone.

With the Axe of Hurricane attached to him, Derrick lowered his head slightly.

"I'm done."

Over the past two months, through patrol missions and arduous practice, he had fully mastered the various Beyonder powers of a Solar High Priest. He was already close to completely digesting the potion.

What he wanted most was to obtain the Sun pathway's Sequence 6 potion formula within the next two or three Tarot Gatherings, thereby ensuring his continued advancement.

Shepherd Lovia's release left him feeling deeply threatened. He believed that only by reaching Sequence 5 himself would he have a chance of keeping her in check and averting the potential danger to the City of Silver. However, with knowledge of the acting method and no shortage of Beyonder ingredients, he needed only to survive the various patrols and exploration missions and earn enough contributions to bring Sequence 6 within reach. Sequence 5, however, required a specific ritual, making it relatively difficult.

The grizzled Colin nodded.

"In two days, I'll lead a small team to the vicinity of the Giant King's Court. We'll conduct a second sweep of Afternoon Town, which we previously discovered, and your powers are well suited to such a task."

Afternoon Town... Having "brushed up" on his knowledge of legends over the past few months, Derrick was no stranger to the name. It was an area one had to pass through when traveling from the Kingdom of Silver to the Giant King's Court, a town where humans and giants had lived together. It clustered around the ancient god's residence, which was forever fixed at sunset, like the final door separating the real world from myth and legend.

"Yes, Your Excellency." Derrick couldn't find a reason to reject.

City of White, Nas. In a particular inn.

Klein sat behind a desk as he looked at the constantly changing clouds outside the window, silently awaiting the arrival of evening.

At eight, he would board the Star Pirates' flagship, the Future, at Dock 6 and head to the easternmost reaches of the Sonia Sea. No matter what happened in the Gargas Archipelago or this region of the sea known as a pirate's playground, it would have nothing to do with him.

Therefore, he hadn't attempted to hunt the handful of wanted pirates he encountered in the streets, alleys, bars, and casinos. He didn't want to disrupt his plans to find mermaids.

My spiritual intuition tells me that Slaughterer Kircheis has targeted me since noon... I wouldn't mind if he wished to send himself to his death. Well, I can look down on him strategically, but I must take him seriously tactically. Without using the Sea God Scepter or Tinder, I'm at best only a little stronger than him. My advantage is that I'm versatile and strange enough... Klein half-closed his eyes and imagined countless spherical lights gathering, using the image to enter Cogitation rapidly and maintain his spiritual acuity.

After an unknown period of time, his spiritual perception was triggered as he opened his eyes immediately.

At that moment, the sun was already setting in the west. Fiery-red light seemed to set the sea ablaze as long shadows stretched through the curtains.

The shadows seemed to come alive as they danced about before distorting and standing up to project themselves on the wall.

Its pitch-black darkness was like a manifestation of the immense evil at the bottom of the human heart.

Klein watched this scene with a deadpan expression. He raised his left palm high as he slowly extended his fingers.

The pitch-black shadow spoke in a voice as rough as sandpaper. Looking at Klein, it said, "Gehrman Sparrow!

"Do not interfere in the matter that happened this afternoon.

"This is the will of the King of Immortality."

With that said, the shadow slid down like flowing water and scattered into the darkness, having its original state restored.

Klein ignored the shadow's changes and instead cast his gaze outside.

He could sense that the person controlling the shadow was somewhere across the street. Hence, he had no intention of attacking an incorporeal entity.

Indeed, Slaughterer Kircheis recognized me as Gehrman Sparrow, the crazy adventurer who loves hunting pirates. That's why he turned around without hesitation at noon. He might not be afraid of me, but another rather powerful adventurer was present as well...

The power he revealed just now bears some resemblance to the Desire Apostle's powers in Backlund. It's increasingly likely that he's a Beyonder of the Devil pathway...

Heh, using the King of Immortality to frighten me. Do you think I'll submit just like that? I never intended to get involved! Now you've actually made me a little curious... Forget it. Finding the mermaids is my highest priority. I shouldn't stir up trouble for myself... Klein withdrew his gaze, lampooning inwardly.

He was originally wondering if he should report the matter to the Church of the God of Combat in Nas, but after serious consideration, he discovered that it was likely to be meaningless.

The Church of the God of Combat is the only legal religion in the Feysac Empire. It definitely has slightly more High-Sequence Beyonders than the Church of the Evernight Goddess, but not by much. Even with the demigods of the Feysac royal family and military included, Feysac's vast territory, numerous colonies, and many critical areas requiring protection would leave it short of manpower. The Gargas Archipelago is far from the empire, relatively unimportant, and reliant solely on whaling. It's undoubtedly an afterthought and has no saints protecting it.

According to what Klein had previously learned, the archbishop of the Church of the God of Combat's Gargas diocese was only a Sequence 5 Guardian. Together with the tribunal's inquisitor, a baron of the royal family, and a commodore from the military, they formed the highest level of Beyonder combat power in the region.

Of course, Sealed Artifacts guaranteed control of the area. Klein suspected that the Church of the God of Combat in Nas had at least one Grade 1 Sealed Artifact. Only by combining it with their fleets and Beyonders could they ensure that the whale oil and ambergris trade didn't fall under pirate control or become subject to one of the Four Kings. In the event of a sudden assault, they could use it to hold out until reinforcements arrived.

It was precisely because of this that the officials in the Gargas Archipelago maintained a defensive posture. As long as the pirates caused no trouble, they were allowed to come and go freely.

Reporting this would be useless... No wonder it's called a pirate's playground, and no wonder Bilt wants to organize an Adventurer Association... Klein sighed and abandoned the idea.

After dinner and resting for a while, Klein took out his gold pocket watch and opened it to check the time.

Seeing that it was already seven, he held a ritual and summoned himself before responding to himself. He then brought different items into the gray fog while adjusting his inventory.

Since he would be adventuring at sea aboard the Future, the Murloc Cufflink and various charms from the Sea God's domain were essential. Therefore, Klein left Tinder and the Sun Brooch above the gray fog. He chose Creeping Hunger as his primary weapon, supplementing it with the Biological Poison Bottle and a revolver loaded with various Beyonder bullets. He still had 7 purifying bullets, 13 demon-hunting bullets, and 2 exorcism bullets.

Wearing his coat, Klein put Azik's whistle, as well as his wallet containing only fifty pounds into his inner pocket. Then, he stored the silver adventurer harmonica and charms together.

After doing all this, he touched the revolver beneath his armpit, adjusted the blue cufflink at his left wrist, and buttoned his double-breasted frock coat. He put on his half top hat, picked up his black suitcase containing only a change of clothes and some daily necessities, and took a carriage from the inn to the harbor.

After waiting for a while at Dock 6, he saw a gigantic sailing ship slowly appear in the distance.

According to his limited knowledge of ships, Klein knew that a sailing vessel of such length wasn't scientifically sound. However, this didn't prevent it from cruising calmly across the black surface of the sea as it headed toward the shining lighthouse.

As the ship approached, its flag gradually became clear. The faint crimson moonlight allowed the people at the harbor to see ten equally sized white stars encircling a cold, eyelashless eye.

"Admiral of Stars!"

"The Future!"

The pirates and sailors who were watching over their ships exclaimed as excitement swept through every dock in the harbor.

After more than ten seconds, the defensive cannons at both ends of the mountain "nervously" adjusted their aim toward the gigantic sailing ship, which made no attempt to conceal itself.

Clang! Clang! Clang!

The distant cathedral sounded light, ethereal chimes, signaling that it was eight o'clock sharp.

As the tension around the dock became more evident, the Future came to a halt.

At some point, a woman dressed in a classical black robe appeared at the bow. Her clothes were covered in various symbols and magical markings, making her look like a powerful warlock from ancient legend.

Under the crimson moonlight, the ground beneath her suddenly lit up. Resplendent starlight scattered down, forming a long, transparent bridge.

The long bridge constantly extended forward before landing on Dock 6.

An impressive display... As expected of one of the seven pirate admirals. Furthermore, she's a full Admiral, a rank higher than Iceberg and Ailment... Klein sighed, wishing he could raise a hand to cover his face.

He didn't wish to let others know that the crazy adventurer Gehrman Sparrow was cooperating with Admiral of Stars Cattleya.

At this point, I can only maintain my persona. Once I've digested the potion and returned to the Northern Continent, Gehrman Sparrow will no longer exist... Klein stepped out of the shadows, pressed down his half top hat, and walked smoothly onto the starry bridge.

Klein's footsteps were firm and his back straight as numerous defensive cannons remained trained on him. Under countless inscrutable gazes, he walked unhurriedly toward the docked Future.

The bridge beneath his feet was transparent, and the almost-black, dark-blue seawater ebbed below. It was enough to make anyone afraid of heights go weak at the knees.

Thankfully, I've long become a Clown. I've even jumped off a clock tower... Klein covered the last ten meters with a cold expression before stepping onto the deck of the Future.

Faced with the black-eyed Admiral of Stars Cattleya, who had a purple tint to her eyes, he didn't show any nervousness. He took off his hat and bowed.

"Good evening, Ma'am."

Chapter 638: Poison Expert

As Klein bowed, the purple tint in the black-haired Cattleya's eyes deepened, making them seem like an abyss capable of swallowing souls. Their already mysterious air became even more pronounced.

She saw that the Astral Projection deep inside Gehrman Sparrow's Ether Body was pure darkness. In tiny areas, dark currents shifted endlessly.

She saw that Gehrman Sparrow's left hand was bright and lustrous, but it was stained with a sanguine color that couldn't be dispersed.

She saw a blue luster at Gehrman Sparrow's wrist and a silver gleam at his left pocket. The colors were bright and distinct, as though connected to the illusory, layered spirit world. The sounds of tides and illusory winds surrounded the items.

She saw intertwined green and black lusters at Gehrman Sparrow's right pocket, as well as two points of gold, silver, and bronze beneath his armpit. At his chest was a deep grayish-white resembling rotting death.

Four mystical items, along with dozens of Beyonder artifacts or spiritual charms... Dazzled, Cattleya couldn't help closing her eyes for a moment.

She nodded as well.

"Good evening, Mr. Sparrow."

Even as a powerhouse famous across the seas and a senior member of the seven pirate admirals, she seldom encountered anyone armed to the teeth with Beyonder items.

Of course, she had seen people with more mystical items, Beyonder artifacts, and spiritual charms, but they had secret organizations backing them. They typically commanded several ships and more than a thousand pirates. Many items had to be turned in, divided, shared, or sold to maintain and strengthen their factions. As a result, they could keep few for themselves.

However, "few" was relative. They could compare with what Gehrman Sparrow presently had, but Cattleya had reduced her items to only two after several trades. They were two items that were sufficiently powerful and mystical enough to match her identity as a pirate admiral.

Apart from the lusters representing different items, Cattleya's black eyes, which were slightly purple, could also tell that Gehrman didn't seem entirely real. He seemed to be hidden behind thick curtains.

Th-this is why he feels unlike a living person at the Tarot Club? He has quite a number of secrets. They involve at least a Sequence 3 saint or an even higher-level angel... Cattleya didn't dare take another look, and the glow in her eyes faded.

Meanwhile, she mocked herself, finding some of her guesses about The World and Gehrman Sparrow rather amusing.

Involves a Sequence 3 saint or an even higher-level angel. Heh heh, there's nothing strange about that. Like me, he's a member of the Tarot Club. We're both involved with Mr. Fool, who's likely a reawakened ancient god.

After exchanging greetings, Cattleya didn't engage in further small talk. She led Klein toward the cabin.

At that moment, the docked Future set sail again. It traced a long wake across the sea as it cruised eastward from the whaling route. With that, the tense atmosphere in Nas Harbor dispersed.

Various sailors cast their gazes over. Klein showed no sign of pressure as he surveyed the area and said in a tone as casual as if he were in his own home, "There are fewer people than I expected."

Cattleya turned her head and glanced at him before answering simply, "The area east of the whaling route is very dangerous. Only Ludwell goes there frequently, as though he's searching for something.

"The pirate crew's other ships won't join the Future. Likewise, most of the sailors have been assigned to those vessels. We've kept only the minimum number needed to ensure a successful voyage."

A very wise choice... Ludwell. That Admiral Hell Ludwell? Mr. Hanged Man mentioned him a long time ago. He said that he was beginning to study Sonia Sea's eastern end of the navigation... Klein nodded a little without a word.

This was Gehrman Sparrow's bearing.

He silently observed his surroundings and realized that the deck, cabins, and spars were covered with mysterious, abstract symbols. Together, they formed what looked like a gigantic ritualistic magic circle.

It's like the Golden Dream. It's a mystical sailing ship, but it hasn't reached the level of a ghost ship... Compared with Admiral of Stars and Vice Admiral Iceberg, Tracy, who became a pirate admiral only a few months ago, is clearly far inferior. The Black Death has nothing special about it... Klein retracted his gaze and followed Cattleya to the cabin entrance.

A man in dungarees and a white shirt waited there. He was in his thirties, strong and fit, with thick body hair. His exposed forearms looked as though they were covered by a brown sweater.

He wore a round hat with a depression in the middle as he revealed a smile and stretched out his right hand.

"The first mate of the Future, Frank Lee.

"Good evening, Mr. Sparrow."

Poison Expert Frank Lee who has a bounty of 7,000 pounds... Klein instantly recognized the man.

Considering how Gehrman Sparrow didn't have the habit of shaking hands, he bent his back slightly and said, "Good evening."

Frank Lee withdrew his right hand and glanced at Cattleya. Maintaining his smile, he said, "You seem to know me? That's right. My portrait appears on my wanted poster.

"Don't mind the title Poison Expert. I'm a very friendly person, as long as I'm not facing those damned bastards! My greatest hobby is studying soil and developing crossbreeding techniques. Believe me, this is the future of humanity."

"But the people from the Church of Earth Mother don't believe him." Cattleya gave Klein an introduction in the most subtle manner.

He was originally from the Church of Earth Mother? Crossbreeding techniques are good. Increased production could feed more people and lift them out of poverty... Klein looked at Frank Lee and said calmly, courteously, and sincerely, "That is commendable."

"You truly are an adventurer who doesn't share the common man's perspective! Excellent! Excellent! There are far too few people like you!" Pleasant surprise shone clearly in Frank Lee's blue eyes. "I merely tried crossbreeding a bull, a cow, and wheat so that calves could grow like wheat. For that, the Church of Earth Mother nearly put me on trial. My benevolent mother, they don't understand your true will at all!"

Cross-breeding a bull, a cow, and wheat... You devil... Only then did Klein realize that his understanding of cross-breeding techniques was different from what Frank Lee was talking about.

He maintained a stoic expression without offering any further explanation. Led by Frank Lee, who had suddenly become enthusiastic, Klein walked through the corridor beside Cattleya.

After climbing the stairs to a higher deck, Klein's spiritual perception stirred, and he cast his gaze toward a shadow beside him.

A figure emerged from the distorted darkness--thin, tall, and pale.

His cheeks were nearly transparent, and the bridge of his nose was incongruously high. His entire being exuded a sickly air.

"This is the second mate of the Future, Heath Doyle," Frank Lee introduced with a smile.

Bloodless Heath whose bounty is higher than yours at 7,600 pounds? Klein quickly matched the face to the correct bounty notice.

Heath nodded as a greeting before receding back into the shadows.

"He's a Rose Bishop." As he turned toward the captain's cabin, Frank Lee cheerfully explained Heath Doyle's situation.

You're telling me your second mate's Sequence outright? Klein nearly raised an eyebrow.

He secretly glanced at Cattleya and discovered her masked exasperation.

"Are you puzzled and worried that he's a devout believer of the True Creator, a lunatic who has been corrupted? No, haha. He's a lucky fellow. In an accident, a potion concocted from a Rose Bishop's Beyonder ingredients splashed onto him while he was an ordinary man. The potion seeped into him bit by bit, and he survived the ordeal, eventually becoming a rather special Beyonder. Since he never experienced the Listener stage, he wasn't corrupted or driven mad. As long as he doesn't actively use the Beyonder power Listen, he remains an ordinary person," Frank Lee said with evident pride.

What an enviable fellow. He reached Sequence 6 in one go. Furthermore, he didn't turn mentally unstable like the sea serpent, Kalvetua... Klein sighed silently as he watched Admiral of Stars open the door to the captain's cabin.

Cattleya gestured inside.

"Your room is diagonally across from here. Frank will send you over. If there's anything, you can come to me directly.

"You may eat in the dining hall at any time, or ask Frank to have someone bring food to your room. The choice is yours."

Admiral of Stars handles matters clearly and directly. Yes, she's a mature and wise lady... Klein praised her inwardly as he replied without changing his expression, "Okay."

He took the opportunity to glance around the captain's cabin, noting a bookshelf, a full-length mirror, a table, and a carpet.

She actually reads. Isn't she unable to digest all the information the Hidden Sage imbues in her... Klein thought in amusement.

Seeing their conversation come to an end, Frank Lee enthusiastically suggested, "Mr. Sparrow, would you like a serving of roasted potatoes tonight?

"They chew and taste like beef, but they're definitely potatoes."

Will I get sick eating that... Klein answered coldly, "I don't eat supper."

"Alright then." Frank Lee drooped his shoulders and was just about to lead Klein to his room.

Cattleya suddenly thought of something and said, "Mr. Sparrow, you've likely hunted quite a number of enemies and obtained plenty of spoils.

"I'm curious how many Beyonder characteristics you have left. Perhaps they can be used to reduce the remaining cost of the voyage."

Klein considered for a moment and answered frankly, "Most of them have been sold.

"I have Interrogator, Pugilist, and Lunatic left."

He didn't mention the All-Black Eye or the Beyonder characteristic of Nightmare.

He really is the most famous adventurer during the past few months... Even after selling most of them, he still has three sets of Beyonder characteristics... Cattleya sighed inwardly as she pondered.

"I want Pugilist."

She had a Warrior subordinate from the Gargas Archipelago who had long made enough contributions to apply for an advancement.

Chapter 639: Name List

Klein had no reason to refuse Cattleya's request. After two seconds of thought, he said, "700 pounds.

"Very fair." Cattleya didn't haggle.

Sequence 8 Beyonder characteristics typically cost 600 to 700 pounds, but there was often a premium when someone desperately needed one. After all, such items seldom appeared at Beyonder gatherings.

Klein didn't immediately complete the transaction. He raised his suitcase and said without a change in expression, "I'll give it to you tomorrow."

He didn't bring the Pugilist Beyonder characteristic with him, as most items of value had been thrown above the gray fog.

Cattleya wasn't surprised by Gehrman Sparrow's response. She nodded.

"That won't be an issue."

She had encountered many similar situations. It usually meant that the Pugilist Beyonder characteristic was stored alongside items that weren't meant to be seen by others. Therefore, Gehrman Sparrow couldn't open his suitcase in front of her and complete the transaction on the spot.

A crazy adventurer definitely had his secrets!

Cattleya paused for a second before adding, "No surveillance will be placed in your room."

She had already seen everything she could.

"It's fine." The corners of Klein's lips curled into a cold smile.

His original plan had been to put down items such as Azik's copper whistle and Will Auceptin's paper crane before performing a ritual or going above the gray fog. This would resist any spying through mystical means, while the washroom would let him avoid physical surveillance.

Of course, he wasn't too worried that Admiral of Stars would overstep. He didn't believe she was afraid of the crazy adventurer Gehrman Sparrow, but Cattleya had no reason to do so. She certainly had subtler, less intrusive means of observation.

For example, getting the help of a Beyonder from the Spectator pathway... Klein took off his hat, bowed, and followed Poison Expert Frank Lee to return to his own room.

Watching him depart at an unhurried pace, Cattleya entered the captain's cabin, picked up a heavy pair of glasses, and put them on.

Backlund, at the Harvest Church south of the Bridge.

Bishop Utravsky, who looked as tall as a half-giant to an ordinary person, put down the Bible in his hand. He glanced around the hall and smiled.

"The number of believers is increasing."

"Is that so?" Emlyn White, dressed in a brown priest's robe, said without looking up from the candle racks he was wiping.

He knew that the number of believers in Earth Mother had indeed increased significantly. In the past, it would've been a miracle for even a handful of people to attend daytime prayers on weekdays. Now, there were often nearly ten.

Father Utravsky looked down at the busy vampire priest and chuckled.

"Anyone can tell if they aren't blind.

"You have contributed greatly to this. Without the medicine you prepared, your treatment of those affected by the plague, and your sincere willingness to teach people about medicine, our faith wouldn't have been so readily accepted by this borough's residents.

Emlyn held a cloth and straightened his back. He tipped his chin slightly.

"I was only acting."

What, what do you mean by "our faith?" Don't lump me with you! Emlyn's expression twisted before he said with a smile, "Speaking of the blind, it reminds me of a joke. It's said that the demand for blind people in Backlund far exceeds supply. This is because they are widely believed to be most suitable to be jurors."

Father Utravsky ignored the joke and said benevolently, "Regardless of your goal, you have contributed greatly to spreading faith in Earth Mother.

"Besides, this also proves that you have a kind heart."

Peh! I said so back then, but why didn't you believe me? Emlyn looked up at the priest's massive body before retracting his gaze in silence.

After finishing his work at the cathedral, he changed into his usual clothes and put on a silk top hat to shelter himself from the sun. He then stepped onto Rose Street.

He discreetly surveyed his surroundings and didn't notice any signs of monitoring.

That Red Glove named Leonard really hasn't appeared again... I thought exposing his secret and buying Tinder would result in my being constantly investigated... Emlyn White shook his head almost imperceptibly, feeling puzzled.

At first, he had planned to adopt The Hanged Man's suggestion and draw the Red Gloves' attention, thereby disrupting the surveillance of the Sanguine's upper echelons. He could then achieve a perfect balance by relying on Utravsky, the Church of Earth Mother's Blessed.

But things didn't develop as he had expected. Leonard Mitchell had apparently left Backlund very quickly.

At the end of February, Emlyn had made a detour to 7 Pinster Street while he was out once, only to discover that it was uninhabited.

Without thinking about this matter any further, he walked to the end of the street, got onto a rental carriage and headed straight for the Odora villa.

An attendant led him to a study on the first floor, where he saw many Sanguine who had recently come of age. Among them was one who had already become a Baron.

I'm not the only one? he thought as he closed the door. He then asked the host, Cosmi Odora, "My lord, wasn't I called here because Lord Nibbs summoned me?"

He had said "my lord" rather indifferently, as though addressing Cosmi by name. This was because he was also a Sanguine Baron, the difference being that he hadn't publicized it.

Cosmi was a very refined middle-aged gentleman. He smiled and said, "All of you are outstanding young Sanguine. My grandfather has entrusted all of you with some tests."

Another Sanguine Baron, Rus Bathory, who had been lucky enough to receive an inheritance, asked, "What kind of tests?"

Cosmi took a sip of dark-red blood blended with grape wine as his gaze swept the room.

"You might not know this, but the Snake of Fate from the Life School of Thought disappeared quite some time ago."

As Emlyn White and the other Sanguine looked puzzled, Cosmi continued, "But I believe all of you know that the Life School of Thought is a loose coalition held together by a shared belief in fate. It formed under the immense pressure exerted by the seven orthodox Churches at the beginning of the Fifth Epoch and consists of Beyonders who consider themselves absolutely rational and aloof, those who worship the Primordial Moon, and those who seek the essence of life and spirituality.

"Thanks to the Snake of Fate, there hadn't been many serious internal problems. Their worship of the Primordial Moon was ultimately restricted to a fixed symbolic meaning. Furthermore, formula exchanges were integrated with a master-apprentice system of inheritance.

"Unfortunately, heh, with the disappearance of the Snake of Fate, this process has been disrupted. The Primordial Moon's believers rapidly grew fanatical, producing a fracture in the faction that represents fate. An intense conflict ensued as a result.

"This situation was exploited by the Rose School of Thought. It dealt them considerable damage."

Emlyn White, Rus Bathory, and the other Sanguine remained perplexed. Having provided the background, Cosmi turned to the details.

"Worshipers of the Primordial Moon are our eternal enemies. They have always been hunting us, concocting us into potions to gain power!

"Now, we will not let go of such an opportunity.

"Here is a list of Primordial Moon worshipers and information about their circumstances. Whoever finds and kills them will win this test. My grandfather and several other important figures will reward you generously.

"If you complete only part of the list, the result will be decided by the number eliminated.

"Of course, these people were specifically selected because all of you are capable of dealing with them. The important figures will track down the rest."

Upon hearing the first few sentences, Emlyn White had no intention of participating despite his hatred for those "artificial" vampires. He found the matter troublesome, laborious, and tiresome. He would rather stay home and quietly chat with his dolls.

Once he realized it was a competition and noticed the gazes Rus Bathory and the other Sanguine directed at him, Emlyn abruptly straightened his back and raised his chin.

"No problem."

His red eyes slowly swept across the other creatures in the room.

On the Future, inside a spacious room with an attached bathroom.

Klein stood before the window, looking out at the undulating blue ocean. In a good mood, he breathed in the cold wind from the north.

The flagship of the Admiral of Stars had already separated from the other ships in the fleet as it began to cruise alone.

After an indeterminate period, two whaling ships appeared ahead. The moment their crews noticed the Future's flag with its stars and eye, they reacted nervously.

They quickly adjusted their positions, turning their starboard sides toward the Future and readying their cannons to fire at any moment.

At the same time, explosive-propelled spears and harpoons were trained in the same direction. The large, blond fishermen of the Gargas Archipelago gripped tridents and guns as they braced for an attack, some standing and others crouching.

Klein also noticed that there were a number of women with Hulk-like physiques. It was obvious at a glance that they were sturdy and formidable in a fight.

The Future paid no heed to their "welcome." It continued sailing past them, heading deeper along the whaling route.

After sailing for a while, Klein saw more pirate ships ahead. They were clustered around the periphery of the sea route, their motives unclear.

The Future approached without ceremony. The pirate ships immediately scattered like startled birds.

Sensing the Future slow down, Klein left his room to investigate.

Just as he approached the captain's cabin, he discovered Cattleya walking out. This woman wore a thick pair of glasses on her nose bridge, hiding her mysterious purple eyes.

She glanced at Klein and said succinctly, "Someone discovered ruins said to date from the Fourth Epoch on the seabed in these waters.

"We plan to take a look on our way."

The seabed? Fourth Epoch ruins? Klein instantly recalled the rumor he had heard in the Lerdal Bar.

So this piece of news has already spread? I was wondering why I got to learn it so easily... he thought with sudden understanding.

Chapter 640: Female Pirate

Klein was curious about the outcome of the exploration. Without asking further, he passed the captain's cabin and descended the stairs.

After a few seconds, he felt his throat itch. He couldn't help but raise his fist to his mouth and cough.

Klein wasn't surprised by this inevitable development. He had carried the Biological Poison Bottle for more than two hours the previous night. Only late that night, after confirming that Admiral of Stars and her crew had no intention of attacking him, had he moved the mystical item into his black suitcase. Unfortunately, he had already fallen ill.

Of course, he hadn't carried the Biological Poison Bottle for very long, and his body wasn't weakened, so the resulting illness wasn't serious. His tonsils were merely swollen and painful.

Cattleya, who was walking slowly behind, saw this scene, but she didn't find it a problem. Instead, she found it normal.

Every knowledgeable Beyonder knew that mystical items inevitably had negative effects. Following the example of the seven orthodox Churches, they classified items whose severe effects prevented prolonged possession or use as Sealed Artifacts.

Cattleya had sold or exchanged many of her low- and mid-level mystical items, both to improve her chances of survival and because managing the negative effects of multiple items was troublesome. Avoiding one effect often meant suffering another, and some could even combine into something worse. Therefore, for most Beyonders, the disadvantages outweighed the advantages.

She had discovered that Gehrman Sparrow was armed to the teeth with mystical items, Beyonder weapons, and spiritual charms. Even amid her astonishment, she had begun guessing what negative effects he faced. From what she saw today, one of them was a simple, temporary illness.

With steady steps, Klein reached the deck and saw Frank Lee. He was still dressed in a white shirt and dungarees, his arms stained with dirt as though he didn't feel the cold wind sweeping across the deck.

"Good morning, Gehrman." Frank waved warmly and said, "Here, try my latest product. This will definitely be the most popular item at sea!"

As he spoke, he raised his other hand. In it was a broad, fat fish of an unknown species.

No, I don't want to know what "monster" you have cooked up this time... Klein paused in his footsteps as he looked at him with a cold expression.

Frank Lee noticed nothing amiss in his attitude. He drew a dagger from his waist, stabbed the fish, and sliced it open.

Blood flowed out and landed squarely in a large beer mug on the deck. It gave off no fishy smell.

"Smell that? What an intoxicating aroma of alcohol!" Frank Lee half-closed his eyes and said fervently, "This fish has red wine instead of blood. That means the wine contains plenty of nutrients!"

Klein found himself at a loss for words.

Frank looked excitedly at the surrounding sailors before saying to Gehrman Sparrow, "Do you know what the greatest difficulty at sea is? Running out of alcohol while nowhere near shore! As long as this kind of fish can reproduce rapidly and become common throughout the ocean, we'll never lack alcohol, no matter where we are. They can also be divided into species. Some will produce Lanti Proof; others, Nepos, red wine, or beer. Beer needs to come from sharks or whales, though. Otherwise, there won't be enough!"

Isn't the greatest difficulty a lack of water? Of course, all of you can replace water with beer most of the time because it doesn't spoil easily... Those poor fish... Klein was considering how to respond to Frank when Cattleya came on deck. She walked past him and questioned her first mate.

"Is Nina done with her preparations?"

"Yes, she has already finished off a bottle of Nepos!" Frank pointed toward a shadow cast by the sails.

So the so-called preparations consist of drinking a bottle of Nepos, a Feysac specialty? The high-proof Nepos that can be set alight? Klein suddenly felt that Nina, the lady mentioned by the first mate, likely had Feysac blood.

"Captain, I want a bottle of Sonia blood wine more!" In the dark, a female figure slowly stood up and walked over.

She was more than 1.8 meters tall, with her blond hair casually tied in a high ponytail. Her features were rather plain, but she bore the distinctive traits of a Feysacian. Her skin was fair, and her eyes were dull.

This lady named Nina wore a tight black suit made of fish skin. The top and bottom seemed to be a one-piece, fully accentuating her stunning figure.

The outfit was already quite revealing, and Nina's breasts were far larger than average. It was obvious what the surrounding pirates were looking at.

Klein felt a little embarrassed and wanted to look away. On second thought, however, Gehrman Sparrow was definitely not so inexperienced. All he could do was make his gaze vacant and look directly at Nina's face.

"Gehrman, this is our boatswain, Nina! She's also the navigator's assistant. Haha, her Sequence is called Seafarer!" Frank Lee still held nothing back in his introductions.

Apart from his research into crossbreeding, this Poison Expert is rather simple... I remember now that this lady named Nina has a bounty of 3,600 pounds. Her nickname is Seabed Murderer. Sigh, after seeing so many bounty notices, there are some I can't immediately recall... Klein looked Nina in the eyes and nodded calmly.

"Good morning, Ma'am."

Nina held back her smile and sized up Klein.

"Good morning, Mr. Sparrow.

"I'm very curious if Vice Admiral Ailment is really as charming as the rumors say?"

As a female pirate who had spent years among the lower and middle classes, she was frank and direct with men and women alike, without the slightest coyness. She had originally intended to ask Gehrman Sparrow whether she lacked charm or whether his cold temperament explained why he had ignored her without the slightest reaction. However, the man before her was powerful: an adventurer who had nearly succeeded in hunting Vice Admiral Ailment and a lunatic who might draw his gun and fire at any moment. She wisely swallowed the teasing words and asked about Vice Admiral Ailment instead.

...How am I supposed to answer that? Klein said gloomily, "Her bounty is extremely charming."

Nina was taken aback and at a loss for a response. Hence, she turned to look at Admiral of Stars.

"Captain, do we begin now?"

For some inexplicable reason, Cattleya suddenly recalled her own bounty. She nodded.

"Begin."

As soon as she said that, Nina strode to the side of the ship. Bracing herself with her right hand, she leaped into the sea and swam downward like a gigantic black fish.

Several splashes followed as a few sailors jumped down to assist her.

This... She dives the moment it's mentioned. She didn't need any additional time to prepare... This lady has the temperament of the Church of Storms. As expected of a Beyonder from the Sailor pathway... Klein looked over the side of the ship and couldn't help coughing.

"You're sick?" Frank Lee asked directly.

Klein nodded slightly and said, "A little."

Frank thought for a moment, then rushed back into the cabin without a word or any explanation of where he was going.

Beside him, Cattleya pushed up her heavy glasses and smiled.

"Frank is a Poison Expert, but he's also an outstanding doctor."

As expected of someone from the Planter pathway... Klein didn't ask further as he stood there, awaiting Nina's preliminary exploration.

As the sudden silence grew awkward, Cattleya took a few steps forward and said, seemingly in passing, "In another day, we'll leave the whaling route."

"But aren't we at least a week away from those waters?" Klein asked after a moment's thought.

"That would be true if we followed the whaling route, but it would take us farther north on a long detour. I know a secret route that will let us reach the waters you seek in two or three days." Cattleya looked into Klein's eyes through her thick glasses, as though trying to determine how much he knew about those extremely dangerous waters.

Klein considered this and simply said, "Very good. That's exactly what I want.

"In addition, those waters are more illusory than real."

Cattleya retracted her gaze in thought as she looked at the entrance to the cabin.

Frank Lee ran over and held a green apple in his hand.

"This is the outcome of another one of my projects. It's a crossbreed of medicine and fruit. It makes taking medicine more enjoyable!" He beamed as he handed Klein the apple.

I'm afraid I'll end up even sicker after eating it... Klein glanced at Admiral of Stars and saw her nod gently. Only then did he force himself to accept the apple, remaining outwardly calm. He then bit into it.

It had the taste of an ordinary apple. It just had a lot more juice, and its flesh was softer.

After a few bites, Klein discovered that his throat had abruptly stopped hurting. He hadn't even noticed when his coughing had ceased.

I have to admit that it's rather magical... As long as it doesn't involve animals or humans, Frank Lee is truly a genius who should be taken seriously by the Church of Earth Mother. Unfortunately, he ultimately became a devil-like figure... Klein looked at the Poison Expert and said frankly, "It's healed."

"Very good." Frank didn't praise himself as he began casually introducing the pirates on the deck.

She held a piece of metal corroded beyond recognition and a piece of black, hardened mud riddled with honeycomb-like holes. She grumbled to Cattleya, "Captain, there's no deep-sea well!

"That well's mouth isn't even bigger than my breasts!

"Of course, it's very deep and dark. It's unknown what's hidden in it."

She gestured using her hands.

"Exaggeration is a common trait of pirates and adventurers." Cattleya nodded without using the term "bragging."

The mouth of the well is that narrow? Puzzled, Klein looked at the item in Nina's hand as he politely shifted his gaze away from the streams of water flowing down her body.

Cattleya's gaze moved in sync with his as she said, "Go into the details."

Chapter 641: A Well Mouth That Humans Can't Pass

Nina was a pirate who had risen through the ranks. She was often quick-tempered, but her wealth of experience made her dependable in a crisis. After some thought, she gave a serious account of what she had found.

"The mouth of the well lies deep on the seabed. I needed time to adjust before I could acclimate to the temperature and pressure down there. That's why it took me so long to reach it.

"It isn't easy to find, but the remains of the steel buildings are quite conspicuous. I spotted them once I'd acclimated.

"They've completely collapsed or rusted away, so there's no telling what they originally looked like. Still, I could tell they once covered a vast area. They've merely shrunk a great deal since then."

At this point, Nina chuckled and swept her gaze over all the men around her.

A genuine female pirate really is different... Klein sighed inwardly.

From his point of view, whether it was Admiral of Stars Cattleya, Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina, or Vice Admiral Ailment Tracy, none of them could be considered a true female pirate. All of them hailed from major factions or secret organizations. When they were Low-Sequence Beyonders, they were either not at sea or were following important figures, so the work they did was relatively safe. Otherwise, they had been independent adventurers, never shaped by the character and atmosphere of low- and mid-level pirates.

After Nina finished laughing, Cattleya pointed to the rusted item that could hardly be described as a metal beam.

"This is part of one of the steel buildings?"

"Yes, Captain. As you know, I don't know much about history or mysticism. All I could do was bring a piece back for you to inspect. You're the expert here." Nina smiled as she handed over the "metal beam."

Then, she pointed at the black piece of hardened mud whose surface was riddled with holes.

"Not far from the metal ruins, I found a well. It isn't very big. If anyone wants to call it 'huge,' I've certainly seen plenty of cannons more deserving of the word.

"Those drunken adventurers are better at bragging than us pirates!

"This is the mud from the inner layer of the well. I can't imagine how such patterns could be formed!"

Nina repeatedly tapped the honeycombed dots on the black mud.

Klein initially believed they were marks left by a dense volley of tiny projectiles, but after careful inspection, he suspected that the "patterns" were remnants of something that had rotted away. Each mark was very shallow, with edges that spread outward irregularly.

Nina handed the black mud to Cattleya as she continued, "The well's mouth is really small. Even a child from Nas wouldn't be able to enter.

"It's very deep. It even felt bottomless. In that environment, the interior was completely dark, as though something was slowly summoning me. Yes--slowly.

"I found a few rocks nearby and threw them in, but there wasn't any response. In short, it's filled with water."

Cattleya held up the "metal beam" and black mud, examining them closely through her thick glasses.

"Since the opening is very small and humans can't enter it, there's no need for us to begin exploring immediately. It would be very dangerous.

"Let's wait until I uncover the secrets hidden in these items and determine whether the ancient well is worth the risk before we return and make an attempt."

"Aye aye, Captain!" Soaked through, Nina trembled in the cold wind. The way she shivered made all the surrounding pirates stare fixedly at her.

Cattleya nudged her glasses and said to Nina, "You can drink a bottle of Sonia blood wine. There's no limit for the rest."

"Long live the Captain!" Nina cried out in joy.

An underwater well that humans can't enter... Klein, who had no desire to explore it, summarized Nina's description.

Suddenly, he had a strange idea.

Humans are unable to enter the well, but that doesn't mean that non-humans can't do it!

Many deep-sea fish aren't necessarily that huge. There's a significant chance that they can pass through the well.

As the "Sea God," he had the means to make marine creatures do his bidding by wielding the scepter!

There's no rush. Let's see if Ma'am Hermit can learn anything from these two objects. I'll consider exploring it on the return trip; otherwise, I might draw the attention of some overwhelming danger... There still isn't enough information. There's no way to perform a divination... As his thoughts wandered, Klein's expression remained impassive.

At that moment, Cattleya shot him a glance out of curiosity. Then, she retracted her gaze without betraying any sign of it.

Why did she suddenly look at me? Was she watching what I was doing? It's impossible for her to know that I possess the Sea God Scepter and can make marine creatures do my bidding. That's not right. She does know, but she only knows that Mr. Fool wields Kalvetua's godhood scepter, not The World... Unless she has figured out that The World is The Fool... But that's even more impossible. Even Mr. Hanged Man still believes that The World is a Blessed. She hasn't even realized that...

Looking at it from another angle, I'll consider it from the Admiral of Stars's perspective... She's pursued by knowledge and is a follower of Queen Mystic. She's loyal to the Moses Ascetic Order and has roamed the sea for years. She possesses plenty of knowledge and experience, so it wouldn't be strange for her to know that the Sea God domain includes the Beyonder power to control marine creatures.

Therefore, after realizing that humans are unable to pass through the ancient well, she naturally connected it to the scepter in Mr. Fool's hand. Does she plan to request help in the future? Did she look at me to find out whether The World had also grasped the relevant information or had a similar idea?

Countless ideas raced through Klein's mind. With the powers of Clown, he forcefully maintained his indifferent expression. He didn't react abnormally in any way.

As Nina was about to draw the Sonia blood wine, Klein pressed down his hat and returned to the cabin.

Just as he was about to approach the door, a figure suddenly surfaced in his mind.

In a room on the cabin's upper level, the windows were tightly shut and the curtains drawn. A pair of blurry eyes was hidden behind them, silently watching the crowd on the deck as well as Gehrman Sparrow.

Who is it? Klein didn't stop. He showed no hesitation as he entered the cabin normally as though nothing had happened.

At three in the afternoon, the bright but not scorching sunlight shone into the garden adjacent to Stoen University.

By the age of forty, Michele Deuth had already become a senior associate professor. Wearing a long tailcoat and a beautiful bow tie, he waited by the door.

The previous evening, he had received a letter from an attendant of the Hall family, the wealthiest aristocratic family in East Chester County. It had been written on behalf of the daughter of an immensely influential Member of Parliament in the House of Lords. She was Miss Audrey Hall, reputed to be Backlund's most dazzling gem.

This noble lady had mentioned in the letter that she had learned from a gathering that Mr. Michele Deuth was an outstanding collector and an aficionado of this domain. She very much wished to pay him a visit.

Michele Deuth had no reason to refuse.

Soon, a classic carriage with a family emblem arrived at the door.

Two servants, who had been instructed to open the outer wrought-iron gates, guided the carriage around the garden to the front of the house.

A housekeeper was the first to alight, followed by guards and maidservants.

Next, a hand wearing a long white gauze glove was extended outside.

With the help of the maidservants, Audrey elegantly stepped onto the carpet Michele had laid out.

Michele was taken aback, and then his eyes lit up. He felt as though the flowers in the garden had instantly lost their color.

He took two steps forward and took off his hat to bow.

"Welcome, my honorable lady.

"Your visit is an honor for me and my family."

Audrey took off the veiled hat and passed it to her maidservant before exchanging a few pleasantries. Then, she followed Michele Deuth into the living room and entered the collector's room on the first floor.

Here, Michele finally found his confidence as the master of the house. He began pointing at his collection and introducing the pieces from left to right.

"This is a helmet from the White Rose War. After extensive research, it was determined that its owner was a member of the Sauron family. Back then, they were still royalty."

The golden helmet had an intricate design. Birds and outspread wings adorned it, and the visor was formed from pieces of golden scales.

"My ancestor obtained his first aristocratic title in that war," Audrey replied with keen interest.

She had adjusted her mental state beforehand so that she would appear to be genuinely interested in touring the collection.

"The defeat in the Twenty Years' War caused the kingdom to suffer years of humiliation, but it also forged many heroes." Michele resorted to flattery.

The White Rose War happened after the Twenty Years' War and before the Battle of the Violated Oath. There, Loen defeated Intis and became strong again.

Michele continued introducing his collection as Audrey solemnly listened, occasionally asking questions as she conversed with him.

Finally, Michele's finger pointed toward a notebook with a black cover.

"This belonged to a knight stationed on Sonia Island in the Twenty Years' War.

"This knight's name has already vanished into the long river of history. This notebook is the only proof of his existence. He once held his ground on Sonia Island until the very last moment.

"This notebook is not only a firsthand account for research into that portion of history, but it also hides certain problems. The knight's grammar has many uncommon habits. This might be a clue to aid us in determining his exact identity."

Audrey instinctively believed that the notebook was her target. Hence, she only leaned in a little. Indeed, the black cover had faint patterns. Together, they outlined an abstract image of a dragon.

From Michele's tone and subtle expressions, his interest is focused on the contents rather than the item itself. He doesn't particularly treasure it... I have a good chance of purchasing it... Audrey calmly made her assessment. Turning her head, she smiled at Michele Deuth and asked, "What exactly are those uncommon habits?"

"He favors short sentences--very simple, concise ones..." Michele explained with a hint of ostentation.

Audrey was always a good listener. She smiled at him and listened attentively. This made Michele speak more.

As she listened, she suddenly felt that the knight's grammatical habits were somewhat familiar.

That is... Audrey's eyes darted around slightly as she quickly remembered the source of the familiarity.

This was a grammatical habit of Dragonese which she had diligently mastered!

Chapter 642: Killing Three Birds With One Stone

A knight who knew Dragonese and even used it during the critical period of his development... No wonder the Psychology Alchemists wish to obtain the notebook he left behind... Audrey understood at once and reined in her wandering thoughts. She cheerfully but earnestly discussed the implications of such grammatical habits with Michele Deuth.

Soon, they concluded the discussion over the notebook as Michele began introducing the other items.

As time passed, the tour slowly drew to a close. Audrey had planned to raise her request to purchase the notebook at their next meeting, making her goal less obvious. However, amid the friendly atmosphere and natural flow of their conversation, she keenly sensed an opportunity. Hence, using the powers of Lie, she made her cheeks flush red.

"Mr. Deuth, that helmet from the Sauron royal family, dating back to the White Rose War, reminds me of my ancestors. Pardon my presumption, but may I buy it from you? I would also like the notebook from the Twenty Years' War. I'm deeply impressed by the knight who stood fast on Sonia Island and would like to have it as well.

"I know it's not a polite request, but I hope you can understand how I feel. Of course, you have the right to decline."

Her eyes visibly darted about, partly deliberate and partly genuine, conveying her uncertainty and embarrassment.
