Michele subconsciously moved his gaze away as he said slowly in a hoarse voice, "I'm a collector. I won't sell my collection."

Neither his tone nor his wording is firm enough... According to the information I gathered earlier, he's a gentleman who values his reputation greatly. He probably finds the idea of selling his collection for cash unacceptable... The Psychology Alchemists likely didn't have someone else complete the mission partly because I needed a way to earn contribution points and partly because they had considered the associate professor's attitude toward such matters... I have to change my approach. Before the visit, Audrey had carefully devised different tests based on the intelligence she had received. After some thought, she changed the subject.

"Mr. Deuth, I heard that you're requesting Stoen University to build an ancient relic research center?"

"Yes, that has been my goal for the past few years." Michele looked at Audrey and answered frankly.

Audrey smiled faintly and said, "I'm very interested in the research in this area. I also feel a deep respect for you and hope to see your wish fulfilled.

"Well, I'm planning on donating 1,000 pounds, a nearby piece of land spanning 2,000 ares [1], and a manor with rather good returns to your faculty at Stoen University. I hope to have a non-profit historical relic research and preservation foundation set up. I know it's not a lot, but I will drum up support from the ladies and gentlemen I know in order to persuade them to contribute as well.

"Mr. Deuth, I think you're the most professional relic collector and researcher I know. I'm wondering if you're willing to be the person in charge of this non-profit foundation?"

Two thousand ares of land near the university. That's worth about 6,000 pounds. Together with the manor and the cash, Miss Audrey would be donating nearly 10,000 pounds... With such a nonprofit foundation for the research and preservation of relics, it would be far easier to secure approval for my research grants... Michele paused for a few seconds before revealing a sincere smile. He bowed solemnly and said, "My honorable lady, the importance you place on academia has deeply moved me. Its radiance matches your beauty and refinement. I believe I have no reason to reject your invitation.

"I've already recorded the contents of the notebook. I will send it and the helmet to your residence tonight. Treat it as a gift from a sincere friend."

Success! Audrey was delighted as she wished to praise herself. However, she appeared reserved and indifferent. She didn't do anything improper.

"It's my honor," she said earnestly.

Although the two items were definitely not worth 10,000 pounds, it wouldn't result in any loss on her part.

In her plans, this proposal accomplished three important goals and yielded three returns!

The first goal was naturally to obtain the item and complete the mission. She would then successfully obtain the Hypnotist potion formula from the Psychology Alchemists.

The second goal was to enhance her prestige, standing, and image by donating to academic research and the preservation of ancient relics. This was something most nobles and tycoons needed to do. Even if the donation wasn't today, Audrey would have to donate 3,000 pounds or more to the various charitable organizations. Therefore, she believed that her father, Earl Hall, wouldn't stop her from doing this with a few of her properties.

The third goal was to establish a foundation focused on researching and preserving ancient relics, making it easier for her to come into contact with valuable historical records or mysterious items. Audrey didn't need to do anything personally. All she needed to do was sit at home and receive items that might benefit her. It was equivalent to using 10,000 pounds to leverage even more money and establish her own "faction."

Of course, if Michele Deuth hadn't accepted this exchange, then she had other plans. The kingdom's Higher Education Commission included a member of the Hall family, as well as noble friends she knew. As long as the associate professor had anything he needed, she was certain that she had the means to satisfy him.

However, Audrey didn't like such methods. She had a nagging feeling that they were underhanded and would harm the public interest.

After discussing the matter, Audrey stayed for a little longer and chatted idly for about fifteen minutes. This kept the visit from ending too sharply or abruptly.

Afterward, she left Michele's house and rode her carriage back to the Hall family's villa in Stoen City.

Past eight in the evening, she received the Sauron royalty's helmet and the notebook from the Twenty Years' War.

Audrey wore a pair of white-silk gloves and sat before her desk with keen interest. She placed the helmet to the side as she began flipping through the contents of the notebook.

She discovered that the entries were sparse. The early entries described how the knight stationed on the ancient elven island had learned to brew Sonia blood wine, pursued women, and passed the tedious days. The later entries covered the Twenty Years' War. They mainly included his curses against the Feysacians, complaints about his companions, and thoughts about his resolve to stand fast. They also included a final account of the initial fall of Sonia Island.

Apart from the grammatical habits being similar to Dragonese, there aren't any serious problems. Nor can I find any hidden clues... Audrey frowned as she closed the notebook.

She had used methods derived from mysticism to do her checks, but they were to no avail.

This made her unwilling to waste time as she planned to turn it over to the Psychology Alchemists.

As her thoughts raced, she suddenly had a new idea.

Mr. World and Mr. Hanged Man often consider problems from different angles to provide suggestions. Should I learn from them?

Well... Looking at it from another angle, if the notebook's contents aren't unusual, could the physical object itself be what the Psychology Alchemists are after?

What's so special about it? I didn't discover anything... It belongs to a knight who's accustomed to using Dragonese. That knight must've experienced something... Divination! Yes, divination! Perhaps I can find the knight's final location using divination with the help of the notebook. And this might be related to a dragon!

Since it's a dragon, there's a high chance that it's a mind dragon, a representative of the Spectator pathway. Clues involving it would indeed be something the Psychology Alchemists would pay close attention to...

Audrey, that's some nice thinking!

Audrey's eyes turned crystal clear as it seemed to hide a clear resplendence.

She couldn't help turning her head to look at the golden retriever sitting by the side.

Susie glanced at her owner and barked.

"Audrey, do you want me to praise you?"

"No, there's no need..." Audrey turned her head back, feeling a little embarrassed.

Then, she discovered an important question. She wasn't able to divine things herself, or it could be said that the revelation that she received from divination would be highly inaccurate!

There's no way to confirm it... No, I can seek Mr. Fool's help! A secret deed ritual? It can only be performed on my body, not on an external object... Artificial sleepwalking? It's similar to a secret deed ritual, so that wouldn't work either... Sacrifice it to Mr. Fool and have "Him" perform the divination before bestowing it back upon me? No, that won't work. It would be rather disrespectful. "He" isn't my father or teacher, but a true god. Such a method is far too casual and somewhat sacrilegious... Audrey's thoughts gradually shifted from Mr. Fool to divination.

Although she was poor at divination, she had a certain level of understanding in the relevant knowledge. She soon locked onto a particular method of divination.

It involved drawing upon the power of a third party--an unknown or mysterious existence--through a particular ritual. The classic example of such a method was magic mirror divination!

Yes... It's indeed very dangerous, but the danger stems from the possibility that the target might be malicious or capable of making a person break down instantly. But I don't have such worries. I can seek Mr. Fool's help! Audrey blinked and suppressed her excitement as she said to the golden retriever, "Susie, guard the door outside. I'm going to use a mysticism technique to study this notebook."

"Didn't you use those methods already?" Susie asked, puzzled.

She's getting harder to fool... Audrey's eyes darted around as she said confidently, "I plan on using magic mirror divination.

"Don't worry. I'll make the plea to a safe existence."

"Alright." Susie determined that Audrey was speaking the truth.

She took a few steps before turning around to exhort her.

"Audrey, you need to be careful about being possessed by a mysterious existence."

"I know," Audrey replied without feeling worried at all.

From her point of view, if Mr. Fool really wanted to do something to her, he had countless opportunities in the past. There was no reason for him to wait until this day.

After Susie opened the door, went out, and closed it behind her, Audrey sat at her desk and recited The Fool's honorific name to request permission to use magic mirror divination.

After a while, Klein, who was on the Future, went to the bathroom and went above the gray fog. There, he heard Miss Justice's prayer.

That works? That's right. As a mysterious and unknown existence, I can serve as the third-party power in a magic mirror divination... Amused, Klein granted her request.

Audrey immediately picked up the notebook, sat in front of the dresser, and lit a candle while facing the mirror.

1. Author's Note: One are is 100 square meters. In the late Victorian era, land in the suburbs was approximately 100-130 pounds per acre. Converting to ares, it's about 2 pounds and 10 shillings to 3 pounds and 5 shillings per are. Well, that's excluding London. Of course, this might not be too accurate. I did an inference from the overall cost of the land purchase and building a house and subtracted the cost of building a house from the information from that era.

Chapter 643: Klein's Version of "Magic Mirror Divination"

With the curtains tightly drawn and all the lights in the room extinguished, Audrey completed the preparations required for magic mirror divination.

Of course, she didn't choose a particular time, as there was no need to go to such trouble when getting Mr. Fool's help.

Looking at the candles and their reflected light, as well as her own reflection, Audrey picked up a bottle of extract with excitement and a touch of unease. She dripped a few drops onto the dim flame.

As the mild and refreshing fragrances filled her nose, for some inexplicable reason, Audrey recalled the times when she was still a mysticism enthusiast.

In the beginning, she had made all kinds of mistakes. At critical moments during rituals, she would realize that she had forgotten to prepare the essential oils and extracts that would please the deity. All she could do was make do with perfume, and her attempts naturally failed.

In fact, according to the information Mr. Fool shared, "He" would still respond even if I used perfume... Audrey exhaled softly and used Cogitation to calm herself.

She knew that her uncontrolled thoughts weren't ordinary behavior coming from her. The tiny bit of anticipation and nervousness had been magnified by Lie!

After making sure she was in an optimal state of mind, Audrey clasped her hands and pressed them to her mouth and nose as if in prayer. She sincerely chanted softly, "The Fool that does not belong to this era.

"The mysterious ruler above the gray fog.

"The King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck."

The secret incantation echoed softly seven times, and Audrey sensed something indescribable lurking in the surrounding darkness, like an undercurrent hidden beneath the water's surface.

Looking up, Audrey extended her right hand, passed it through the still candle flame, and stroked the mirror's surface from top to bottom.

At this point, the magic mirror divination was basically done. If the mysterious and unknown existence's interest was aroused, it would respond through the mirror.

Above the gray fog, in the ancient and magnificent palace, Klein watched the crimson star representing Miss Justice expand and contract, rippling with an illusory glow. Finally, it became tinged with a serenity so deep that it was nearly black and condensed into a mirror-sized circular halo that seemed to lead to the outside world.

It's different from past rituals. Interesting... Klein leaned back into his chair and extended his spirituality to make contact with the black circular hole.

Silently, his vision changed. The long bronze table and crimson star overlapped with the spacious candlelit room before becoming distinct once again.

At that moment, Klein felt that a mirror had become an extension of his body or eye. It allowed him to clearly see and interfere with the real world despite being above the gray fog.

Yes, clearly!

All the objects in Klein's vision no longer looked blurry as they appeared distinctly in his eyes!

His gaze suddenly froze for two seconds.

In front of him was a lady in a white, gold-trimmed dress. Her long blonde hair was casually tied up, flowing down in smooth, lustrous waves. Her emerald eyes reflected a somewhat pale flame, appearing as deep as the ocean and as pure as gemstones. Her features and the contours of her face came together in stunning beauty, and her bearing was elegant, clear, and pure.

Klein shifted his gaze away as he felt guilty for some inexplicable reason.

I nearly mistook her for a rather high-Sequence Demoness. Thankfully, I remembered in time that Miss Justice is a Beyonder of the Spectator pathway. It's impossible for her to become a Demoness...

This extract smells quite good. Very distinctive... It has a subtle quality to it. Is that the mystical item made from the Faceless Beyonder characteristic? Miss Justice had it made into a necklace... Klein's gaze landed on the mystical item disguised as a diamond necklace.

Then, he heard Miss Justice say with utmost anticipation, "Mirror, Mirror, please tell me the location of the owner of this notebook."

Audrey knew that she was asking Mr. Fool, but "Mirror, Mirror" were words she had always wanted to recite from the stories she had heard from a young age. She finally had a chance to use it in practice.

My past failures don't count! Yes! Audrey nodded inwardly.

Klein instantly looked at the black notebook placed between Miss Justice and the candle. He discovered that his spirituality could easily spread out with the help of the mirror. He could "hold" the divination medium as though he was using his hand.

Above the gray fog, he quickly wrote the corresponding divination statement.

"The location of the original owner of the notebook."

With one "hand" holding the notebook and another hand holding the piece of paper, Klein recited as he leaned back in his chair. With the help of Cogitation, he quickly fell into a deep sleep.

Audrey looked at the mirror intently with her emerald eyes, awaiting Mr. Fool's reply.

After a few seconds, she saw ripples being produced on the mirror's surface.

It worked! Magic mirror divination really worked! Audrey's eyes widened as they reflected the scenes flashing inside the mirror.

It was a view of a village from above!

The scene zoomed in as the dragon drawings on the various buildings gradually turned clear.

A cathedral quickly occupied the mirror in its entirety before being replaced by the cemetery attached to it.

Finally, the scene was fixed on a tombstone in a corner. On it were words that had turned blurry as a result of the elements. The only thing that could still be discerned was the name, "Lindelira."

At that moment, the mirror suddenly darkened before restoring to normal. It reflected Audrey and the candle in front of her once again.

Isn't this the village with the custom of dragon worship? The knight who wrote the notebook was named Lindelira and came from that village. Did he return to his hometown after the defeat in the Twenty Years' War and remain there until his death? Or was his corpse sent home? Hmm... That village is in East Chester County. Stoen City is also part of East Chester County, so it makes perfect sense that the notebook Associate Professor Michele obtained came from there... There really is a mind dragon living in the sea of collective subconscious there... Enlightened, Audrey thanked Mr. Fool and ended the magic mirror divination.

Under the candlelight's illumination, she stared at the notebook for a moment before deciding to hand it over. She wanted to know what the Psychology Alchemists would divine or figure out.

At the very least, I currently lack the strength to make contact with that mind dragon. That's all I can do... Besides, even if the Psychology Alchemists discover something and benefit from it, once I gradually rise through the organization's ranks, some of it will belong to me~ Audrey's mood quickly brightened again.

Above the gray fog, Klein rapped the corner of the long bronze table. With Miss Justice's description, he made his judgment.

The notebook and the village had something to do with a dragon, and dragons often implied treasure!

What a pity. Miss Justice isn't strong enough; otherwise, I would've encouraged her to explore it and pray to the "Sea God" if anything happened, while I supported her through various means. Yes, it's too dangerous for her. It can only be set aside for now... If the Psychology Alchemists don't discover anything, perhaps we can attempt it in the future... Klein suppressed his regret as he recalled the experience of the magic mirror divination.

This kind of divination is extremely advantageous for the third party. There's almost no price to pay for connecting to the real world. If I so wished, I could've torn my way out of the mirror in my Spirit Body form! But for the user, it's indeed dangerous. They are exposed to the "gaze" of an unknown existence without any protection. From there, they can be possessed, controlled, cursed, or corrupted... Klein sighed inwardly.

He wasn't worried that Miss Justice would abuse magic mirror divination, as she had the best and safest target to pray to. There was no need for her to seek anyone else.

And that would be me! Klein retracted his finger and sat at the end of the long bronze table and waited in silence for twenty to thirty seconds.

Soon, he returned to the real world. He didn't stay too long since he was on the Future. There was someone watching him from the dark.

After leaving the bathroom and putting away Azik's copper whistle and Will Auceptin's paper crane, Klein glanced at the crimson moon which had been shrouded by clouds as he pondered silently for a moment.

He put on his half top hat, pulled open the door, and went into the corridor.

After taking a few steps forward, Klein deliberately slowed his footsteps and glanced at the first room on the left through the corner of his eye.

Based on his judgment, the mysterious pair of eyes that were silently observing him and the deck during the day was in that room.

Klein walked slower and slower as he nearly stopped in front of the door.

He didn't hide the extension of his left palm as he reached for the handle when scenes naturally surfaced in his mind.

Not far behind the door was a clothes rack with nothing hanging on it.

Faint starlight scattered across the clean floorboards. There was a sense of solitude and serenity that lacked the aura of humans.

The windows had opened at some point in time as the sea breeze blew in from the outside, lifting the curtains which weren't too heavy.

There's no one there? Klein had planned on probing the area, but he immediately retracted his left palm and walked towards the stairs as though nothing had happened.

When he arrived on deck, Klein strolled along the starboard side in the cold night breeze, wearing a cold and casual expression as though he had merely come out for some air.

Suddenly, he saw a man sitting ahead of him. The man was wearing dungarees and a white shirt.

Frank Lee? Klein continued approaching without stopping.

The man sensed something as he turned his body halfway around to look at the person approaching.

He was none other than Poison Expert Frank Lee, but his usual smile was gone. Blood-red liquid streamed from the corner of his mouth.

Klein pricked up his brows without saying a word.

Frank Lee suddenly raised his hands and grabbed a silver-scaled fish that wasn't struggling.

He said dejectedly, "It failed... Their lifespan is shorter than I imagined. It's impossible to breed them, even if they are planted in the ground..."

As he spoke, he raised the silver-scaled fish in his right hand and bit down on it.

That's good... Otherwise, I'd really be afraid of what kind of abominations you might create... So, you're drowning your sorrows in alcohol--no, fish? Klein silently heaved a sigh of relief.

Chapter 644: Laughter

Klein couldn't go against his own wishes to console Frank Lee, nor could he bring himself to tell Frank Lee that not being able to breed red wine fish was a good thing. All he could do was pretend that this was a trivial problem and not provide a response.

He took two diagonal steps around Frank and went to the starboard rail before gazing out over the undulating sea.

By then, the clouds high in the sky had thinned. The crimson moon's moonlight brightened up the rather dark night.

In that environment, Klein could make out the nearby scenery. Dark clouds hung low, and he saw a hurricane raging across an unknown swath of sea.

Under the illumination of the silver lightning, fine rain danced amidst the wind, forming a scene that resembled the dawn of the apocalypse.

Such a terrifying disaster was only a few nautical miles away from the Future, but the Future didn't seem affected. Only the wind had become a little stronger.

This is what a safe sea route means--advancing alongside a storm... Without a good navigator, ordinary sailing could send a ship straight into disaster... Klein turned his head to look in the direction the ship was heading. He saw a faint glow shining through the fog. Visibility was extremely poor, making it difficult to tell with the naked eye whether they were in a safe or dangerous zone.

Such situations weren't obvious west of Oravi Island. But here, it was common once they took the secret sea route.

Klein was just about to retract his gaze when he saw a black, massive object seemingly hiding in the periphery of the storm!

A sea monster? He suddenly recalled some of the rumors he had heard at the different bars he had been to.

After straying from a safe sea route, it's very easy to encounter all kinds of monsters. Some are enormous and possess tremendous offensive power. Once they surface, they can swallow a ship whole.

The Future didn't stop advancing. Before long, it closed the gap between it and the gloomy but massive object.

Klein finally managed to discern the entity's appearance and discovered that it wasn't a sea monster, but a gigantic sailboat which was even larger than the Future.

The sailboat was nearly two hundred meters long. With its bow and stern raised high, it appeared like a crescent.

Its hull was black. The cannons were arranged along its side in three somewhat uneven rows, one above another. Apart from the masts, the structures rising from the deck were similarly tall and massive, at least as high as a five-story building.

The ship's strangest feature was that it had only one sail, upon which was the image of a black tomb.

"The Death Announcer..." A heavy voice sounded in Klein's ears. At some point, Frank Lee had thrown away the silver-scaled fish whose blood had been replaced by red wine and come to his side.

The first mate who had a bounty of 7,000 pounds held his head high as his muscles remained tense. It was as though an attack would be launched anytime at the slightest movement of the gigantic sailboat.

Death Announcer? Klein was first taken aback before he recalled what the name represented.

It was one of the most legendary and infamous ships on the Five Seas!

It was the flagship of the King of Immortality Agalito!

We actually encountered one of the Four Kings... Klein clicked his tongue inwardly as he involuntarily went on high alert.

However, he maintained a superficial level of indifference and calm as he continued looking at the Death Announcer.

Recalling how the second mate of the Death Announcer, Slaughterer Kircheis, who had a bounty of 9,500 pounds, had recently appeared in Nas and warned him, Klein understood. He was no longer puzzled as to why he would bump into the Death Announcer not far from the Gargas Archipelago.

Setting that question aside, he recalled even more rumors, several of which had been confirmed as accurate.

King of Immortality Agalito is a middle-aged man who's so pale that he's on the brink of rotting at any moment.

The bounty placed on him by a single country reached as high as 100,000 pounds.

His former enemies are all dead, regardless of whether they were pirates, adventurers, or members of a naval fleet. Only the other three members of the Four Kings remained alive.

He has never entered into direct combat with an official demigod. He is extremely restrained in such matters.

No one really knows his exact Sequence. No one knows what his Beyonder powers are.

He's cruel by nature and enjoys killing. He can launch an attack on any person or any ship at random.

He likes finding excuses to break his promises. In addition to being called the King of Immortality, he is often nicknamed "The Untrustworthy."

Thankfully, I'm on the Future. The King of Immortality will likely give Admiral of Stars a free pass. After all, she's one of the seven pirate admirals... No, the Four Kings and the Pirate Admirals can ordinarily cooperate or oppose one another, except during the pirate convention organized by the King of the Five Seas... Given the King of Immortality's character, he might leave us no chance of survival! Just as this thought flashed through Klein's mind, he heard a deep, rumbling horn.

Whoosh!

The sailors resting in the cabins jolted awake. Without even dressing, they ran to the cannons on deck and prepared for battle. The Future instantly went from peaceful to combat-ready.

Klein turned his head and looked up. He saw that the captain's windows were already open. Cattleya wore the usual black dress as she stood there and observed the Death Announcer.

She wasn't wearing the thick glasses, and her pitch-black eyes were tainted with some purple, making them mysterious and deep.

Indeed, she's also worried about a sudden attack from the King of Immortality... Klein retracted his gaze and looked at the Death Announcer with its stern raised high.

At that moment, the two ships had just passed each other by. The pirates could see their counterparts on the other ship.

The pirates aboard the other ship were also looking over. Silent and motionless as statues, as though devoid of all emotion, they brandished their knives and trained their guns on the Future in an extremely provocative display.

At that moment, a single spark would have been enough to ignite the tension and start a battle.

Finally, the Death Announcer didn't take any action. It remained silently anchored on the spot, "watching" the Future pass it by before it distanced itself.

Phew... At Klein's side, Frank Lee didn't conceal his exhalation.

He smiled and said to Klein, "Heh heh, there have always been many rumors about the King of Immortality's strength. Some say he's truly a demigod, while others claim he's only a Sequence 5 who can reach the level of Sequence 4 thanks to the Death Announcer. Regardless, he has lived for a very long time. Uh... Tell me, do you think crossbreeding him with my fish would improve their survivability?"

Frank suddenly had an idea.

First, you need to capture the King of Immortality, or else make him interested in your fish... Klein lampooned inwardly before saying calmly, "You can discuss it with him."

Frank Lee was taken aback as he sighed, depressed.

"He won't agree to it. He will plant me into the soil."

Just as he said that, the Death Announcer which had fallen behind the Future suddenly turned its sails.

Shrill, malicious laughter rang out from several hundred meters away and swept over the Future.

"Hahaha!

"Hahaha!"

The laughter resounded without pause. It shifted from shrill to hoarse to chaotic, at times resembling ravings and at others singing. One after another, the sailors on deck fell to the ground. They desperately covered their ears, but could only struggle in pain.

A number of Beyonders began to produce fish scales on their bodies.

Klein was similarly affected. He felt that his brain was instantly injected with all kinds of thoughts. There were the good, the bad, the bright, and the dark.

They were abnormally disordered, combining with the ever-changing laughter to produce a swelling sensation that threatened to burst his brain.

Klein's facial expression distorted slightly as inconspicuous flesh tendrils began wriggling under his skin.

If he hadn't experienced the True Creator's ravings and Mr. Door's cries for help, or suffered similar torment each time he passed through the gray fog, building a certain degree of resistance, Klein would've ended up like Poison Expert Frank Lee--clutching his head and kneeling with a contorted expression as he resisted the pain brought by the terrifying laughter.

Klein noticed that some short orange hair was growing from Frank's face, making him seem to transform into a bear.

At that moment, the symbols and magical labels on the Future's walls, deck, and mast lit up. They became like a resplendent sea of stars in a moonless night.

The terrifying laughter, by turns shrill and hoarse, didn't weaken, but everyone instantly seemed to grow distant from one another. Sounds from farther away became hollow and illusory.

Klein's swelling head instantly found relief as he used the rest of his strength to look up.

Behind the open windows of the captain's cabin, dark traces had appeared on the Admiral of Stars's face. They looked ready to split apart at any moment and release something horrible.

At that moment, Cattleya's palms pressed against the windowsill as bits of starlight swirled around her, seemingly matching the Future which was on the resplendent sea.

Whoosh!

A squall stirred for no reason as the ship began its own adjustment. Despite the Future having so many of its sailors down, it accelerated instead of slowing. It quickly opened up a gap from the Death Announcer.

Klein looked at the room with the mysterious pair of eyes that observed the deck, and he discovered that the windows were rattling from the strong winds without any signs of abnormalities.

Whoosh!

Amid the strong winds, starlight descended to form a sheet of "floating ice" that dragged the Future into "rapid flight."

Finally, the Death Announcer vanished behind it. The terrifying laughter which could make a person lose control or go mad turned more illusory and distant.

Frank Lee lowered his hands and panted before barely recovering. Many of the sailors on the deck were still tumbling about and struggling in extreme pain, but their condition didn't worsen.

This ability of the King of Immortality is truly impressive. There's almost no way to defend against it... No wonder he's one of the Four Kings... Klein frowned slightly as he sighed.

Although he was experienced and rather knowledgeable, this was the first time he had been directly attacked by a demigod in the truest sense. He discovered that even Agalito, ranked last among the Four Kings, made him feel weak and incapable of resistance.

With Agalito's character, will he make the Death Announcer pursue us? Although there's a high chance he would wait for Slaughterer Kircheis to return, that possibility cannot be excluded... If he catches up, I'll pray to myself and use the Sea God Scepter to give him a maelstrom and a thunderstorm to wipe him out... After making the decision, he once again looked at Cattleya.

The pirate admiral's expression was already ghastly pale. However, there were no longer any signs of darkness. The starlight that twirled around her slowly extinguished one after another.

Chapter 645: Calming Method

After the surrounding astral glow completely vanished, Cattleya lowered her head and said to the slowly recovering Frank Lee, "Calm them down."

She was referring to the sailors who were still struggling in pain.

Just as she said that, Cattleya drew back as the windows closed.

At that instant, Klein vaguely saw a green vine grow upward, covering the Admiral of Stars in layers.

Since he sensed neither danger nor malevolence, he intuitively believed it was a mysticism technique Ma'am Hermit used to treat her injuries and restore her energy.

A power from her Beyonder pathway? Or is it from some mystical item she possesses? She began treating herself without any concern, leaving Frank to deal with the chaos on the ship. Isn't she afraid the Death Announcer will catch up to us? How far did the Future "fly" in that last burst? Klein stopped craning his neck and turned to look at Frank Lee.

This first mate, who was both a poison expert and a capable doctor, had already retrieved a coiled soft tube from the pocket of his dungarees. It was connected to a small glass bottle, and on the other side was an embedded thin and sharp needle.

"A sedative I concocted," Frank Lee said with a forced smile while still having lingering fear.

I've bought something similar before. They were all given to Zombie Maric... Klein surveyed the area and said, "This might not be enough."

"No, it isn't for them. I need an assistant. They'll be helped by beer with a sedative effect. Haha, they often get rowdy when they're drunk, so I've added the necessary ingredients to most of the barrels of alcohol," Frank explained casually.

Spiking their drinks without telling anyone... The corner of Klein's mouth nearly twitched.

At that moment, he had a deep appreciation for the aptness of the nickname "Poison Expert."

Simply because he found the pirates rowdy when drunk, Frank had added a modified sedative to most of the alcoholic beverages without any sense of guilt or shame. It was as though he was doing something very ordinary.

This fellow is genuinely straightforward and warm in some respects, but in others, he's more terrifying than a devil because he doesn't believe anything he does is evil... How did the Church of the Earth Mother produce such a mad scientist? Klein controlled his expression and followed Frank Lee at a distance until they found Boatswain Nina writhing in the shadows by the bulkhead.

This pirate with an exaggerated figure was slumped there, writhing in pain. She kept scratching the deck and producing ear-piercing sounds as bloody marks were left behind.

Just watching the scene made Klein feel pain in his fingers.

"Gehrman, help me press her down to prevent her from struggling." Frank raised the needle and tube in his hand.

Klein didn't object to it, but he didn't say a word. He calmly crouched by the side and pressed down on Nina's shoulders.

He felt his hands slip the moment he touched her, finding it difficult to grab her. It was as though she wasn't a lady but a gigantic fish covered in slippery scales.

Klein instantly corrected his actions. Using a Clown's precise control, he grabbed Nina firmly by the shoulders.

However, Nina's struggles were surprisingly powerful, far stronger than Klein. Soon, his fingers began to ache, and he could barely hold on.

As expected of a Sequence 7 Beyonder from the Sailor pathway, while I'm not a Beyonder who excels in physical strength... If not for the need to feed it, I could activate Creeping Hunger, switch to Steel Maveti's soul, and use a Zombie's strength, which would be the most suitable response... As these thoughts ran through his mind, Klein saw Frank approach, crouch down, and press his knee against Nina's back.

His muscles bulged as he quickly stopped Nina's struggles.

A Beyonder from the Planter pathway possesses considerable physical strength as well... However, Mr. Frank Lee, you'll probably have a hard time finding a girlfriend if you treat a lady so roughly... Of course, you definitely don't mind. You could always grow your child in the soil... Klein lampooned inwardly as he watched Frank Lee insert the needle into the back of Nina's hand.

As the small bottle's sedative was injected, Nina stopped her attempts at struggling. Klein released his hands and stood up.

After a few seconds, Nina combed her hair and rolled to her feet. She grumbled at Frank Lee, "Why must you always be so rough like a bear? Can't you try a different method?"

As she spoke, she stretched her arms without concealing the pained expression on her face.

Unlike during her earlier dive, she was now wearing a linen shirt and a brown coat. She looked no different from an ordinary pirate.

Frank wasn't fazed by Nina's complaints as he asked, puzzled, "How was I rough?

"Alright, let's not argue on this topic. Let's help them first.

"Get the beer barrel out. Let's work together to make them drink.

"Gehrman, you don't mind participating in this, right?"

Klein shot a glance at the sailors on the deck. After contemplating for a few seconds, he asked, "The goal is to let them calm down?"

"That's right." Frank Lee nodded heavily.

"Can I simply knock them unconscious?" Klein asked calmly.

This has almost the same effect as sedative-laced beer, and it's more efficient... he added inwardly.

Nina turned her head in surprise and was momentarily stunned speechless.

Frank Lee thought seriously for a moment.

"Okay."

"Alright." Klein walked to a cutlass he had long noticed, picked it up, and struck its owner with the dull edge.

Bang!

Through his precise control, the pirate who was rolling in pain calmed down and fell unconscious.

Nina's expression froze for a second before she recovered.

She then slowed as she passed Klein. Suppressing her laughter, she said, "I've heard rumors about you, but I never expected you to be more outrageous than the rumors. Usually, it's the other way around.

"Yes... Your way of thinking is very, very unusual. It's completely different from everyone else's and closer to Frank's. That's probably why he came to regard you as a friend so quickly."

No, there are differences even among lunatics. Gehrman Sparrow hasn't reached Frank's level yet... Klein observed silently and replied inwardly.

Ignoring Nina, he carried the cutlass across the deck, knocking the sailors unconscious so they could wake naturally.

Frank wanted to do the same, but abandoned the rash idea when Nina asked whether he could guarantee that he would only knock them unconscious rather than kill them. He entered the cabin, brought out a barrel of beer, and proceeded with his original plan.

Bam! Bam! Bam!

Klein struck as he walked all the way to the bow. At that moment, an elder in his fifties with a pointed bonnet had gradually come around.

When he saw the cold and crazy adventurer approach, he hurriedly tried to stand up and blurted out, "No, I don't need it!

"I'm already fine. I'm fine!"

There's no need to explain. It's very clear... Klein held back his amusement and turned for the bow.

At that moment, the elder introduced himself, "I'm the navigator of the Future, Ottolov."

Navigator? Klein turned his head and discovered several books dropped around Ottolov. They were either spread on the ground face up or down, flat or sideways.

"Haha, I fell from above and brought them with me. They nearly went mad," Ottolov explained.

Klein shifted his gaze to Ottolov's eyes and discovered a deep-sea hue in their blue depths.

It's not the pair of eyes that were observing me in the day... But this feeling is somewhat similar to the Admiral of Stars... They are of the same pathway? Klein retracted his gaze and watched Nina and Frank Lee calm the other sailors.

Just as he was about to look back to see if the Death Announcer had continued its pursuit, Ottolov suddenly shouted, "Be careful!"

Whoosh!

A huge wave struck the bow, causing the Future to shake vigorously.

If not for his balancing ability as a Clown, Klein would've fallen like Frank had. And when the huge spray pattered down like rain, it drenched his half top hat and double-breasted frock coat.

There should be a steam iron here, right... A crazy adventurer washing his clothes doesn't violate his persona. It would be strange if he didn't wash them... I should've worn clothes in the style of the Rorsted Archipelago natives! At that moment, Klein felt a pang of heartache.

He saw a storm ahead with huge waves coming one after another as they surged high up like mountains. He could sense the crazy winds and the rumbling thunder.

W-we've reached the boundary of the safe sea route? That "flight" might've allowed us to shake off the Death Announcer, but it also sent the ship off course? Klein watched as Ottolov, Nina, Frank, and Bloodless Heath Doyle, who had appeared at some point, roused the sailors and took their positions. They frantically set about steering the Future.

Through their efforts, the Future changed direction in time, cut through the gigantic waves, and dodged the bolts of lightning before returning to the safe sea route.

After everything calmed down, Klein released the Sea God domain charm and breathed a sigh of relief.

Looking back, he saw no sign of the Death Announcer pursuing them. He finally relaxed, reflecting that there had been nothing peaceful about the night.

After surveying the pirates as they rubbed their heads or gasped for breath, exhausted and on the verge of collapse, Klein left the deck with a faint sense of guilt. He entered the cabin and sighed inwardly.

We only recently left the Gargas Archipelago, and the ship was nearly destroyed. The search for mermaids really isn't going to be simple...

Following the staircase to the upper level, Klein walked past the captain's cabin and slowed down. He observed for a moment and saw green leaves covering the crack in the door. Everything was isolated.

He shifted his gaze and returned to his room. He considered praying to The Fool, going above the gray fog, and responding to the prayer to gain an overview of the nearby waters and search for traces of the Death Announcer. He wanted King of Immortality Agalito, who attacked others for no reason, to have a taste of his own medicine.

No, there are "eyes" watching. I should try my best not to do such things. Besides, it will only teach the King of Immortality a lesson and not seriously injure him. It's not worth the risk... I shouldn't let my anger get the better of my rationality... I'll think about it again after advancing successfully! Klein repressed his urge.

Chapter 646: Leymano's Spellbook

Backlund, outside the steam locomotive station.

Fors wore a finely checkered black veiled hat decorated with blue flowers and stood in the hall at the station entrance, awaiting her teacher, Dorian Gray.

The street drizzle and the cold drafts blowing through it made the female author tremble slightly. She felt that she had underestimated Backlund's spring.

I wonder what Xio is doing that makes her busy all year round. Sigh, she said that she wouldn't even get out of bed when her father was still alive. Apart from going to the bathroom, both food and water would be served to her by her servants. Now, she's leaving early in the morning every day and only returning late at night regardless of the weather. She has completed one mission after another, capturing one fugitive after another. With this in mind, Fors couldn't help but admire Xio.

Last week, this female Sheriff had already paid off her debt and had even saved up 200 pounds!

I have to say, Sheriff is a Beyonder profession perfectly suited to bounty hunting. Of course, her choices are limited to Low-Sequence Beyonders... As Fors's thoughts wandered, her outward gaze suddenly caught sight of a familiar figure.

It was a man of medium build, and he was dressed in a black suit, the most popular attire in Loen. He wore a half top hat, and unusually, his shoulders appeared broad. They were broad in an almost exaggerated sense.

He was none other than Fors's teacher, one of the few remaining members of the Abraham family, Dorian Gray Abraham.

Fors was delighted as she immediately went over with her umbrella in hand.

With so many people on the street, she didn't approach him directly. However, their gazes met, and she saw him raise his clenched right fist toward the first button of his black suit.

This... means danger! Fors diverted her gaze without changing her expression. Smiling, she looked at a young gentleman behind Dorian and walked past him as though nothing had happened.

An ancient family possessed unimaginable experience in all kinds of matters, so Dorian Gray had long since taught Fors the signals and gestures to use in emergencies. The previous gesture had a very simple meaning: stay away!

The young man behind was somewhat taken aback to be stared at by the languid, mature lady. He instinctively adjusted his clothes and pressed down on his hat.

Just as he finished this series of actions, Fors had overtaken him and continued heading forward.

In the drizzle, Fors circled the area, boarded a rental carriage, and headed to the Hat Trick Inn at 22 Hope Street in Cherwood Borough. Dorian Gray had reserved a room there in advance.

Having spent so many years in mysticism circles, the experienced Fors calmly rented a neighboring room and stood by the window to observe the guests entering the inn.

Finally, she saw Dorian Gray get off a carriage and walk through the door.

Fors quickly turned around and came to the bend of the stairwell. She covertly observed her teacher being led to his room by an attendant.

After a while, she took off her hat and mussed her hair before going to Room 2016 and knocking.

Her plan was very simple. If her teacher's response wasn't problematic, she would enter the room to converse with him. If it was abnormal, she would pretend as though she had come to the wrong room or that she was a prostitute trying to get some business.

Creak. The door slowly opened as Dorian looked at his student in front of him before looking both ways down the corridor.

Then, he raised his right arm and spread his fingers to press on the second button of his suit.

This meant that he wasn't controlled and that the abnormality was over.

Fors let out a silent sigh of relief and instantly entered the room.

"Teacher, what happened just now?" she asked, feeling worried and concerned.

Dorian closed the door and smiled wryly.

"I saw someone familiar."

He sighed and added, "An enemy."

It was his former student who had later betrayed the Abraham family with Traveler Boulaya and nearly wiped out the upper echelons of the Abraham family.

According to what Dorian knew, his former student had already joined the Aurora Order and was likely one of the 22 Oracles.

"What did he do? Is he very strong?" Fors asked curiously.

Dorian fell silent for a few seconds and divulged a little.

"Lawrence, Laubero, Aulisa, and I belong to a particular organization. A few members betrayed it and dealt a heavy blow to it.

"That was one of the traitors."

He didn't describe it as a family, nor did he mention the conflict between bloodline members and apprentices, lest Fors become emotionally involved.

"How despicable!" Fors immediately recalled the kind Mrs. Aulisa and the friendly and amiable Mr. Lawrence.

"Alright, let's not chat about such unhappy matters." Dorian took out a piece of paper that had been folded a few times and handed it to Fors. "Here's the Astrologer potion formula. Your digestion of the Trickmaster and Apprentice potions has exceeded my expectations. This is the greatest surprise I've had in the past ten years."

"I specially joined a circus." Fors didn't hide the fact, and she even felt that it was worth flaunting.

As she spoke, she unfolded the piece of paper to read the Astrologer's potion formula.

At that moment, Dorian nodded in relief.

"I'm very sorry. Due to our previous losses, I'm unable to provide you with the main ingredients of the Astrologer potion. Treat obtaining them as a final test.

"However, I have prepared a gift for you."

As he spoke, he took out a palm-sized book from his inner left pocket. Its cover looked hard, and it was entirely bronze-green in color. It looked rather ancient.

The notebook contained three types of paper. The yellow parchment had the fewest pages, there were about ten pages of yellowish-brown goatskin, and the rest were ordinary white paper. Written on the cover in ancient Feysac were the words: "I came, I saw, I recorded."

Fors's gaze moved away from the Astrologer potion formula and onto the notebook. She recognized it to be the item left behind by Mr. Lawrence. It was the item she had specially traveled all the way to Pritz Harbor to hand to her teacher!

Dorian smiled.

"I believe you are no stranger to it."

After Fors nodded, he sighed.

"This is a rather powerful mystical item. It ranks among the top five similar items I've seen. That doesn't mean it's more powerful than everything ranked below it, but its negative effects are among the easiest to mitigate. Taken together, that makes it extremely valuable."

"What is its name? What negative side effects does it have?" Fors couldn't disguise her excitement and agitation.

Dorian caressed the notebook's cover and said, "It's called 'Leymano's Travels,' but we prefer calling it 'Leymano's Spellbook.'

"This spellbook not only gives you access to more kinds of Beyonder powers, but also allows you to record them.

"The power will materialize on a particular page for long-term storage. You can use it at any time, but remember that after each use, the corresponding page will become blank again and await your next recording." It sounds amazing. Fors felt as though she was dreaming.

I can record any Beyonder powers I see and use them once?

I-isn't this another kind of Shepherd? The Shepherd mentioned by Little Sun... Yes, it's only limited to a single prepared battle.

I wonder if I can record the Beyonder powers of a demigod. If it's possible, in a prepared battle, I'll be even stronger than a Shepherd!

Dorian seemed to read her mind as he explained in detail.

"You could say that. It corresponds to a Sequence 6 Scribe of the Apprentice pathway. Recording a Beyonder power above that Sequence isn't guaranteed to succeed. The greater the gap, the greater the chance of failure. Furthermore, the power's effects will be halved.

"According to the experience of past users, while the chance of successfully recording a Sequence 5 Beyonder power is considerably high, once it reaches Sequence 4, which is the level of a demigod, it becomes relatively difficult. There might not be a single instance of success within ten attempts. And I believe no demigod will use it again and again for you. If it's an enemy, they'd have killed you ten times over.

"Do you see these? The yellow parchment pages are specifically used to record Beyonder powers with godhood. There are a total of three pages, which is to say that even if you are very lucky, you can record at most three demigod Beyonder powers, and you can only use them each once."

Although it appears to have many restrictions that make it impossible to bring out the traveling notebook's full might, they aren't obstacles for two types of people: someone extremely lucky, or someone backed by a large organization or faction with a demigod willing to help... Fors instantly thought of asking Mr. Fool to display "His" powers so she could record them. However, she immediately found the thought sacrilegious and hurriedly apologized inwardly.

Afterward, she sincerely hoped that the supposedly strongest Ma'am Hermit in the Tarot Club would quickly become a demigod.

Dorian noticed that Fors's thoughts were wandering and continued, "The goatskin pages can record the Beyonder powers of Sequence 5 or 6. There are ten pages in total, and the recorded powers retain more than half their original effects, roughly 70 to 80%.

"The remaining pages can record Beyonder powers below Sequence 6. There are 25 pages in total, and the recorded powers are almost as effective as the originals, though still slightly inferior.

"Due to past events, the spellbook still has five pages with Beyonder powers left. The rest are blank. You can test them for yourself."

Dorian paused before saying solemnly, "Before giving it to you, I must warn you about its negative effects. Each time you use it, it will cause you to lose your way, and in a manner that leads you into danger. You have to draw a little blood and smear it evenly across the cover to reduce the effects.

"Remember, don't underestimate getting lost. You must resolve it as quickly as possible."

"Yes, Teacher," Fors replied seriously.

Dorian then handed Leymano's Travels to Fors.

"This is my gift."

Teacher is so kind... Fors pursed her lips and asked, "What's the name of the student who betrayed you? What does he look like?

"If there's a chance, I wish to seek vengeance on him for both you and Mr. Lawrence."

"No, don't think about it. You are much weaker than him. Back when he betrayed us, he was already a Scribe. Now, he might even be a Traveler," Dorian said solemnly. "However, you do need to remember him and avoid him. His name is Lewis Wien. I'll draw a picture of him later."

"Alright." Fors nodded.

On the Future, in the pirates' dining hall.

Klein met Cattleya who was exiting just as he walked in.

The lady was no longer as pale as the previous night. With a pair of thick glasses, she calmly said, "Make your preparations. We will be arriving in those waters in a while."

That fast? How far exactly did we fly last night? Klein was taken aback.

Chapter 647: Sea of Ruins

Although Gehrman Sparrow showed nothing on his face, Cattleya seemed to notice his surprise. She simply explained, "It's a kind of flight that makes it impossible for the Death Announcer to continue pursuing us."

As she said this, she noticed that Gehrman Sparrow had changed his entire outfit. He wore a round-necked shirt, a brown jacket, pantaloons, and a dark-colored cap. He no longer looked like an adventurer, instead resembling a native of the City of Generosity, Bayam.

According to Nina, his clothes were drenched by a huge wave last night... Does he own only one decent set of clothes? Cattleya remembered the incident and understood.

She wasn't surprised. She even felt that it suited Gehrman Sparrow's madness. He owned only one decent set of clothes and spent the rest of his money on mystical items, Beyonder weapons, and spirituality charms--all for the sole purpose of increasing his strength.

No wonder the King of Immortality gave up. He merely attacked a passing ship. There wasn't enough hatred to drive him to pursue us all the way... Yes, even against the Four Kings, senior pirate admirals still have some chance of escaping...

We're almost there... Man, I haven't finished digesting the potion...

However, acting as a crazy adventurer yesterday seemed rather effective. The crew of the Future responded very spontaneously and enthusiastically. I should completely digest the potion in two or three days. And even after we enter the dangerous waters, finding mermaids won't be that simple. I should have enough time... Klein nodded and suppressed his urge to ask. He planned to walk past Admiral of Stars and head toward his usual spot for breakfast.

At that moment, he saw a pirate rush in carrying a blue fish that was alive and thrashing. The pirate went straight to a corner.

Seated there was the Future's second mate, Heath Doyle. His face was so pale as to be nearly translucent, and the bridge of his nose looked oddly out of proportion.

Thud!

The fish which was nearly a meter long was placed in front of Bloodless.

Heath Doyle extended his hands and pressed down on the fish. He bent his body bit by bit as he inched his head downwards, pressing his face to the scales as though he was kissing it.

Suddenly, the fish stiffened and, like wax meeting fire, melted rapidly and turned into a disgusting pool of flesh and blood.

The flesh and blood liquefied, surging into Heath Doyle's mouth and flowing over his skin.

Amid the horrific squirming, the entire fish--bones, flesh, and blood--vanished. Nothing remained, and Heath Doyle's face was perfectly clean. Only his lips were left bright red, like a blooming rose.

Rose Bishop... The name of the Sequence surfaced in Klein's mind.

Cattleya, who was beside him, also saw the scene as she nudged her glasses.

"Every Rose Bishop needs to replenish themselves with sufficient flesh and blood. That way, they can fully display their Beyonder powers and avoid losing control after being injured in an intense battle.

Her lips curled slightly as she added, "However, the lunatics from the Aurora Order have a preference for human flesh and blood. In fact, substitutes work just as well."

From the looks of it, this Bloodless, with a bounty of 7,600 pounds, is truly lucky. For one thing, he was fortunate enough to become a Sequence 6 in a single step without suffering any abnormalities or losing control. For another, he joined Admiral of Stars's pirate crew. Without the secret knowledge possessed by this lady pursued by knowledge, he might've turned into a monster craving human flesh and blood sooner or later, even if he never listened to the True Creator's voice... Klein sighed silently.

He was increasingly convinced that the Secrets Suppliant pathway, which led to Shepherd, was the Beyonder pathway most prone to losing control and going mad. Nothing else came close; even the Abyss pathway, which represented evil, fell slightly short.

Klein retracted his gaze, and just as he was about to take a step forward, the entire ship jolted.

In an instant, the scene ahead of the Future naturally surfaced in Klein's mind.

A gigantic crack split the blue sea, and an immense volume of seawater plummeted like a waterfall into the bottomless darkness!

This scene was magnificent and mystical. It made one wonder whether they were still on Earth.

Whoosh!

The Future wasn't able to stop in time as it rushed to the edge and quickly plummeted.

Bang! Bang! Bang!

All the pirates in the dining hall flew up and slammed into the ceiling. Toast, white bread, butter, margarine, beer, and roasted fish flew wildly through the air.

Klein also lost his balance as he couldn't help but be thrown toward the ceiling.

He extended his palm in time and pushed against the ceiling, swiftly adjusting his posture with an acrobatic maneuver so that he appeared less pathetic.

Not far away, Nina displayed her remarkable balance, using the ceiling to steady herself. Whether intentionally or not, she kicked Frank, sending the Poison Expert flying diagonally into a beer barrel and drenching him in pale yellow liquid.

At that moment, Cattleya was the calmest and least affected. Stars swirled around her as radiance coalesced beneath her feet, causing her to float in midair, unaffected by the sudden plunge.

Apart from her, Bloodless was least disheveled. At some point in time, this Rose Bishop had blended into the shadows and disappeared.

With the Future about to plummet into the bottomless crevice, a plume of water surged up suddenly!

It caught the ship and hurled it high into the air toward the opposite edge.

After a moment in the air, Klein felt the Future land steadily on the sea's surface. Once again, the Sea God domain charm clutched tightly in his right hand went unused.

At that moment, the sunlight outside the window shone inside, illuminating the mess inside the dining hall.

Klein, who had long regained his balance, briskly walked to the window and saw that the endless sea seemed to be burning with golden flames. It appeared to have been noon the entire time.

It was only morning moments ago!

Klein looked up and narrowed his eyes to see that the sky was filled with sunlight. There were no clouds nor the sun, just a swath of golden rays.

No wonder Arrodes described this place as no longer an ordinary ocean, but a relic of a war between the gods.

Sweeping his gaze around, he discovered nearby ruins ahead and off to one side.

The ruins were mostly submerged. Gray rocks and stone columns protruded from the surface, forming a peak crowned by a dome-like structure. Judging from its curvature, it had once enclosed a vast space.

The ruins had clearly been massive in their prime. Through the clear water, Klein saw their foundations extend endlessly toward the seabed.

"These are extremely dangerous waters." At some point in time, Cattleya came beside him.

Klein turned his head over and waited for her to continue.

Cattleya's gaze was cast forward as she said somewhat wistfully, "I haven't been here many times. Furthermore, all my visits happened a long time ago."

"My," not "our"... That means she wasn't with the Future's crew... Was it back when she was Queen Mystic's subordinate? Klein keenly noticed Admiral of Stars's choice of words and formed a hypothesis.

Cattleya didn't turn her head as she looked at the sky and sea which were covered in golden flames.

"No one knows where these waters end, nor how vast they are.

"Do you know what's most dangerous about this place?" I should've gotten Arrodes to give me a more detailed explanation... Klein honestly shook his head.

Previously, he had nearly been caught by a demigod of the True Creator. He had only survived thanks to the Die of Probability; therefore, in the past two months, he didn't dare bring the radio transceiver back to the real world. He was afraid that the True Creator, who was closely watching the region, would sense it. Hence, he didn't have an opportunity to contact Arrodes.

His original plan had been to seek Will Auceptin's help through the paper crane once he approached the dangerous waters, so he could understand the hazards of the environment. To his surprise, they had encountered King of Immortality Agalito, and the Future had arrived at its destination before giving him any time to prepare.

Behind the thick glasses, Cattleya's black eyes with a purple hue glazed over for a moment.

"Many ruins float here, along with all kinds of mutated monsters. Among them are numerous demigods who have lost control and ancient evil creatures.

"That isn't the most dangerous part. If that were all, these waters would've become a hunting ground for the seven Churches, a treasure trove where they could obtain High-Sequence ingredients or items. Of course, many monsters and evil creatures here have lost control, but some of them might not be real. They can kill us, yet might leave nothing behind when killed."

Upon hearing this, Klein made the connection to the Dragon of Imagination, Ankewelt.

This dragon king could conjure anything through his imagination!

Could it be that these waters were where the ancient gods of the Second Epoch fought? Klein nearly frowned.

Cattleya continued in an ethereal voice, "Here, you can never guess what danger you'll encounter next. Perhaps you'll melt while approaching a ruin and turn into a waxlike monster. Perhaps a slight deviation from the explored sea routes will cause you to turn to stone amid a storm and shatter into pieces.

"To Beyonders at our level, that is the greatest danger."

Klein turned his head slightly and asked pointedly, "What about Beyonders above our level?"

Cattleya sighed and smiled.

"The waters here are filled with a voice that shouldn't be heard.

"The higher the Sequence, the easier it is to hear it. Therefore, most demigods who dare explore these waters have ended up with problems. They either went mad or lost control, ending up lost here forever."

No wonder the seven Churches don't send their experts here to reap the harvest... Most of them ended up in trouble... Does that mean a small number of demigods can survive here? Understanding dawned on Klein as he looked out the window again.

At that moment, the Future had closed in on the ruins.

Suddenly, everyone heard loud, distinct panting!

Chapter 648: Noon and Night

Whoosh! Gasp!

The loud panting entered Klein's ears in a slow, steady rhythm. A chill ran down his spine, filling him with an inexplicable sense of horror, yet he sensed no premonition of danger.

He wasn't the only one. Cattleya, Frank Lee, and the other pirates also heard the panting. They turned their heads, looked outside, raised their weapons, or went on high alert, displaying their extensive experience.

After trying to discern its source, Klein discovered that the intense panting came from the ruins ahead, from somewhere amid the peak formed by rocks and stone columns.

At that moment, Bloodless Heath Doyle floated out of the shadows. He clasped his head and softly grunted in pain.

"There's a corpse... There's a corpse there!"

A corpse? One that pants loudly? Klein's thoughts raced. Cattleya had subconsciously removed her thick glasses to look toward the ruins, and her expression suddenly turned solemn. She turned toward the pirates in the dining hall and said, "Quick!

"Quickly circle around that area and do not approach it!"

Her voice carried a magnetic allure that jolted everyone awake. The sailors rushed out of the dining hall and headed wherever help was needed. Under the instructions of Navigator Ottolov and Boatswain Nina, they adjusted the sails and changed course, passing the ruins at a considerable distance.

Only when the peak formed by rocks and stone columns vanished beyond the horizon did Bloodless Heath Doyle lower his hands, the pain gone from his expression.

Upon seeing this, Klein narrowed his eyes. He felt that this Rose Bishop, the Future's second mate, might pose an enormous latent risk on the voyage.

This wasn't due to contempt for Beyonders of the Secrets Suppliant pathway, but was a judgment based on the Admiral of Stars's description and Heath Doyle's reaction.

Although everyone had heard the loud panting, Heath Doyle had been the only one in pain. He instinctively believed a corpse was buried in the ruins, and Cattleya's reaction after observing them proved him right.

That means that even if Heath Doyle doesn't actively listen to the True Creator's voice, merely possessing the Beyonder powers of a Listener is enough for him to hear more than ordinary people and most Low- and Mid-Sequence Beyonders under normal circumstances. Hence, when we were close enough to the source of the panting, he was affected the most and learned more about the danger.

Here, the problem can't be solved merely by avoiding similar ruins. According to Cattleya, these waters are filled with voices that can make a demigod lose control--voices that shouldn't be heard. If Heath Doyle's condition were poor or he became overly receptive, he might end up hearing those lethal whispers.

Similarly, even if a Sequence 6 Rose Bishop is inferior to a demigod skilled at listening, the gap can't be too great. In terms of the Die of Probability, just 2 points--not even 1--would be enough to make Heath Doyle hear voices he shouldn't and go mad or lose control... I have to warn Ma'am Hermit, even though she should've realized this long ago and made the necessary preparations... Klein retracted his gaze and heard his stomach growl softly.

He had yet to eat breakfast.

Beer covered the floor, and butter was splattered everywhere. All kinds of food--roasted fish, toast, and white bread--lay scattered across the floor or hung from various objects. All of it was somewhat dirty.

It should still be edible if I peel off the outer layer... Klein looked at a piece of bread leaning against a table leg, torn over what to do.

This was at odds with Gehrman Sparrow's persona!

As he decided to wait for lunch, Cattleya instructed the chef, "Prepare another breakfast for everyone.

"Leave this to Frank. P-perhaps he has uses for it."

For the rearing of monsters? Klein lampooned inwardly.

After a while, he finally received a breakfast that wasn't as sumptuous as the first: a smoked pork sausage, two thoroughly burnt pieces of toast, and a cup of light beer without sedatives, served in place of water.

Because they were traveling through extremely dangerous waters where disaster could strike at any moment, Klein displayed the eating skills he had developed in college. Just as he had in the college mess hall, he finished breakfast in only a minute or two.

He left the pirates' dining hall and went on deck, taking a post-meal stroll while observing his surroundings.

At that moment, the sea still appeared as though it was illuminated by a midday sun as it was colored gold.

Klein stopped and looked into the distance, where a point of light ahead was expanding.

Under the sunlight, refraction caused the point of light to shimmer with a multicolored luster like a gigantic transparent gemstone.

As the Future continued forging ahead, the point of light gradually revealed itself.

It first split apart before becoming clear: four gigantic columns made of pure diamond.

They resembled legendary pillars supporting the sea, extending downward as they stood firm and held up a sizable floating island.

The floating island's soil was charred black, without a trace of greenery. In its depths, lights shone with such unnatural brilliance that they outshone the midday sky.

Suddenly, a long screech rang out from the island.

It was loud and unrestrained, but it gave people a hair-raising sense of danger.

Before long, Klein heard galloping hooves and saw two steeds seemingly forged from gold rush out from the floating island. Behind them was a beautiful chariot likewise made of gold.

At that moment, Cattleya hurriedly amplified her voice, making it resound throughout every corner of the Future.

"Look down!

"Don't look at it!"

Klein was never one to put on a brave front. He subconsciously lowered his head upon hearing those words and looked at his leather boots.

He noticed the sunlight illuminating the deck grow brighter before dimming and rapidly returning to its former brilliance.

"It's all right now." Cattleya's voice sounded throughout the ship again, devoid of any obvious emotion.

Only then did Klein look up. The two steeds forged from gold and the beautiful chariot they pulled had vanished. The diamond pillars silently supported the floating island as resplendent light swirled around it.

What a huge diamond... What a strange floating island. What would happen if I hadn't lowered my head and watched the golden chariot charge forward? Klein looked around as he suddenly frowned.

A pirate who had stood seven or eight meters away had vanished. In his place were two pitch-black footprints.

Looking at the ashes floating in midair, Klein vaguely knew the outcome of not lowering his head.

Thankfully, Admiral of Stars has been here a few times in the past. She knows what to avoid and when to bow her head. If I hired Mr. Hanged Man, even if he was the one steering the ghost ship, we might've already been wiped out by now... No, if the Future hadn't arrived at its destination ahead of time without giving me any time to prepare, I would've long sought out Will Auceptin's advice. A magician never performs unprepared... Besides, if I hired Mr. Hanged Man, I definitely would've purchased the relevant information from Ma'am Hermit... Klein first sighed before recovering his calm.

He didn't suggest exploring the floating island. He let the Future sail past it and continue onward.

For the rest of the time, the sea resembled the outside world. There were only undulating waves, vastness, silence, and an endless horizon.

Klein occasionally saw fire embers floating on the surface of the sea, but he didn't find any signs of marine creatures, including mermaids.

Time ticked by, and lunch was soon underway.

Just as Klein was about to leave the deck for the dining hall, he suddenly realized that his surroundings had darkened!

The sky, which had remained fixed at midday, lost all sunlight and became shrouded in profound darkness.

The change was so abrupt that Klein's first reaction was to wonder who had switched off the lights!

Silently, the Future was covered in a layer of resplendent stars that illuminated paths in every direction.

Cattleya's magnetically alluring voice was magnified once again, resounding in everyone's ears.

"Return to your room or find any corner you can and go to sleep.

"Then, wait until you wake up naturally."

Puzzled, Frank Lee asked loudly, "What will happen if I don't go to sleep?"

At that moment, his voice boomed like a speaking bear.

Cattleya stood behind the window of the captain's cabin and said, "When we wake up, we will find you gone, never to be found again."

The night here is that terrifying? Klein was curious, but had no intention of trying to stay awake.

He returned to his room and, using the starlight that still shone aboard the Future, unfolded a paper crane and picked up a pencil to quickly write: "What should I watch out for when traveling through the dangerous waters at the easternmost reaches of the Sonia Sea?

"Where can I find mermaids there?"

Putting down the pencil, he folded the crane. Without taking off his coat, he lay in bed and, with the help of Cogitation, quickly fell asleep.

In a hazy world, he suddenly jolted awake, clearly knowing that he was dreaming.

No one is infiltrating it... Klein surveyed his surroundings and found himself on a mountaintop. Behind and to either side of him were black, cloister-like buildings. Ahead stood a withered tree and a protruding boulder.

Atop the boulder, Cattleya sat alone, hugging her knees and leaning forward as she stared at the mountain opposite them.

She was still dressed in the classical black robe that exuded an air of mystery. Her face bore an indescribable look of confusion.

At that moment, she didn't move at all; it was as though she was a stone sculpture.

Why is she in my dream? Klein took a few steps forward and leaped onto a boulder.

Before he could ask, the vast scene before his eyes stunned him, striking both body and soul.

Before the boulder was a bottomless cliff, and across the chasm stood a mountain covered in countless palaces, spires, and majestic city walls.

The opulent buildings were arranged in concentric tiers. Each one was abnormally huge and bore no resemblance to a human dwelling. Together, they possessed an indescribably epic grandeur, as though they belonged to legend or myth.

The sun hung in the distance, casting the colors of sunset over the city in light that seemed frozen.

"This is a dream shared by all of us..." Still sitting there with her knees hugged to her chest, Cattleya spoke as though in a reverie.

Chapter 649: Black Cloister

A dream shared by everyone? Klein repeated Cattleya's words inwardly as he slowly realized the situation he was in.

Nighttime in these dangerous waters would connect the dreams of all living creatures!

Any creature that didn't sleep would lack the necessary protection, as its Soul Body wouldn't be in the dream. As such, it would suffer an unknown attack.

As for why such an attack would lead to one's disappearance and not instant death, Klein, who hadn't actually experienced it, had no grounds for speculation.

As his thoughts raced, Klein withdrew his gaze from Cattleya and turned it back toward the magnificent city across the cliff. He wondered curiously,

If this world is truly formed by connecting the dreams of every creature here, whose mind could have conceived such an unimaginable city?

He observed for a few seconds before asking, "What is its name?"

What's the name of this city that can only exist in myths and legends?

Cattleya stared ahead blankly and spoke as though in a reverie. "I don't know... We have a chance of seeing it each time we enter the dream, but it's impossible to approach.

"She said that it resembles the Great Twilight Hall in Feysac.

"She likely has her own guesses, but she has never told me."

She? Queen Mystic? The Great Twilight Hall is the papal seat of the Church of the God of Combat... Klein surveyed the area and deliberated before saying, "I plan to look around."

He believed the Future wouldn't leave these waters anytime soon. He would definitely experience more nights and enter this dream world several more times. Therefore, to guard against accidents and gather intelligence, he needed to explore the area.

And such exploration undoubtedly required a partner.

Cattleya remained seated, hugging her knees. In the same ethereal tone, she said, "I'm not interested." This isn't how a mature pirate admiral should speak. You could've been more tactful. Ma'am Hermit, you resemble a petulant young lady... Klein was taken aback and wondered whether he had misheard. This conflicted with his impression of Admiral of Stars.

Remembering that Gehrman Sparrow also had a side that didn't fear dirt or hardship, he came to a realization and quickly formed a hypothesis.

Cattleya isn't completely awake in the dream. She knows that she's in a dream, but she's unable to effectively control it!

In other words, she would unconsciously reveal the feelings buried deep in her heart and aspects of her personality that she usually suppressed.

No wonder she said that she was never able to approach that miraculous city. It's because she never had the intention of exploring it herself... Klein thought for a moment and deliberately probed, "We might be able to discover something over there."

"I'm not going," Cattleya replied without hesitation, though she didn't shake her head. "I'll wait here! Wait!"

She really is in a semi-unconscious state... Klein made the judgment based on her reaction and tone.

He didn't waste any more time and turned to leap off the boulder.

Thud!

Klein landed on the ground and subconsciously looked back.

Cattleya remained seated there, hugging her knees. No one else was around. The frozen sunset over the city opposite her cast a long shadow behind her, which blended with one of the shadows cast by the withered trees.

A gentle mountain breeze blew, causing the dark silhouette to sway slightly.

Cattleya didn't move as she stubbornly waited on the spot.

At times like this, a Psychiatrist is needed to interpret the dream's emotions. This has nothing to do with revelations obtained through divination... Klein curled his lips and surveyed his surroundings, seeking a direction to explore.

He discovered that no matter which direction he took, he would end up at the black cloister buildings. A towering wall separated them from the cliff. No matter how one explored, one had to pass through the cloister unless one jumped off the cliff.

Since there was no other choice, Klein went directly to the pitch-black door of the cloister.

The door was nearly ten meters tall and didn't look as though it had been made for humans. Klein sized it up for a few seconds, took a deep breath, and extended his hands to push against both sides.

A creak followed. The door was far heavier than Klein had imagined. His muscles bulged and his face flushed red, yet he managed to move it only a fraction without pushing it open.

Thankfully, it's only a dream. As long as I have reason to believe it, I can increase my strength without truly activating Creeping Hunger... Klein exhaled and made his left glove turn pale.
