In the midst of dark green sparkles, he obtained a Zombie's strength. His arm turned thicker as his legs swelled.

Screech!

A deep grinding sound boomed as the door slowly opened to reveal its interior.

Two dark spires and the black buildings were connected by covered bridges, surrounding a vast square paved in gray stone.

The square was riddled with holes in which enormous arrows were embedded. Burning piles stood in various places, as though the square had once come under attack.

Klein passed through the gateway and entered the square. Unsurprisingly, he saw Frank Lee, Nina, Ottolov, and company there.

Is this their dream? It doesn't seem like it... Or is everyone's dream confined to themselves, after which they're randomly placed somewhere in this world? Klein guessed without confidence.

Frank Lee was the closest to him. He was holding a shovel and digging at some rubble. Beside him were the white bread, toast, and roasted fish which had previously fallen to the floor.

He plans to use them as fertilizer? He's planting things even in his dreams... Klein walked over and casually asked, "What are you doing?"

Frank didn't stop, but he revealed a smile.

"I'm cultivating some little things. They need to sleep in the soil for a while before they can grow large and reproduce."

"What's their use?" Klein asked, both worried and curious.

Frank beamed and said, "They're a hybrid fungus. It can make bulls produce milk. That way, we can obtain more milk and allow more people to drink good milk."

Spare those bulls... Klein's face twitched as he asked, "Will it succeed?"

"There are no problems with the effects, but I'm very worried that they can't reproduce," Frank said with a frown.

May Death forever favor them... Klein prayed as he walked past Frank Lee and headed for the black building's entrance across the square.

Along the way, he passed by Nina and Navigator Ottolov, who were drinking beside a collapsed pillar.

"Have you ever thought about leaving the pirate crew when you're older, finding a man to marry, and settling down? I don't think anyone wants to drift at sea their entire life." Ottolov removed his pointed bonnet, revealing slightly graying hair.

His gaze and tone told Klein that the implication was: If that's what you want, why not consider me?

Mr. Navigator, you're old enough to be Nina's father. You need to consider your health... As he passed, Klein couldn't help lampooning the conversation inwardly.

Nina gulped a mouthful of beer and looked in a particular direction.

"No, that's not the life I wish to lead.

"Before joining you, I once tried settling down on Feysac's eastern coast and giving up piracy, but I couldn't bear the boredom. I had to haul wood and move things every day, and I could only stay home at night. I wasn't allowed to go to bars or hunt in the wilderness. That kind of life was always the same! Furthermore, I had to endure all kinds of criticism and put up with those irritating people. Even when I wanted to beat them up, I had to worry about the police!

"Life is still better on the ship. Although it's boring most of the time, we often visit different places and encounter different things. Heh heh. Even during the dullest periods, I can torment those fellows and train them into passable pirates. I can also tell them that whoever performs best each month can spend the night in my room. Then, I'll watch them eagerly endure the torment. Of course, spending the night and having sex are two different things. It depends on my mood."

A true female pirate... Everyone wants something different... Klein made a neutral assessment and saw nothing wrong with Nina's ideas.

I won't object to her choice, but if she frequently murders, commits arson, and plunders, I won't mind exchanging her head for a bounty the next time we meet... Klein retracted his gaze and arrived at what appeared to be the entrance to the black buildings and spires.

Subconsciously, he turned his head and realized that the shadows in the corner seemed normal, but something about them seemed different.

Bloodless Heath Doyle? He hides in the shadows even in a dream? According to my limited knowledge of psychology, this is the result of a profound lack of security... Klein pushed against another door that was likewise nearly ten meters tall.

Amidst the grinding sounds, his gaze suddenly froze.

Behind the door was a vast hall which had two rows of stone pillars supporting it.

The hall was abnormally dark, without a single candle. As the main door opened, light streamed in from outside and illuminated the interior.

Klein saw murals in various colors covering the dome, predominantly gold. They connected seamlessly, giving him a sense of magnificence and sanctity.

Thump! Thump! Thump!

A figure stood with its back to Klein, using an axe to chop at a huge, long tree for some unknown purpose.

The figure was dressed in a white shirt and a black vest. It didn't look like any of the pirates on the ship.

There's someone else in these waters? Or could this be the owner of the mysterious eyes that had been observing the deck and me? Klein's heart sank as he slowed. He approached warily and moved to the figure's side, where he could see him clearly.

He was a young-looking man with short blond hair parted thirty-seventy. His emerald-green eyes appeared focused and serious.

"What are you doing? What is this place?" Klein asked cautiously.

He intuitively believed that the person wasn't the owner of the mysterious eyes.

The young man raised his hand to touch his earlobes without turning his head.

"Why are you asking these questions? My ship sank, and I'm busy making myself a canoe. I don't have time to talk to you."

Klein thought for a moment before asking, "Who are you?"

"Who am I? I'm the unlucky Anderson. Ever since I saw that mural, I've been plagued with bad luck." The young man pointed in a direction.

Tracing his finger, Klein saw a mural.

The mural depicted a sea of fire that split in the middle to produce a path.

A long line of people traveled along the path. Some bowed their heads in pious devotion, while others prostrated themselves on the ground. Their destination lay in the depths of the sea.

Their leader was a lanky man with long silver hair. His features were gentle, his eyes tightly closed, and layers of wings covered his back.

This... Klein's pupils constricted suddenly.

He recognized the leader on the mural!

It was the Angel of Fate which Little Sun had once depicted!

It was the Tail Devourer, Ouroboros!

Chapter 650: The Unlucky Anderson

After recognizing the leader on the mural, Klein subconsciously suspected that it was something from his dream.

However, he quickly rejected the idea. The unlucky Anderson wasn't someone he knew, nor had the man ever left an impression on him. It was unlikely that Anderson was a product of his own dream.

Furthermore, Anderson had clearly said that his bad luck began after he saw the mural. Since he was so strongly connected to it, the mural could only be part of Anderson's dream!

Focusing on it, Klein quickly realized that this mural differed somewhat from the one Little Sun had seen in the True Creator's abandoned temple.

The background here was a sea burning with golden flames, while the previous mural had depicted a desolate plain.

Here, the destination was in the depths of the sea, while the previous path led toward a distant mountain. On the mountaintop stood a huge cross with a figure hanging upside down.

Beneath the Angel of Fate Ouroboros's feet was black sludge, with heads hanging downward and fish trapped within. Previously, there had been a meandering river.

It's a different mural, but more like a memory of another stage in the same pilgrimage... Klein nodded as he formed a hypothesis.

A similar scene surfaced in his mind.

A very long time ago, at some point during the Fourth Epoch, Ouroboros led a devout group of pilgrims, perhaps remnants of the True Creator's believers. Pursued by many powerful enemies, they sailed through this sea.

For some reason, "He" abandoned the boat. With the help of the True Creator or "His" own powers, "He" parted the sea and led the devotees through it into the Forsaken Land of the Gods, leaving behind the seeds of organizations such as Rose Redemption and the Aurora Order.

In the Forsaken Land of the Gods, they crossed desolate plains and built temples along their pilgrimage. One of those temples was discovered by Little Sun and his companions.

Given how Will Auceptin was forced to restart a cycle and be reborn as a baby, the Tail Devourer is likely still alive... Does this mean that "He" eventually reached the destination with the pilgrims--the True Creator's holy residence? Does that mean the True Creator's holy residence is somewhere in the Forsaken Land of the Gods? At this thought, Klein suddenly felt an inexplicable sense of melancholy.

If my theory is correct, then no matter how the City of Silver tries to save itself or pass on the flame, once the True Creator fully awakens or returns to "His" original state, they will inevitably be destroyed!

When you're so close to an evil god's divine kingdom or holy residence, survival has nothing to do with how hard you struggle!

They're like bubbles formed when waves surge onto the shore. Once the waves recede, they vanish.

Human civilizations and peoples are simply that fragile beneath the gaze of an evil god.

No, I can't be that pessimistic. This is only a hypothesis. Perhaps it wasn't Ouroboros who forced Will Auceptin to reincarnate. Perhaps the True Creator won't recover or awaken so easily. "He" might be sealed by the seven deities...

Therefore, the City of Silver still has a chance. They need to open a path between the Forsaken Land of the Gods and the outside world before the evil god escapes "His" shackles, allowing everyone to evacuate! This is probably why the City of Silver's Chief released the Shepherd elder. They need to use every bit of power available to them... Klein forcibly reined in his thoughts.

He suddenly felt uneasy, afraid that merely arriving here had trapped him in an endless cycle of fate created by the Tail Devourer.

At this point, he instinctively wanted to take four steps counterclockwise and go above the gray fog. He wished to forcibly search for any memories he might have lost, but ultimately resisted the impulse and decided to observe first.

According to his interpretation of the symbols, there were no looping rivers, only black sludge with fish embedded in it. This didn't signify a cycle of fate, only lingering bad luck!

This was identical to what Anderson said!

As a King of Angels, Ouroboros definitely possesses more than the power to cycle fate. Different temples have different murals, so depicting different powers is entirely logical... Besides, this is a dream!

Besides, even if I did nothing and became trapped in a repeating cycle, forcing me to converse with the Admiral of Stars and make the same observations over and over, the problem would be exposed on Monday. The Tarot Club wouldn't be held, so Miss Justice and the others would become suspicious and pray. I could use their prayers to recover my memories... Klein instantly regained his confidence, though the chaos and tension in his heart were merely buried rather than gone.

He looked ahead and discovered that the hall extended endlessly into the distance. Light shone only near the entrance; everything else was dark, growing darker the deeper it went. All he could see were wooden doors on both sides, with no indication of where they led.

At the sight of this dark and eerie scene, Klein's desire to continue exploring dwindled.

To encounter a mural left behind by a King of Angels here, who knows what will happen if I delve deeper or enter some room...

Fear of the unknown was an ancient instinct. Premonitions of danger from unseen sources had given rise to a profound terror of the unknown. Klein examined his feelings for a few seconds and went no farther.

He turned toward Anderson, who was chopping the gigantic tree.

"Why are you here?"

Anderson looked up and scoffed.

"I'm a treasure hunter.

"So tell me, why would I be here?"

Treasure hunter... Klein casually asked, "Is there treasure here?"

Anderson continued working on the so-called canoe. His voice suddenly turned deep.

"There is treasure everywhere in these waters.

"As long as you can claim it and leave alive."

That's true... The problem is that this place is extremely dangerous for anyone who isn't a demigod, but even more dangerous for demigods... Klein looked into the depths of the hall and asked, "Do you know where this place is?"

Anderson followed his gaze and said, "I don't know.

"At least a third of my companions formed a team and went inside to explore, but they never returned."

"Are you referring to the real world or the dream world?" Klein asked, his thoughts perfectly clear.

Bang!

After the axe landed, Anderson laughed.

"Of course it's in the real world.

"Another third of my companions went to explore the dream. They didn't return either." Klein inhaled sharply and fell into thought.

"Where are their bodies in the real world?"

"They mutated into monsters and killed quite a few of my companions." Anderson raised his axe and swung it down.

Clang!

With a crisp crack, his axe split in two. Because the fracture ran toward the back of the axe, a fragment immediately shot toward him.

Blood immediately gushed from the right side of Anderson's chest and abdomen.

He held his left hand to his wound and looked up at Klein. Smiling bitterly, he said, "I told you I was plagued with bad luck ever since I saw that mural.

"Thankfully, this isn't considered too unlucky. At the very least, they didn't destroy my exceptionally handsome face."

...Is that really the right adjective to use? Klein watched Anderson swiftly pull the fragment from his body, treat the wound, and take some medicine. Anderson remained unfazed, and the practiced ease of his movements showed that he had long since grown accustomed to such mishaps.

With one hand in his pocket, Klein toyed with the coins inside. After some deliberation, he asked, "When your companions began exploring, were you part of the group that stayed behind to study the mural?"

Anderson was taken aback as he tucked his medicine pouch into his belt and wiped his mouth.

"No.

"I was part of the group that went exploring..."

As he spoke, he grinned and revealed a genial smile.

This... Klein's pupils constricted as he bent forward slightly and raised his left palm.

At that moment, blinding sunlight turned everything dazzling white. Then, it dimmed and vanished.

Klein opened his eyes naturally and discovered that the world outside had returned to its midday state.

He took out the pocket watch in his inner pocket and opened it.

Only half an hour has passed. The night here is rather short...

That unlucky Anderson looked perfectly normal. Who would've known he was so terrifying!

Rolling to his feet, Klein suddenly recalled something. Snake of Fate Will Auceptin had yet to "reply" to him!

Was "He" unable to locate me because everyone's dreams had been drawn into a world disconnected from the spirit world? Or did "He" sense Ouroboros's aura and not dare approach? Or is there something inherently wrong with these waters? As his thoughts whirled, Klein decided to investigate.

The method of verifying it was simple. He could go back to sleep while it was still "noon."

However, he wasn't in a hurry because he didn't know whether there were any taboos against sleeping during the day.

Wearing his cap, Klein went to the captain's cabin and knocked on the door.

After three knocks, he retracted his hand and patiently waited.

Before long, Cattleya opened the door.

She no longer looked as lost as she had in the dream. She was wearing her thick glasses again.

"Is it all right to sleep during the day?" Klein asked directly.

Cattleya nodded.

"Yes."

After answering, she hesitated and asked, "You seemed very active in that dream?"

Realizing how much danger lurked in these waters, and that he would inevitably be forced to reveal some of his abilities, Klein decided to lay the groundwork for future explanations.

He looked at Cattleya and smiled politely.

"Yes.

"This is a gift bestowed upon me by my Lord."

My Lord... Cattleya's eyes visibly shimmered behind her thick glasses.

Unusually, she frowned slightly, then relaxed without asking any further questions.

Klein thought for a moment, then he added, "Be careful of Heath Doyle."

Cattleya clearly understood what he meant and answered directly, "Don't worry. He has a Sealed Artifact whose negative effect limits him to hearing only nearby voices."

That's a clever use of a negative effect... Klein didn't belabor the point. He removed his hat and bowed before returning to his room.

He lay down once again and used Cogitation to fall asleep.

In the dream world, he woke up and saw the familiar pitch-black plains and black steeple.

Phew, I can still establish a connection... Klein heaved a sigh of relief and entered the steeple. In the usual place, he saw the scattered tarot cards and the new words.

"There are many dangers there, the greatest being the dream that begins when night falls.

"The danger isn't simply that you'll disappear if you don't sleep, but there is one thing you must remember.

"Do not explore that dream!

"Absolutely do not explore that dream!

"Since there's not enough space, I won't explain why. Alright, it's just a joke. The reason is that the area contains some of the dreams left behind by a deity."
